Persistence of Rademacher-type and Sobolev-to-Lipschitz properties Thanks: This research was funded in whole, or in part, by the Austrian Science Fund (FWF) ESPRIT 208. For the purpose of open access, the author has applied a CC BY public copyright licence to any Author Accepted Manuscript version arising from this submission. Thanks: K.S. gratefully acknowledges funding by the Alexander von Humboldt Stiftung, Humboldt-Forschungsstipendium
Lorenzo Dello Schiavo, Kohei Suzuki

Abstract
. We consider the Rademacher- and Sobolev-to-Lipschitz-type properties for arbitrary quasi-regular strongly local Dirichlet spaces. We discuss the persistence of these properties under localization, globalization, transfer to weighted spaces, tensorization, and direct integration. As byproducts we obtain: necessary and sufficient conditions to identify a quasi-regular strongly local Dirichlet form on an extended metric topological σ \sigma -finite possibly non-Radon measure space with the Cheeger energy of the space; the tensorization of intrinsic distances; the tensorization of the Varadhan short-time asymptotics.



2020 Mathematics Subject Classification
31C25,30L99, 31E05

1.
Introduction

The interplay between analysis, geometry, and stochastic analysis on non-smooth spaces has recently gained incredibly large attention.
Extraordinarily insightful theories have been developed connecting these three aspects on:

•

Ricci-limit spaces;

•

sub-Riemannian manifolds;

•

Alexandrov and Cartan–Alexandrov–Topogonov spaces;

•

metric measure spaces satisfying synthetic Ricci-curvature lower bounds à la Lott–Sturm–Villani and Ambrosio–Gigli–Savaré;

•

Lorentzian metric measure spaces;

•

configuration, Wasserstein, Wiener, and other infinite-dimensional spaces;

only to name a few.

On the one hand, every metric measure space
(X,\mathsf{d},\mathsf{m})
may be endowed with a natural convex energy functional nicely capturing the properties of the metric measure structure, namely the
Cheeger energy

\mathsf{Ch}_{\mathsf{d},\mathsf{m}}
on the space of real-valued functions on
X
.
Starting from
\mathsf{Ch}_{\mathsf{d},\mathsf{m}}
, it is possible to build a sophisticated theory of non-smooth analysis on
(X,\mathsf{d},\mathsf{m})
encompassing for instance: first- and second-order Sobolev spaces, a (non-linear) ‘Laplacian’, a (non-linear) ‘heat flow’, etc; see e.g.
[
6
,
27
]
.
It was a particularly fruitful intuition —in great generality put forward by N. Gigli— that the validity of the parallelogram identity for
\mathsf{Ch}_{\mathsf{d},\mathsf{m}}
—equivalently, the linearity of the heat flow— provides a setting most suitable to the study of the interplay mentioned above.

On the other hand however, other standard —this time naturally
quadratic
— energy forms appear in many settings and are in principle unrelated to the metric-measure structure of the underlying space.
This includes, in particular, Dirichlet energy forms on configuration spaces
[
3
,
35
,
21
,
17
,
19
]
, spaces of measures
[
16
,
23
,
30
]
, Wiener spaces
[
1
,
2
,
29
]
, and others.

Linking —and possibly reconciling— these two aspects of the theory of non-smooth spaces provides great insight, allowing us to import tools from stochastic analysis into metric measure geometry, and vice versa.
To this end, in
[
18
]
we introduced several Rademacher- and Sobolev-to-Lipschitz-type properties comparing the domain of the energy form under consideration with the space of Lipschitz functions induced by an assigned distance.
We used this comparison to give sufficient conditions for the validity of the integral-type Varadhan short-time asymptotics of the heat semigroup with respect to an assigned distance.

Here, we address the persistence of these Rademacher- and Sobolev-to-Lipschitz-type properties under various constructions/transformations on arbitrary quasi-regular strongly local Dirichlet spaces, including:

•

localization
to form restrictions (in the sense of e.g.
[
31
, §3]
);

•

globalization
from form restrictions on coverings of the space;

•

transfer to
weighted spaces
(more general than Girsanov transforms);

•

tensorization
to product spaces;

•

direct integration
(in the sense of
[
15
,
20
]
).

As anticipated, our first goal is to compare notions in Dirichlet-form theory (quasi-regular strongly local forms, energy measures, square field operators) with notions in metric measure geometry (Cheeger energies, minimal weak upper gradients, minimal relaxed slopes).
Whenever possible, we do so in great generality, on extended metric topological
\sigma
-finite non-Radon measure spaces; in particular, away from any assumption of geometric type (e.g., measure doubling, Poincaré inequalities, synthetic curvature bounds, etc.).

Applications to particle systems

Apart from the theoretical motivations explained above, the persistence of the aforementioned properties is inspired also by geometric and analytic constructions of infinite particle systems.
Each of the above transformations of Dirichlet forms plays a significant role for the construction of interacting particle systems of diffusions from a single diffusion process on the base space.
Starting from a one-particle diffusion associated with the Dirichlet energy on the base space, the correspondence between each operation on Dirichlet spaces and diffusion processes is the following:

•

localization

\rightsquigarrow

killing
of a one-particle diffusion upon exiting given set;

•

globalization

\rightsquigarrow

patching
a one-particle diffusions;

•

tensorization

\rightsquigarrow

independent copy
of a one-particle diffusion;

•

transfer to
weighted spaces

\rightsquigarrow

interacting
many-particle system;

•

direct integration

\rightsquigarrow

superposition
of finite-particle systems to the space of infinite particles.

The study of the persistence under these transformations leads us to lift metric measure properties of the base space to the space of infinite particles such as the configuration space or the space of atomic probability measures, see e.g.,
[
17
,
19
]
for the application to the configuration space and
[
16
]
for the Wasserstein space.

1.1.
The Rademacher and Sobolev-to-Lipschitz properties

Let
(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
be a quasi-regular strongly local Dirichlet form on the space
L^{2}(\mathsf{m})
of a topological Luzin space
(X,\tau)
endowed with a Radon measure
\mathsf{m}
.
Given a
\sigma
-finite Borel measure
\mu
on
X
(possibly different from
\mathsf{m}
), we denote by
\mathbb{L}^{\mu}_{\mathrm{loc},b}
the space of bounded functions in the
broad local domain
(see §
2.4
) of
(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
with
\mu
-uniformly bounded
\mathcal{E}
-energy
(see §
2.4.2
).
In particular —in order to exemplify this concept— when
(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
admits carré du champ operator
\Gamma
, and
\mu=g\mathsf{m}
is absolutely continuous w.r.t.
\mathsf{m}
, then
\mathbb{L}^{\mu}_{\mathrm{loc},b}
is the space of all functions
f
in the broad local domain of
(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
additionally satisfying
\Gamma(f)\leq g

\mathsf{m}
-a.e..

For
\mu
as above, we introduced in
[
18
]
the
intrinsic distance

\mathsf{d}_{\mu}
of
(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
induced by
\mu
, see (
2.13
).
It is an extended pseudo-distance, that is, possibly taking the value
+\infty
and/or vanishing outside the diagonal in
X^{\scriptscriptstyle{\times 2}}
.
When
\mu=\mathsf{m}
and
(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
is a regular Dirichlet form,
\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}}
coincides with the standard intrinsic distance of
(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
.

Now, let
\mathsf{d}\colon X^{\scriptscriptstyle{\times 2}}\to[0,\infty]
be any extended pseudo-distance.
For the sake of notational simplicity, throughout this introduction we denote by
\mathrm{Lip}^{1}(\mathsf{d})
the space of
measurable

\mathsf{d}
-Lipschitz functions on
X
with Lipschitz constant less than
1
, postponing to later sections a discussion of measurability issues and the choice(s) of a
\sigma
-algebra.
Finally, for
f\in L^{0}(\mathsf{m})
, denote by
\hat{f}
and
\tilde{f}
any of its (everywhere defined)
\mathsf{m}
-representatives.

After
[
18
]
(also cf. Dfn.
2.14
below), we say that
(X,\mathcal{E},\mathsf{d},\mu)
has:

(
\mathsf{Rad}_{\mathsf{d},\mu}
)

the
Rademacher property
if, whenever
\hat{f}\in\mathrm{Lip}^{1}(\mathsf{d})
, then
f\in\mathbb{L}^{\mu}_{\mathrm{loc}}
;

(
{\mathsf{d}}\textrm{-}\mathsf{Rad}_{\mu}
)

the
distance-Rademacher property
if
\mathsf{d}\leq\mathsf{d}_{\mu}
;

(
\mathsf{ScL}_{\mathsf{m},\tau,\mathsf{d}}
)

the
Sobolev–to–continuous-Lipschitz property
if each
f\in\mathbb{L}^{\mu}_{\mathrm{loc}}
has a
\tau
-continuous
\mathsf{m}
-representative
\hat{f}\in\mathrm{Lip}^{1}(\mathsf{d})
;

(
\mathsf{SL}_{\mu,\mathsf{d}}
)

the
Sobolev–to–Lipschitz property
if each
f\in\mathbb{L}^{\mu}_{\mathrm{loc}}
has an
\mathsf{m}
-representative
\hat{f}\in\mathrm{Lip}^{1}(\mathsf{d})
;

(
{\mathsf{d}}\textrm{-}\mathsf{cSL}_{\mu,\mathsf{d}}
)

the
\mathsf{d}
-continuous-Sobolev–to–Lipschitz property
if each
f\in\mathbb{L}^{\mu}_{\mathrm{loc}}
having a
\mathsf{d}
-continuous (measurable) representative
\hat{f}
also has a representative
\tilde{f}\in\mathrm{Lip}^{1}(\mathsf{d})
(possibly,
\tilde{f}\neq\hat{f}
);

(
\mathsf{cSL}_{\tau,\mathsf{m},\mathsf{d}}
)

the
continuous-Sobolev–to–Lipschitz property
if each
\tau
-continuous
f\in\mathbb{L}^{\mu}_{\mathrm{loc}}
satisfies
f\in\mathrm{Lip}^{1}(\mathsf{d})
;

(
{\mathsf{d}}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SL}_{\mu}
)

the
distance Sobolev-to-Lipschitz property
if
\mathsf{d}\geq\mathsf{d}_{\mu}
.

For implications between the above properties in the present setting see (
2.14
).

1.2.
Main results

Let us now summarize our main results.

Localization/globalization

We prove that the Rademacher property is stable under localization (Prop.
3.6
), while the Sobolev–to–Lipschitz property is stable under globalization (in the sense of sheaves, see Prop.
3.12
) but not under localization (Ex.
3.10
).
Let us stress that these stability results are trivial (or trivially false) if one replaces the local space
\mathbb{L}^{\mathsf{m}}_{\mathrm{loc},b}
with its global counterpart
\mathbb{L}^{\mathsf{m}}_{b}
(see §
2.4.2
), which is a first indication—see below for stronger ones—that the Rademacher and Sobolev-to-Lipschitz properties ought to be phrased in terms of
local
spaces (as opposed to: subspaces of the domain).

Weighted spaces

Both the Rademacher and the Sobolev-to-Lipschitz property for
(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
on
L^{2}(\mathsf{m})
transfer to weighted space
L^{2}(\theta\mathsf{m})
for any density
\theta
bounded away from
0
and infinity locally in the sense of quasi-open nests.
For simplicity, we state here a single result under stronger assumptions than necessary, combining the transfer of the Rademacher property with the transfer of the Sobolev-to-Lipschitz property (for minimal assumptions see Prop.s
4.8
and
4.12
respectively).

Theorem

(Cor.
4.4
)
.

Let
(\mathbb{X},\mathcal{E})
and
(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathcal{E}^{\prime})
be quasi-regular strongly local Dirichlet spaces with same underlying topological measurable space
(X,\tau,\Sigma)=(X^{\prime},\tau^{\prime},\Sigma^{\prime})
and possibly different measures
\mathsf{m}
and
\mathsf{m}^{\prime}
.
Further let
\mathsf{d}\colon X^{\scriptscriptstyle{\times 2}}\to[0,\infty]
be an extended pseudo-distance.
Assume that:

(1)

there exists a linear subspace
\mathcal{D}
both
\mathcal{F}
-dense in
\mathcal{F}
and
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
-dense in
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
, additionally so that
\Gamma=\Gamma^{\prime}
on
\mathcal{D}
.

(2)

\mathsf{m}^{\prime}=\theta\mathsf{m}
for some
\theta\in L^{0}(\mathsf{m})
, and there exists
E_{\bullet},G_{\bullet}
quasi-open nests for both
\mathcal{E}
and
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
with the following properties:

(a)

for each
k\in{\mathbb{N}}
there exists a constant
a_{k}>0
such that

0<a_{k}\leq\theta\leq a_{k}^{-1}<\infty\quad\mathsf{m}\text{-a.e.}\quad\text{on }G_{k}\,\,\mathrm{;}\;\,

(that is,
\theta,\theta^{-1}\in{{\big({L^{\infty}(\mathsf{m})}\big)}^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{loc}}}(G_{\bullet})
.)

(b)

for each
k\in{\mathbb{N}}
it holds
E_{k}\subset G_{k}

\mathcal{E}
- and
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
-quasi-everywhere, and there exists
\varrho_{k}\in\mathcal{D}
with
\car_{E_{k}}\leq\varrho_{k}\leq\car_{G_{k}}

\mathsf{m}
-a.e..

Then,

(1)

\big({\Gamma,\mathbb{L}^{\mathsf{m}}_{\mathrm{loc},b}}\big)=\big({\Gamma^{\prime},\mathbb{L}^{\prime\,\mathsf{m}^{\prime}}_{\mathrm{loc},b}}\big)
and
\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}}=\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}^{\prime}}
;

(2)

(\mathsf{ScL}_{\mathsf{m},\tau,\mathsf{d}})
, resp.
(\mathsf{SL}_{\mathsf{m},\mathsf{d}})
,
(\mathsf{cSL}_{\tau,\mathsf{m},\mathsf{d}})
,
(\mathsf{Rad}_{\mathsf{d},\mathsf{m}})
, holds if and only if
(\mathsf{ScL}_{\mathsf{m}^{\prime},\tau,\mathsf{d}})
, resp.
(\mathsf{SL}_{\mathsf{m}^{\prime},\mathsf{d}})
,
(\mathsf{cSL}_{\tau,\mathsf{m}^{\prime},\mathsf{d}})
,
(\mathsf{Rad}_{\mathsf{d},\mathsf{m}^{\prime}})
holds.

The above theorem provides us with the following general guidelines:

•

the broad local space of functions with uniformly bounded energy, the intrinsic distance, and various Rademacher and Sobolev-to-Lipschitz-type properties are all
completely determined by a dense subspace and in a local fashion
.

•

the notion of broad local space introduced by K. Kuwae in
[
31
]
is the right one to address the interplay between the Dirichlet-space and metric measure space structures.

•

the intrinsic distance, and therefore —under the assumption of both the Rademacher and the Sobolev-to-Lipschitz property— the Varadhan-type short-time asymptotics for the heat-semigroup/kernel, both transfer to weighted spaces in far greater generality than Girsanov transforms.

When considering applications to metric measure geometry it is natural to choose the space
\mathcal{D}
in the above Theorem to be some algebra of Lipschitz functions.
In this case, as a consequence of the above result, the Rademacher, Sobolev-to-Lipschitz-type and related properties may be shown in the simplified setting of probability spaces and subsequently transferred to general
\sigma
-finite spaces.
This applies in particular to the comparison of a Dirichlet space
(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
with the Cheeger energy
\mathsf{Ch}_{\mathsf{d},\mathsf{m}}
of the underlying extended metric measure space, for which we prove two comparison results (Prop.
4.8
and
4.12
) separately showing the inequalities
\mathcal{E}\leq\mathsf{Ch}_{\mathsf{d},\mathsf{m}}
and
\mathcal{E}\geq\mathsf{Ch}_{\mathsf{d},\mathsf{m}}
under minimal assumptions.
Combining the two, we further have—in the general case of
\sigma
-finite extended metric spaces—the following identification of
\mathcal{E}
with
\mathsf{Ch}_{\mathsf{d},\mathsf{m}}
, previously shown by L. Ambrosio, N. Gigli, and G. Savaré
[
8
]
for energy-measure spaces, and by L. Ambrosio, M. Erbar, and G. Savaré
[
4
]
for extended metric-topological
probability
spaces.
Namely we prove:

Theorem

(Cor.
4.14
)
.

Let
(\mathbb{X},\mathcal{E})
be a quasi-regular strongly local Dirichlet space admitting carré du champ operator
\Gamma
, and
\mathsf{d}\colon X^{\scriptscriptstyle{\times 2}}\to[0,\infty]
be an extended distance.
Further assume that
(\mathsf{Rad}_{\mathsf{d},\mathsf{m}})
and
(\mathsf{cSL}_{\tau,\mathsf{m},\mathsf{d}})
hold.
Then,
\mathcal{E}\leq\mathsf{Ch}_{\mathsf{d},\mathsf{m}}
.
The equality
\mathcal{E}=\mathsf{Ch}_{\mathsf{d},\mathsf{m}}
holds if and only if
(\mathbb{X},\mathcal{E})
is additionally
\tau
-upper regular (see Dfn.
4.10
).

See Remark
4.15
below for a thorough comparison of our results with those in
[
8
,
4
]
.
Let us further stress that the above Theorem allows us to
deduce
the parallelogram identity for the Cheeger energy, from that for the form
\mathcal{E}
, thus providing necessary and sufficient conditions to implement the
Riemannian
point of view in the sense of Gigli’s.

Tensorization

In the case when
\mathsf{d}
is a distance (
not
extended), we also discuss the tensorization of the Rademacher and Sobolev-to-Lipschitz properties.

When
\mathcal{E}=\mathsf{Ch}_{\mathsf{d},\mathsf{m}}
is the Cheeger energy of a metric measure space, the tensorization of the Rademacher property is a byproduct of the tensorization of the Cheeger energy, discussed under different geometric assumptions in
[
7
,
9
]
and recently settled by S. Eriksson-Bique, T. Rajala, and E. Soultanis for infinitesimally (quasi-)Hilbertian metric measure spaces in
[
22
]
; see §
5.2
below for a detailed account.
Here, we discuss the case of general quasi-regular strongly local Dirichlet spaces
(\mathbb{X},\mathcal{E})
, without any geometric assumption.

As it turns out, a product space inherits the Rademacher property from its factors.

Theorem

(Thm.
5.9
)
.

Let
\mathbb{X}\eqdef(X,\mathsf{d},\mathsf{m})
be a metric measure space (Dfn.
5.1
), and
(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
be a quasi-regular strongly local Dirichlet form on
\mathbb{X}
admitting carré du champ operator
\Gamma
and satisfying
(\mathsf{Rad}_{\mathsf{d},\mathsf{m}})
.
Further let
(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathcal{E}^{\prime})
be satisfying the same assumptions as
(\mathbb{X},\mathcal{E})
.

Then, their product space
(\mathbb{X}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}},\mathcal{E}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}})
satisfies
(\mathsf{Rad}_{\mathsf{d}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}},\mathsf{m}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}})
.

As for the Sobolev-to-Lipschitz property, we show that a product space satisfies the continuous-Sobolev-to-Lipschitz property if the factors satisfy the Sobolev-to-Lipschitz property.

Theorem

(Thm.
5.10
)
.

Let
\mathbb{X}\eqdef(X,\mathsf{d},\mathsf{m})
be a metric measure space (Dfn.
5.1
), and
(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
be a quasi-regular strongly local Dirichlet form on
\mathbb{X}
satisfying
(\mathsf{SL}_{\mathsf{m},\mathsf{d}})
.
Further let
(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathcal{E}^{\prime})
be satisfying the same assumptions as
(\mathbb{X},\mathcal{E})
.
Then, their product space
(\mathbb{X}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}},\mathcal{E}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}})
satisfies
(\mathsf{cSL}_{\tau^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}},\mathsf{m}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}},\mathsf{d}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}})
.

Notation

For a measure
\mu
on a measurable space
(X,\Sigma)
we denote by
\mu A
, resp.
\mu f
, the
\mu
-measure of
A\in\Sigma
, resp. the integral with respect to
\mu
of a
\Sigma
-measurable function
f
(whenever the integral makes sense).

2.
Setting

2.1.
Metric and topological spaces

Let
X
be any non-empty set. A function
\mathsf{d}\colon X^{\scriptscriptstyle{\times 2}}\rightarrow[0,\infty]
is an
extended pseudo-distance
if it is symmetric and satisfying the triangle inequality. Any such
\mathsf{d}
is: a
pseudo-distance
if it is everywhere finite, i.e.
\mathsf{d}\colon X^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\times 2}}}\rightarrow[0,\infty)
; an
extended distance
if it does not vanish outside the diagonal in
X^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\times 2}}}
, i.e.
\mathsf{d}(x,y)=0
iff
x=y
; a
distance
if it is both finite and non-vanishing outside the diagonal.

Let
x_{0}\in X
and
r\in(0,\infty]
. We write
B^{\mathsf{d}}_{r}(x_{0})\eqdef\left\{\mathsf{d}_{x_{0}}<r\right\}
.
We call
B^{\mathsf{d}}_{\infty}(x_{0})
the
\mathsf{d}
-accessible component
of
x_{0}
in
X
.
Note that, if
\mathsf{d}
is an extended pseudo-metric, then both of the inclusions
\left\{x_{0}\right\}\subset\cap_{r>0}B^{\mathsf{d}}_{r}(x_{0})
and
B^{\mathsf{d}}_{\infty}(x_{0})\subset X
may be strict ones.
We say that an extended metric space is
complete
if
B^{\mathsf{d}}_{\infty}(x)
is complete for each
x\in X
.
Finally set

\displaystyle\mathsf{d}({\,\cdot\,},A)\eqdef

\displaystyle\inf_{x\in A}\mathsf{d}({\,\cdot\,},x)\colon X\longrightarrow[0,\infty]\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,\qquad A\subset X\,\,\mathrm{.}

Lemma 2.1
.

Let
\mathsf{d}\colon X^{\scriptscriptstyle{\times 2}}\to[0,\infty]
be an extended pseudo-distance.
Furter fix
x\in X
and let
\mathscr{A}_{x}
be any family of subsets with
x\in\cap\mathscr{A}_{x}
and
\inf_{A\in\mathscr{A}_{x}}\mathrm{diam}_{\mathsf{d}}A=0
.
Then,

\sup_{A\in\mathscr{A}_{x}}\mathsf{d}({\,\cdot\,},A)=\mathsf{d}({\,\cdot\,},x)\,\,\mathrm{.}

Proof.

The inequality ‘
\leq
’ is always satisfied by definition of point-to-set extended pseudo-distance, thus it suffices to show the converse inequality.
Up to restricting to any up-to-countable sub-family of
\mathscr{A}_{x}
, it suffices to show the assertion in the case when
\mathscr{A}_{x}\eqdef\left(A_{n}\right)_{n}
is up-to-countable.

Now, fix
y\in X
and
\varepsilon>0
.
For each
n\in{\mathbb{N}}_{1}
let
x_{n}
be so that
\mathsf{d}(y,A_{n})\geq\mathsf{d}(y,x_{n})+\varepsilon
.
Then,

\displaystyle\sup_{n}\mathsf{d}(y,A_{n})\geq

\displaystyle\ \varepsilon+\sup_{n}\mathsf{d}(y,x_{n})\geq\varepsilon+\sup_{n}\big({\mathsf{d}(y,x)-\mathsf{d}(x,x_{n})}\big)\geq\varepsilon+\mathsf{d}(y,x)-\inf_{n}\mathrm{diam}_{\mathsf{d}}(A_{n})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \varepsilon+\mathsf{d}(y,x)\,\,\mathrm{.}

By arbitrariness of
\varepsilon>0
we conclude that
\sup_{n}\mathsf{d}(y,A_{n})=\mathsf{d}(y,x)
, and the conclusion follows by arbitrariness of
y
.
∎

For an extended pseudo-distance
\mathsf{d}
on
X
, let
\tau_{\mathsf{d}}
denote the (possibly
not
Hausdorff) topology on
X
induced by the pseudo-distance
\mathsf{d}\wedge 1
.
The topology
\tau_{\mathsf{d}}
is Hausdorff if and only if
\mathsf{d}
is an extended distance.
The topology
\tau_{\mathsf{d}}
is separable if and only if there exists a countable family of points
\left(x_{n}\right)_{n}\subset X
so that
X=\cup_{n}B^{\mathsf{d}}_{\infty}(x_{n})
and
(B^{\mathsf{d}}_{\infty}(x_{n}),\mathsf{d})
is a separable pseudo-metric space for every
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
.

Lipschitz functions

A function
\hat{f}\colon X\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}
is
\mathsf{d}
-Lipschitz if there exists a constant
L>0
so that

(2.1)

\displaystyle\char 12\relax{\hat{f}(x)-\hat{f}(y)}\leq L\,\mathsf{d}(x,y)\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,\qquad x,y\in X\,\,\mathrm{.}

The smallest constant
L
so that (
2.1
) holds is the (global)
Lipschitz constant of
\hat{f}
, denoted by
\mathrm{L}_{\mathsf{d}}(\hat{f})
.
Further let the
slope
of
\hat{f}
at
x
be defined as

\char 12\relax{\mathrm{D}\hat{f}}_{\mathsf{d}}(x)\eqdef\limsup_{\mathsf{d}(x,y)\to 0}\frac{\left\lvert f(x)-f(y)\right\rvert}{\mathsf{d}(x,y)}\,\,\mathrm{.}

Conventionally,
\char 12\relax{\mathrm{D}\hat{f}}_{\mathsf{d}}(x)=0
whenever
x
is isolated relative to
\mathsf{d}
.
We omit the specification of
\mathsf{d}
whenever apparent from context.

Remark 2.2
.

It is worth stressing that —
conventionally
— in (
2.1
) we set
0\cdot\infty\eqdef\infty
.
We further note that, for
\hat{f}\colon X\to{\mathbb{R}}
, having
\mathrm{L}_{\mathsf{d}}(\hat{f})=0
does not imply that
\hat{f}
is constant, unless
\mathsf{d}
were in fact a distance (
not
: extended).
Indeed, if
\mathsf{d}
has accessible components
\left(X_{i}\right)_{i\in I}
, then every function
\hat{f}
constant on each
X_{i}
is
\mathsf{d}
-Lipschitz with
\mathrm{L}_{\mathsf{d}}(\hat{f})=0
.

For any non-empty
A\subset X
we write
\mathrm{Lip}(A,\mathsf{d})
, resp.
\mathrm{Lip}_{b}(A,\mathsf{d})
for the family of all finite, resp. bounded,
\mathsf{d}
-Lipschitz functions on
A
. For simplicity of notation, further let
\mathrm{Lip}(\mathsf{d})\eqdef\mathrm{Lip}(X,\mathsf{d})
, resp.
\mathrm{Lip}_{b}(\mathsf{d})\eqdef\mathrm{Lip}_{b}(X,\mathsf{d})
.

Topological spaces

A Hausdorff topological space
(X,\tau)
is:

(1)

a
topological Luzin space
if it is a continuous injective image of a Polish space;

(2)

a
metrizable Luzin space
if it is homeomorphic to a Borel subset of a compact metric space.

Let
(X,\tau)
be a Hausdorff topological space. A family of pseudo-distances
\mathsf{UP}
is a
uniformity
(
of pseudo-distances
) if:

(1)

it is directed, i.e.,
\mathsf{d}_{1}\vee\mathsf{d}_{2}\in\mathsf{UP}
for every
\mathsf{d}_{1},\mathsf{d}_{2}\in\mathsf{UP}
;
and

(2)

it is order-closed, i.e.,
\mathsf{d}_{2}\in\mathsf{UP}
and
\mathsf{d}_{1}\leq\mathsf{d}_{2}
implies
\mathsf{d}_{1}\in\mathsf{UP}
for every pseudo-distance
\mathsf{d}_{1}
on
X
.

A uniformity is
Hausdorff
if it separates points.

Extended metric-topological space

The next definition is a reformulation of
[
4
, Dfn. 4.1]
.

Definition 2.3

(Extended metric-topological space)
.

Let
(X,\tau)
be a Hausdorff topological space. An extended pseudo-distance
\mathsf{d}\colon X^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\times 2}}}\rightarrow[0,\infty]
is
\tau
-
admissible
if there exists a uniformity
\mathsf{UP}
of
\tau^{\scriptscriptstyle{\times 2}}
-continuous
pseudo-distances
\mathsf{d}^{\prime}\colon X^{\scriptscriptstyle{\times 2}}\rightarrow[0,\infty)
, so that

(2.2)

\displaystyle\mathsf{d}=\sup\left\{\mathsf{d}^{\prime}:\mathsf{d}^{\prime}\in\mathsf{UP}\right\}\,\,\mathrm{.}

The triple
(X,\tau,\mathsf{d})
is an
extended metric-topological space
if
\mathsf{d}
is
\tau
-admissible, and there exists a uniformity
\mathsf{UP}
witnessing the
\tau
-admissibility of
\mathsf{d}
, and additionally Hausdorff and generating
\tau
.

Let
\mathsf{d}\colon X^{\scriptscriptstyle{\times 2}}\to[0,\infty]
be an extended pseudo-distance on
X
.
We denote by
\tau_{\mathsf{d}}
the topology induced by
\mathsf{d}
and we note that, even in the case when
\mathsf{d}
is
\tau
-admissible,
\tau_{\mathsf{d}}
is in general strictly finer than
\tau
.
If
\mathsf{d}
is a distance however, then
\tau_{\mathsf{d}}=\tau
.

2.2.
Measure spaces

Let
(X,\tau)
be a Hausdorff topological space. We denote by
\mathscr{B}_{\tau}
the Borel
\sigma
-algebra of
(X,\tau)
. Given a Borel measure
\mu
on
(X,\mathscr{B}_{\tau})
, we denote by
\mathscr{B}_{\tau}^{\mu}
the (Carathédory) completion of
\mathscr{B}_{\tau}
with respect to
\mu
.
Given
\sigma
-finite measures
\mu_{0}
,
\mu_{1}
on
(X,\mathscr{B}_{\tau})
, we write
\mu_{0}\leq\mu_{1}
to indicate that
\mu_{0}A\leq\mu_{1}A
for every
A\in\mathscr{B}_{\tau}
.
Every Borel measure on a strongly Lindelöf space has support, e.g.
[
33
, p. 148]
.

Let
\Sigma
be a
\sigma
-algebra over
X
.
We denote by
\mathcal{L}^{0}(\Sigma)
, resp.
\mathcal{L}^{\infty}(\Sigma)
, the vector space of all everywhere-defined real-valued, resp. uniformly bounded, (
\Sigma
-)measurable functions on
X
.
When
\mu
is a measure on
(X,\Sigma)
, we denote by
L^{0}(\mu)
the corresponding vector space of
\mu
-classes.
We denote by
\mathcal{L}^{2}(\mu)
the vector space of all
\mu
-square-integrable functions in
\mathcal{L}^{0}(\Sigma)
, by
L^{2}(\mu)
the corresponding space of
\mu
-classes.
Let the corresponding definition of
\mathcal{L}^{p}(\mu)
, resp.
L^{p}(\mu)
, be given for all
p\in[1,\infty)
.

As a general rule, we denote measurable functions by either
\hat{f}
or
\tilde{f}
, and classes of measurable functions up to a.e. equality simply by
f
.
When
\mu
has full support on
X
, we drop this distinction for
\tau
-continuous functions, simply writing
f
for both the class and its unique
\tau
-continuous representative.

Measurability and continuity of Lipschitz functions

Let
(X,\tau)
be a Hausdorff space,
\mathsf{d}\colon X^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\times 2}}}\rightarrow[0,\infty]
be an extended distance on
X
. Let
\hat{f}\colon X\rightarrow[-\infty,\infty]
be
\mathsf{d}
-Lipschitz with
\hat{f}\not\equiv\pm\infty
. In general,
\hat{f}
is
neither
everywhere finite, nor
\tau
-continuous, nor
\mathscr{B}_{\tau}^{\mathsf{m}}
-measurable, see
[
18
]
. For a given
\sigma
-algebra
\Sigma
on
X
, this motivates to set

\displaystyle\mathrm{Lip}(\mathsf{d},\Sigma)\eqdef\mathrm{Lip}(\mathsf{d})\ \cap

\displaystyle\ \mathcal{L}^{0}(\Sigma)\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,

\displaystyle\mathrm{Lip}_{b}(\mathsf{d},\Sigma)\eqdef\mathrm{Lip}_{b}(\mathsf{d})\ \cap

\displaystyle\ \mathcal{L}^{0}(\Sigma)\,\,\mathrm{.}

\displaystyle\mathrm{Lip}(\mathsf{d},\tau)\eqdef\mathrm{Lip}(\mathsf{d})\ \cap

\displaystyle\ \mathcal{C}(\tau)\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,

\displaystyle\mathrm{Lip}_{b}(\mathsf{d},\tau)\eqdef\mathrm{Lip}_{b}(\mathsf{d})\ \cap

\displaystyle\ \mathcal{C}(\tau)\,\,\mathrm{.}

Main assumptions

Everywhere in the following,
\mathbb{X}
is a quadruple
(X,\tau,\Sigma,\mathsf{m})
so that
\mathscr{B}_{\tau}\subset\Sigma\subset\mathscr{B}_{\tau}^{\mathsf{m}}
, the reference measure
\mathsf{m}
is positive
\sigma
-finite on
(X,\Sigma)
, and one of the following holds:

(1)

(X,\tau)
is a Hausdorff space;

(2)

(X,\tau)
is a topological Luzin space and
\mathrm{supp}[\mathsf{m}]=X
;

(3)

(X,\tau)
is a second countable locally compact Hausdorff space,
\mathsf{m}
is Radon and
\mathrm{supp}[\mathsf{m}]=X
.

2.3.
Dirichlet spaces

Given a bilinear form
(Q,\mathscr{D}({Q}))
on a Hilbert space
H
, we write

\displaystyle Q(h)\eqdef Q(h,h)\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,\qquad Q_{\alpha}(h_{0},h_{1})\eqdef Q(h_{0},h_{1})+\alpha\left\langle h_{0}\,\middle|\,h_{1}\right\rangle\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,\alpha>0\,\,\mathrm{.}

Let
\mathbb{X}
be satisfying Assumption
1
. A
Dirichlet form on
L^{2}(\mathsf{m})
is a non-negative definite densely defined closed symmetric bilinear form
(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
on
L^{2}(\mathsf{m})
satisfying the Markov property

\displaystyle f_{0}\eqdef 0\vee f\wedge 1\in\mathcal{F}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\mathcal{E}(f_{0})\leq\mathcal{E}(f)\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,\qquad f\in\mathcal{F}\,\,\mathrm{.}

If not otherwise stated,
\mathcal{F}
is always regarded as a Hilbert space with norm
\left\lVert{\,\cdot\,}\right\rVert_{\mathcal{F}}\eqdef\mathcal{E}_{1}({\,\cdot\,})^{1/2}=\sqrt{\mathcal{E}({\,\cdot\,})+\left\lVert{\,\cdot\,}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathsf{m})}^{2}}
.
A
Dirichlet space
is a pair
(\mathbb{X},\mathcal{E})
, where
\mathbb{X}
satisfies
1
and
(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
is a Dirichlet form on
L^{2}(\mathsf{m})
.
A
pseudo-core
is any
\mathcal{F}
-dense linear subspace of
\mathcal{F}
.

2.3.1.
Quasi-notions

For any
A\in\mathscr{B}_{\tau}
set
\mathcal{F}_{A}\eqdef\left\{u\in\mathcal{F}:u=0\text{~$\mathsf{m}$-a.e.~on~}X\setminus A\right\}
.
A sequence
\left(A_{n}\right)_{n}\subset\mathscr{B}_{\tau}
is a
Borel
\mathcal{E}
-nest
if
\cup_{n}\mathcal{F}_{A_{n}}
is dense in
\mathcal{F}
.
For any
A\in\mathscr{B}_{\tau}
, let
(p)
be a proposition defined with respect to
A
. We say that ‘
(p_{A})
holds’ if
A
satisfies
(p)
.
A
(p)
-
\mathcal{E}
-nest
is a Borel nest
\left(A_{n}\right)
so that
(p_{A_{n}})
holds for every
n
. In particular, a
closed
\mathcal{E}
-nest
, henceforth simply referred to as an
\mathcal{E}
-nest
, is a Borel
\mathcal{E}
-nest consisting of closed sets.

A set
N\subset X
is
\mathcal{E}
-polar
if there exists an
\mathcal{E}
-nest
\left(F_{n}\right)_{n}
so that
N\subset X\setminus\cup_{n}F_{n}
.
A set
G\subset X
is
\mathcal{E}
-quasi-open
if there exists an
\mathcal{E}
-nest
\left(F_{n}\right)_{n}
so that
G\cap F_{n}
is relatively open in
F_{n}
for every
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
.
A set
F
is
\mathcal{E}
-quasi-closed
if
X\setminus F
is
\mathcal{E}
-quasi-open.
Without loss of generality, and without explicit mention, we will assume that
\mathcal{E}
-quasi-open/closed, sets are additionally Borel measurable, see
[
18
, Lem. 2.6]
.
Any countable union or finite intersection of
\mathcal{E}
-quasi-open sets is
\mathcal{E}
-quasi-open; analogously, any countable intersection or finite union of
\mathcal{E}
-quasi-closed sets is
\mathcal{E}
-quasi-closed; see
[
24
, Lem. 2.3]
.

A property
(p_{x})
depending on
x\in X
holds
\mathcal{E}
-
quasi-everywhere
(in short:
\mathcal{E}
-q.e.) if there exists an
\mathcal{E}
-polar set
N
so that
(p_{x})
holds for every
x\in X\setminus N
.
Given sets
A_{0},A_{1}\subset X
, we write
A_{0}\subset A_{1}

\mathcal{E}
-q.e. if
\car_{A_{0}}\leq\car_{A_{1}}

\mathcal{E}
-q.e. Let the analogous definition of
A_{0}=A_{1}

\mathcal{E}
-q.e. be given.

A function
\hat{f}\in\mathcal{L}^{0}(\Sigma)
is
\mathcal{E}
-quasi-continuous
if there exists an
\mathcal{E}
-nest
\left(F_{n}\right)_{n}
so that
\hat{f}\big\lvert_{F_{n}}
is continuous for every
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
.
Equivalently,
\tilde{f}
is
\mathcal{E}
-quasi-continuous if and only if it is
\mathcal{E}
-q.e. finite and
\tilde{f}^{-1}(U)
is
\mathcal{E}
-quasi-open for every open
U\subset{\mathbb{R}}
, see e.g.
[
25
, p. 70]
.
Whenever
f\in L^{0}(\mathsf{m})
has an
\mathcal{E}
-quasi-continuous
\mathsf{m}
-version, we denote it by
\tilde{f}\in\mathcal{L}^{0}(\Sigma)
.

Spaces of measures

We write
\mathfrak{M}^{+}_{b}(\Sigma)
, resp.
\mathfrak{M}^{+}_{\sigma}(\Sigma)
,
\mathfrak{M}^{\pm}_{b}(\Sigma)
,
\mathfrak{M}^{\pm}_{\sigma}(\Sigma)
, for the space of finite, resp.
\sigma
-finite, finite signed, extended
\sigma
-finite signed, measures on
(X,\Sigma)
. A further subscript ‘
r
’ indicates (sub-)spaces of Radon measures, e.g.
\mathfrak{M}^{+}_{b\text{{r}}}(\Sigma)
.
We write
\mathfrak{M}^{\pm}_{\sigma}(\Sigma,\mathscr{N}_{\mathcal{E}})
for the space of extended
\sigma
-finite signed measures not charging sets in the family
\mathscr{N}_{\mathcal{E}}
of
\mathcal{E}
-polar Borel subsets of
X
.

2.3.2.
General properties

Let
\mathbb{X}
be a topological measure space as in §
2.2
.
When
(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
is a Dirichlet form on
L^{2}(\mathsf{m})
, we say that
(\mathbb{X},\mathcal{E})
is a
Dirichlet space
.

A Dirichlet space
(\mathbb{X},\mathcal{E})
is
quasi-regular
if each of the following holds:

(1)

there exists an
\mathcal{E}
-nest
\left(F_{n}\right)_{n}
consisting of
\tau
-compact sets;

(2)

there exists a dense subset of
\mathcal{F}
the elements of which all have
\mathcal{E}
-quasi-continuous
\mathsf{m}
-versions;

(3)

there exists an
\mathcal{E}
-polar set
N
and a countable family
\left(u_{n}\right)_{n}
of functions
u_{n}\in\mathcal{F}
having
\mathcal{E}
-quasi-continuous versions
\tilde{u}_{n}
so that
\left(\tilde{u}_{n}\right)_{n}
separates points in
X\setminus N
.

Let
(\mathbb{X},\mathcal{E})
be a quasi-regular Dirichlet space,
\left(F_{n}\right)_{n}
be an
\mathcal{E}
-nest witnessing its quasi-regularity, and set
X_{0}\eqdef\cup_{n}F_{n}
, endowed with the trace topology
\tau_{0}
,
\sigma
-algebra
\Sigma_{0}
, and the restriction
\mathsf{m}_{0}
of
\mathsf{m}
to
\Sigma_{0}
.
Then,
\mathbb{X}_{0}
satisfies
2
, and the space
L^{p}(\mathsf{m})
may be canonically identified with the space
L^{p}(\mathsf{m}_{0})
,
p\in[0,\infty]
, since
X\setminus X_{0}
is
\mathcal{E}
-polar, hence
\mathsf{m}
-negligible.
By letting
\mathcal{E}_{0}
denote the image of
\mathcal{E}
under this identification,
(\mathbb{X}_{0},\mathcal{E}_{0})
is a quasi-regular Dirichlet space, and
\mathcal{F}_{0}
is canonically linearly isometrically isomorphic to
\mathcal{F}
. See
[
33
, Rmk. IV.3.2(iii)]
for the details of this construction.

Remark 2.4
.

When considering a quasi-regular Dirichlet space
(\mathbb{X},\mathcal{E})
, we may and shall therefore assume, with no loss of generality, that
\mathbb{X}
satisfies
2
.
In particular
(X,\tau)
is separable.

A Dirichlet space
(\mathbb{X},\mathcal{E})
with
\mathbb{X}
satisfying
2
is

•

local
if
\mathcal{E}(f,g)=0
for every
f,g\in\mathcal{F}
with
\mathrm{supp}[f]
,
\mathrm{supp}[g]
compact,
\mathrm{supp}[f]\cap\mathrm{supp}[g]=\emp
;

•

strongly local
if
\mathcal{E}(f,g)=0
for every
f,g\in\mathcal{F}
with
\mathrm{supp}[f]
,
\mathrm{supp}[g]
compact and
f
constant on a neighborhood of
\mathrm{supp}[g]
;

•

regular
if
\mathbb{X}
satisfies
3
, and
\mathcal{C}_{0}(\tau)\cap\mathcal{F}
is both dense in
\mathcal{F}
and dense in the space
\mathcal{C}_{0}(\tau)
of all
\tau
-continuous functions on
X
vanishing at infinity.

Domains

Let
(\mathbb{X},\mathcal{E})
be a Dirichlet space. We write
\mathcal{F}_{b}\eqdef\mathcal{F}\cap L^{\infty}(\mathsf{m})
. The
extended domain

\mathcal{F}_{e}
of
(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
is the space of all functions
f\in L^{0}(\mathsf{m})
so that there exists an
\mathcal{E}^{1/2}
-fundamental (i.e. Cauchy) sequence
\left(f_{n}\right)_{n}\subset\mathcal{F}
with
\mathsf{m}
-a.e.-
\lim_{n}f_{n}=f
. We write
\mathcal{F}_{eb}\eqdef\mathcal{F}_{e}\cap L^{\infty}(\mathsf{m})
. The bilinear form
\mathcal{E}
on
\mathcal{F}
extends to a (non-relabeled) bilinear form on
\mathcal{F}_{e}
,
[
31
, Prop. 3.1]
. Furthermore,

•

\mathcal{F}_{b}
is an algebra with respect to the pointwise multiplication,
[
11
, Prop. I.2.3.2]
;

•

if
(\mathbb{X},\mathcal{E})
is quasi-regular, then
\mathcal{F}_{b}
is dense in
\mathcal{F}
,
[
31
, Cor. 2.1]
;

•

if
(\mathbb{X},\mathcal{E})
is quasi-regular, then
\mathcal{F}
is separable,
[
33
, Prop. IV.3.3, p. 102]
;

Quasi-interior, quasi-closure

Let
(\mathbb{X},\mathcal{E})
be a quasi-regular Dirichlet space. Every
f\in\mathcal{F}
has an
\mathcal{E}
-q.e.-unique

\mathcal{E}
-quasi-continuous
\mathsf{m}
-representative, denoted by
\tilde{f}
,
[
33
, Prop. IV.3.3.(iii)]
.
For
A\subset X
set

\displaystyle\mathscr{U}(A)\eqdef

\displaystyle\left\{G:G\text{ is an~$\mathcal{E}$-quasi-open subset of }A\right\}\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,

\displaystyle\mathscr{F}(A)\eqdef

\displaystyle\left\{F:F\text{ is an~$\mathcal{E}$-quasi-closed superset of }A\right\}\,\,\mathrm{.}

By
[
24
, Thm. 2.7]
,
\mathscr{U}(A)
has an
\mathcal{E}
-q.e.-maximal element denoted by
\interior_{\mathcal{E}}A
,
\mathcal{E}
-quasi-open, and called the
\mathcal{E}
-
quasi-interior
of
A
.
Analogously,
\mathscr{F}(A)
has an
\mathcal{E}
-q.e.-minimal element denoted by
\mathrm{cl}_{\mathcal{E}}A
,
\mathcal{E}
-quasi-closed, and called the
\mathcal{E}
-
quasi-closure
of
A
.

Carré du champ operators

Let
(\mathbb{X},\mathcal{E})
be a Dirichlet space with
\mathbb{X}
satisfying
1
, and set

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{f,g}(h)\eqdef\mathcal{E}(fh,g)+\mathcal{E}(gh,f)-\mathcal{E}(fg,h)\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,\qquad\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{f}(h)\eqdef\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{f,f}(h)\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,\qquad f,g,h\in\mathcal{F}_{b}\,\,\mathrm{.}

We say that
(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
admits
carré du champ operator

\Gamma
if there exists
\Gamma\colon\mathcal{F}_{b}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\times 2}}\to L^{1}(\mathsf{m})
such that

\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{f,g}(h)=2\int h\,\Gamma(f,g)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mathsf{m}\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,\qquad f,g,h\in\mathcal{F}_{b}\,\,\mathrm{.}

Energy measures

Not every quasi-regular strongly local Dirichlet space admits a carré du champ operator.
However we have the following:

Theorem 2.5

(Cf.
[
31
, Thm. 5.2, Lem.s 5.1, 5.2]
)
.

Let
(\mathbb{X},\mathcal{E})
be a quasi-regular strongly local Dirichlet space. Then, a bilinear form
\mu_{{\,\cdot\,},{\,\cdot\,}}
is defined on
\mathcal{F}_{b}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\times 2}}}
with values in
\mathfrak{M}^{\pm}_{b\text{{r}}}(\mathscr{B}_{\tau},\mathscr{N}_{\mathcal{E}})
by

(2.3)

\displaystyle 2\int\tilde{h}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mu_{f,g}=\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{f,g}(h)\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,\qquad f,g,h\in\mathcal{F}_{b}\,\,\mathrm{.}

The bilinear form
\mu_{{\,\cdot\,},{\,\cdot\,}}
constructed above is called the
energy measure
of
(\mathbb{X},\mathcal{E})
.
When
(\mathbb{X},\mathcal{E})
is quasi-regular strongly local and additionally admits carré du champ operator, then
\mu_{f,g}\ll\mathsf{m}
for every
f,g\in\mathcal{F}_{b}
, in which case
\Gamma(f,g)\eqdef\frac{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mu_{f,g}}{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mathsf{m}}
is the Radon–Nikodym derivative of
\mu_{f,g}
w.r.t.
\mathsf{m}
.

2.4.
Broad local spaces and energy moderance

Let
(\mathbb{X},\mathcal{E})
be a quasi-regular Dirichlet space.
For any
\mathcal{E}
-quasi-open
E\subset X
set

(2.4)

\displaystyle\mathscr{G}(E)\eqdef

\displaystyle\ \left\{G_{\bullet}\eqdef\left(G_{n}\right)_{n}:G_{n}\text{~$\mathcal{E}$-quasi-open,~}G_{n}\subset G_{n+1}\text{~$\mathcal{E}$-q.e.,~}\cup_{n}G_{n}=E\;{\mathcal{E}}\text{-q.e.}\right\}\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,

When
E=X
we simply write
\mathscr{G}
in place of
\mathscr{G}(X)
.
We say that
G_{\bullet}\in\mathscr{G}(E)
is
\mathcal{E}
-moderate if for each
n\in{\mathbb{N}}_{0}
there exists
e_{n}\in\mathcal{F}
with
e_{n}\geq 1

\mathsf{m}
-a.e. on
G_{n}
, and we set

(2.5)

\displaystyle\mathscr{G}_{0}(E)\eqdef

\displaystyle\left\{G_{\bullet}\in\mathscr{G}(E):G_{\bullet}\text{~is $\mathcal{E}$-moderate}\right\}\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,

\displaystyle\mathscr{G}_{c}(E)\eqdef

\displaystyle\left\{G_{\bullet}\in\mathscr{G}_{0}(E):\mathrm{cl}_{\tau}G_{n}\text{~is $\tau$-compact for all~$n$}\right\}\,\,\mathrm{.}

For
G_{\bullet}\in\mathscr{G}_{0}(E)
, we write
e_{\bullet}\eqdef\left(e_{n}\right)_{n}
for any sequence of functions witnessing the
\mathcal{E}
-moderance of
G_{\bullet}
.
When the sequence
e_{\bullet}
is relevant, we write as well
(G_{\bullet},e_{\bullet})\in\mathscr{G}_{0}(E)
.
As usual, we omit the specification of
E=X
.
Since
\car\in{{\mathcal{F}}^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{loc}}}
by (
2.11
), then
\mathscr{G}_{0}\neq\emp
.
Clearly,
\mathscr{G}_{c}\subset\mathscr{G}_{0}\subsetneq\mathscr{G}
.

Definition 2.6

(
\mathcal{E}
-moderance,
[
18
, Dfn. 2.22]
)
.

Let
(\mathbb{X},\mathcal{E})
be a quasi-regular Dirichlet space. For a measure
\mu\in\mathfrak{M}^{+}_{\sigma}(\mathscr{B}_{\tau},\mathscr{N}_{\mathcal{E}})
we say that
(G_{\bullet},e_{\bullet})\in\mathscr{G}_{0}
is
\mu
-moderated
if
e_{\bullet}
is additionally so that
\mu\tilde{e}_{n}<\infty
for every
n
.
We say that
\mu
is:

•

\mathcal{E}
-moderate
if there exists a
\mu
-moderated
G_{\bullet}\in\mathscr{G}_{0}
;

•

absolutely
\mathcal{E}
-moderate
if for every
G_{\bullet}\in\mathscr{G}_{0}
there exists
e_{\bullet}
so that
(G_{\bullet},e_{\bullet})
is
\mu
-moderated.

We refer the reader to
[
18
, §2.5.2]
for the heuristics behind all the above definitions.

2.4.1.
Broad local spaces

For
G_{\bullet}\in\mathscr{G}(E)
and
\mathscr{A}\subset L^{0}(\mathsf{m})
, we say that
f\in L^{0}(\mathsf{m}_{E})
is in the
broad local space

{{\mathscr{A}}^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{loc}}}(E,G_{\bullet})
if for every
n
there exists
f_{n}\in\mathscr{A}
so that
f_{n}=f

\mathsf{m}
-a.e. on
G_{n}
.
The
broad local space

{{\mathscr{A}}^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{loc}}}(E)
of
(\mathbb{X},\mathcal{E})
relative to
E
is the space
[
31
, §4, p. 696]
,

(2.6)

\displaystyle{{\mathscr{A}}^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{loc}}}(E)\eqdef\bigcup_{G_{\bullet}\in\mathscr{G}(E)}{{\mathscr{A}}^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{loc}}}(E,G_{\bullet})\,\,\mathrm{.}

The set
{{\mathscr{A}}^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{loc}}}(E,G_{\bullet})
depends on
G_{\bullet}
. Again, we omit the specification of
E=X
.

Proposition 2.7

(Extension of energy measure,
[
18
, Prop. 2.12]
)
.

Let
(\mathbb{X},\mathcal{E})
be a quasi-regular strongly local Dirichlet space.
Then, the quadratic form
\mu_{{\,\cdot\,}}\colon\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\mathfrak{M}^{+}_{b\text{{r}}}(\mathscr{B}_{\tau},\mathscr{N}_{\mathcal{E}})
associated to the bilinear form
\mu_{{\,\cdot\,},{\,\cdot\,}}
in (
2.3
) uniquely extends to a non-relabeled form on
{{\mathcal{F}}^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{loc}}}
with values in
\mathfrak{M}^{+}_{\sigma}(\mathscr{B}_{\tau},\mathscr{N}_{\mathcal{E}})
, satisfying:

(1)

the representation property

(2.7)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}(f,g)=\tfrac{1}{2}\mu_{f,g}X\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,\qquad f,g\in\mathcal{F}_{e}\,\,\mathrm{;}\;\,

(2)

the truncation property

(2.8)

\displaystyle f\wedge g\in{{\mathcal{F}}^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{loc}}}\quad\text{and}\quad\mu_{f\wedge g}=\car_{\{\tilde{f}\leq\tilde{g}\}}\mu_{f}+\car_{\{\tilde{f}>\tilde{g}\}}\mu_{g}\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,\qquad f,g\in{{\mathcal{F}}^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{loc}}}\,\,\mathrm{;}

\displaystyle f\vee g\in{{\mathcal{F}}^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{loc}}}\quad\text{and}\quad\mu_{f\vee g}=\car_{\{\tilde{f}\leq\tilde{g}\}}\mu_{g}+\car_{\{\tilde{f}>\tilde{g}\}}\mu_{f}\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,\qquad f,g\in{{\mathcal{F}}^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{loc}}}\,\,\mathrm{;}

(3)

the chain rule

(2.9)

\varphi\circ f\in{{\mathcal{F}}^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{loc}}}\quad\text{and}\quad\mu_{\varphi\circ f}=(\varphi^{\prime}\circ\tilde{f})^{2}\cdot\mu_{f}\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,\qquad f\in{{\mathcal{F}}^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{loc}}}\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,\quad\begin{aligned} &\varphi\in\mathcal{C}^{1}({\mathbb{R}})\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,\\
&\varphi(0)=0\end{aligned}\,\,\mathrm{;}\;\,

(4)

the strong locality property

(2.10)

\displaystyle\car_{G}\mu_{f}=\car_{G}\mu_{g}\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,\qquad G\text{~$\mathcal{E}$-quasi-open}\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,f,g\in{{\mathcal{F}}^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{loc}}}\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,f\equiv g\text{~$\mathsf{m}$-a.e.\ on $G$}\,\,\mathrm{.}

Furthermore,
{{\mathcal{F}}^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{loc}}}
is an algebra for the pointwise multiplication, and

(2.11)

\displaystyle\car\in{{\mathcal{F}}^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{loc}}}\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,\qquad\mu_{\car}\equiv 0\,\,\mathrm{.}

Corollary 2.8

(Extension of carré du champ operator)
.

Let
(\mathbb{X},\mathcal{E})
be a quasi-regular strongly local Dirichlet space admitting carré du champ operator
(\Gamma,\mathcal{F}_{b})
.
Then, the bilinear form
\Gamma\colon\mathcal{F}_{b}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\times 2}}\to L^{1}(\mathsf{m})
extends to a non-relabeled bilinear form
\Gamma\colon{{\mathcal{F}}^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{loc}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\times 2}}\to L^{0}(\mathsf{m})
representing the quadratic form
\mu_{{\,\cdot\,}}\colon{{\mathcal{F}}^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{loc}}}\to\mathfrak{M}^{+}_{\sigma}(\mathscr{B}_{\tau},\mathscr{N}_{\mathcal{E}})
in Proposition
2.7
in the sense that
\tfrac{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mu_{f}}{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mathsf{m}}=\Gamma(f,f)
for every
f\in{{\mathcal{F}}^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{loc}}}
.

Furthermore, the form
\Gamma\colon{{\mathcal{F}}^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{loc}}}\to L^{0}(\mathsf{m})
satisfies the strong locality property

(2.12)

\Gamma(f,h)=\Gamma(g,h)\quad\mathsf{m}\text{-a.e.}\quad\text{on}\quad\left\{f\equiv g\right\}\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,\qquad f,g,h\in{{\mathcal{F}}^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{loc}}}\,\,\mathrm{.}

Proof.

Since
{{\mathcal{F}}^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{loc}}}={{(\mathcal{F}_{b})}^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{loc}}}
, e.g.
[
18
, Eqn. (2.15)]
, the extension is an immediate consequence of the definition of
\Gamma\colon\mathcal{F}_{b}\to L^{1}(\mathsf{m})
together with the strong locality property of
\mu_{{\,\cdot\,}}
in Proposition
2.7
4
.
Noting that our definition of broad local space
{{\mathcal{F}}^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{loc}}}
is more restrictive than the definition of local space in
[
11
, Dfn. I.7.1.3]
, the equality (
2.12
) is a standard consequence of e.g.
[
11
, Prop. I.7.1.4]
.
∎

2.4.2.
Local domains of bounded-energy and intrinsic distances

Let
(\mathbb{X},\mathcal{E})
be a quasi-regular strongly local Dirichlet space, and
\mu\in\mathfrak{M}^{+}_{\sigma}(\mathscr{B}_{\tau},\mathscr{N}_{\mathcal{E}})
. For
\mathcal{E}
-quasi-open
E\subset X
and any
G_{\bullet}\in\mathscr{G}_{0}(E)
, set

\displaystyle\mathbb{L}^{\mu}_{\mathrm{loc}}(E,G_{\bullet})\eqdef

\displaystyle\left\{f\in{{\mathcal{F}}^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{loc}}}(E,G_{\bullet}):\mu_{f}\leq\mu\right\}\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,\qquad\mathbb{L}^{\mu}_{\mathrm{loc}}(E)\eqdef\big\{f\in{{\mathcal{F}}^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{loc}}}(E):\mu_{f}\leq\mu\big\}\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,

\displaystyle\mathbb{L}^{\mu}(E)\eqdef

\displaystyle\ \mathbb{L}^{\mu}_{\mathrm{loc}}(E)\cap\mathcal{F}\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,\quad\ \mathbb{L}^{\mu,\tau}_{\mathrm{loc}}(E)\eqdef\mathbb{L}^{\mu}_{\mathrm{loc}}(E)\cap\Cont(E,\tau)\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,\quad\ \mathbb{L}^{\mu,\tau}(E)\eqdef\mathbb{L}^{\mu,\tau}_{\mathrm{loc}}(E)\cap\mathcal{F}\,\,\mathrm{.}

As usual, we omit
E
from the notation whenever
E=X
. For
G_{\bullet}\in\mathscr{G}_{0}(E)
we additionally denote by

\displaystyle\mathbb{L}^{\mu}_{\mathrm{loc},b}(E,G_{\bullet})\eqdef\mathbb{L}^{\mu}_{\mathrm{loc}}(E,G_{\bullet})\cap L^{\infty}(\mathsf{m})

the space of
\mathsf{m}
-essentially uniformly bounded functions in
\mathbb{L}^{\mu}_{\mathrm{loc}}(E,G_{\bullet})
.
Let the analogous definitions for
\mathbb{L}^{\mu}_{\mathrm{loc},b}(E)
,
\mathbb{L}^{\mu}_{b}(E)
,
\mathbb{L}^{\mu,\tau}_{\mathrm{loc},b}(E)
, and
\mathbb{L}^{\mu,\tau}_{b}(E)
be given.

Lemma 2.9

(
[
18
, Prop. 2.26]
)
.

Let
(\mathbb{X},\mathcal{E})
be a quasi-regular strongly local Dirichlet space, and
\mu\in\mathfrak{M}^{+}_{\sigma}(\mathscr{B}_{\tau},\mathscr{N}_{\mathcal{E}})
be absolutely
\mathcal{E}
-moderate.
Then, for every
G_{\bullet},G_{\bullet}^{\prime}\in\mathscr{G}_{0}
,

\mathbb{L}^{\mu}_{\mathrm{loc},b}(G_{\bullet})=\mathbb{L}^{\mu}_{\mathrm{loc},b}(G_{\bullet}^{\prime})\,\,\mathrm{.}

In particular, if
f\in\mathbb{L}^{\mu}_{\mathrm{loc},b}
, then there exists
G_{\bullet}\in\mathscr{G}_{c}
additionally so that
\mathrm{cl}_{\tau}G_{k}\subset G_{k+1}
for all
k\in{\mathbb{N}}
, and
f_{\bullet}\subset\mathcal{F}
, such that
(G_{\bullet},f_{\bullet})
witnesses that
f\in\mathbb{L}^{\mu}_{\mathrm{loc},b}
.

Proof.

The first assertion is shown in
[
18
, Prop. 2.26]
. The second follows from
[
31
, Lem. 3.5(iii)]
.
∎

Lemma 2.10
.

Let
(\mathbb{X},\mathcal{E})
be a quasi-regular strongly local Dirichlet space, and
\mu\in\mathfrak{M}^{+}_{\sigma}(\mathscr{B}_{\tau},\mathscr{N}_{\mathcal{E}})
be absolutely
\mathcal{E}
-moderate.
If
\car\in\mathcal{F}
, then

\mathbb{L}^{\mu}_{\mathrm{loc},b}=\mathbb{L}^{\mu}_{b}\,\,\mathrm{.}

Proof.

Since
\car\in\mathcal{F}
, the constant sequence
X_{\bullet}\eqdef\left(X\right)_{k}
satisfies
X_{\bullet}\in\mathscr{G}_{0}
.
Since
\mu
is absolutely
\mathcal{E}
-moderate,
X_{\bullet}
is
\mu
-moderated, and we have that
\mathbb{L}^{\mu}_{\mathrm{loc},b}=\mathbb{L}^{\mu}_{\mathrm{loc},b}(X_{\bullet})
by Lemma
2.9
.
On the other hand, it follows from the definition that
\mathbb{L}^{\mu}_{\mathrm{loc},b}(X_{\bullet})=\mathbb{L}^{\mu}_{b}
.
∎

2.4.3.
Intrinsic distances

We recall a definition of generalized intrinsic distance introduced in
[
18
]
.
For more information on intrinsic distances, we refer the reader to
[
18
, §2.6]
.

Definition 2.11

(Intrinsic distance)
.

Let
\mu\in\mathfrak{M}^{+}_{\sigma}(\mathscr{B}_{\tau},\mathscr{N}_{\mathcal{E}})
. The
intrinsic distance generated by
\mu
is the extended pseudo-distance
\mathsf{d}_{\mu}\colon X^{\times 2}\rightarrow[0,\infty]
defined as

(2.13)

\displaystyle\mathsf{d}_{\mu}(x,y)\eqdef\sup\big\{f(x)-f(y):f\in\mathbb{L}^{\mu,\tau}_{\mathrm{loc},b}\big\}\,\,\mathrm{.}

Note that
\mathsf{d}_{\mu}
is always
\tau^{\scriptscriptstyle{\times 2}}
-l.s.c., hence
\mathscr{B}_{\tau^{\scriptscriptstyle{\times 2}}}
- and
\Sigma^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes 2}}}
-measurable, for it is the supremum of a family of
\tau^{\scriptscriptstyle{\times 2}}
-continuous functions. Furthermore,
\mathsf{d}_{\mu}
is
\tau
-admissible by definition, as witnessed by the bounded uniformity

\displaystyle\mathsf{UP}_{\mu}\eqdef\left\{\mathsf{d}_{f}(x,y)\eqdef\left\lvert f(x)-f(y)\right\rvert:f\in\mathbb{L}^{\mu,\tau}_{\mathrm{loc},b}\right\}\,\,\mathrm{.}

In the case when
(\mathbb{X},\mathcal{E})
is a regular strongly local Dirichlet space and
\mu\eqdef\mathsf{m}
, (
2.13
) coincides with the standard definition of intrinsic distance, see
[
18
, Prop. 2.31]
.

2.4.4.
Localizability

Let us recall (a version of) the definition of
\mu
-uniform
\tau
-localizability
given in
[
18
, §3.4]
, also cf.
[
18
, §2.5.2]
, and amounting to the existence of good Sobolev cut-off functions.

Definition 2.12
.

Let
(\mathbb{X},\mathcal{E})
be a quasi-regular strongly local Dirichlet space, and
\mu\in\mathfrak{M}^{+}_{\sigma}(\mathscr{B}_{\tau},\mathscr{N}_{\mathcal{E}})
.
We say that
(\mathbb{X},\mathcal{E},\mu)
is
\mu
-uniformly latticially
, resp.
algebraically
,
\tau
-localizable
, in short:
(\mathbb{X},\mathcal{E},\mu)
satisfies
(\mathsf{Loc}_{\mu,\tau})
, resp.
(\mathsf{Loc}^{\times}_{\mu,\tau})
, if
\mu
is
\mathcal{E}
-moderate, and there exists a latticial approximation to the identity
\left(\theta_{n}\right)_{n}
, resp. an algebraic approximation to the identity
\left(\psi_{n}\right)_{n}
, uniformly bounded by
\mu
in energy measure, viz.

(\mathsf{Loc}_{\mu,\tau})

\displaystyle 0\leq\theta_{n}\leq\theta_{n+1}\nearrow_{n}\infty\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,\qquad\theta_{n}\in\mathbb{L}^{\mu,\tau}_{b}\quad\big({\subset\mathcal{C}_{b}(\tau)}\big)\,\,\mathrm{.}

(\mathsf{Loc}^{\times}_{\mu,\tau})

resp.

\displaystyle 0\leq\psi_{n}\leq\psi_{n+1}\nearrow_{n}1\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,\qquad\psi_{n}\in\mathbb{L}^{\mu,\tau}_{b}\quad\big({\subset\mathcal{C}_{b}(\tau)}\big)\,\,\mathrm{.}

Remark 2.13

(
[
18
, Rmk. 3.28, 3.29]
)
.

Let us note the following.

(1)

We may additionally assume with no loss of generality that
\inter_{\tau}\left\{\theta_{n}=n\right\}\neq\emp
and that
\theta_{n}\leq n
for every
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
.

(2)

If
\car\in\mathcal{F}
, then

(

Loc
×

μ
,
τ

)

is trivially satisfied with
\psi_{n}\eqdef\car
for all
n
, and the same holds for

(

Loc

μ
,
τ

)

for the functions
\theta_{n}
in
[
18
, Rmk. 3.29]
.

(3)

(

Loc

μ
,
τ

)

implies

(

Loc
×

μ
,
τ

)

by setting
\psi_{n}\eqdef\theta_{n}\vee 1
.

2.5.
Rademacher and Sobolev-to-Lipschitz properties

Let
(\mathbb{X},\mathcal{E})
be a quasi-regular strongly local Dirichlet space,
\mu\in\mathfrak{M}^{+}_{\sigma}(\mathscr{B}_{\tau})
, and
\mathsf{d}\colon X^{\scriptscriptstyle{\times 2}}\to[0,\infty]
be an extended pseudo-distance.

Definition 2.14

(Rademacher and Sobolev-to-Lipschitz properties
[
18
]
)
.

We say that
(\mathbb{X},\mathcal{E},\mathsf{d},\mu)
has the:

(
\mathsf{Rad}_{\mathsf{d},\mu}
)

Rademacher property
(
for

\Sigma
) if, whenever
\hat{f}\in\mathrm{Lip}^{1}(\mathsf{d},\Sigma)
, then
f\in\mathbb{L}^{\mu}_{\mathrm{loc}}
;

(
{\mathsf{d}}\textrm{-}\mathsf{Rad}_{\mu}
)

distance-Rademacher property
if
\mathsf{d}\leq\mathsf{d}_{\mu}
;

(
\mathsf{ScL}_{\mathsf{m},\tau,\mathsf{d}}
)

Sobolev–to–continuous-Lipschitz property
if each
f\in\mathbb{L}^{\mu}_{\mathrm{loc}}
has an
\mathsf{m}
-representative
\hat{f}\in\mathrm{Lip}^{1}(\mathsf{d},\tau)
;

(
\mathsf{SL}_{\mu,\mathsf{d}}
)

Sobolev–to–Lipschitz property
(
for

\Sigma
) if each
f\in\mathbb{L}^{\mu}_{\mathrm{loc}}
has an
\mathsf{m}
-representative
\hat{f}\in\mathrm{Lip}^{1}(\mathsf{d},\Sigma)
;

(
{\mathsf{d}}\textrm{-}\mathsf{cSL}_{\mu,\mathsf{d}}
)

\mathsf{d}
-continuous-Sobolev–to–Lipschitz property
(
for

\Sigma
) if each
f\in\mathbb{L}^{\mu}_{\mathrm{loc}}
having a
\mathsf{d}
-continuous
\Sigma
-measurable representative
\hat{f}
also has a representative
\tilde{f}\in\mathrm{Lip}^{1}(\mathsf{d},\Sigma^{\mathsf{m}})
(possibly,
\tilde{f}\neq\hat{f}
);

(
\mathsf{cSL}_{\tau,\mathsf{m},\mathsf{d}}
)

continuous-Sobolev–to–Lipschitz property
if each
f\in\mathbb{L}^{\mu,\tau}_{\mathrm{loc}}
satisfies
f\in\mathrm{Lip}^{1}(\mathsf{d},\tau)
;

(
{\mathsf{d}}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SL}_{\mu}
)

distance Sobolev-to-Lipschitz property
if
\mathsf{d}\geq\mathsf{d}_{\mu}
.

As noted in
[
18
]
, in all the above definitions we may equivalently additionally require that
\hat{f}
be uniformly bounded.
In the following, we shall make use of this fact without further mention.

We refer the reader to
[
18
, Rmk.s 3.2, 4.3]
for comments on the terminology and to
[
18
, Lem. 3.6, Prop. 4.2]
for the interplay of all such properties.
For a quasi-regular strongly local Dirichlet space
(\mathbb{X},\mathcal{E})
, they reduce to the following scheme:

(2.14)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathsf{Rad}_{\mathsf{d},\mu})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces({\mathsf{d}}\textrm{-}\mathsf{Rad}_{\mu})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{\cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{Number}{LzDSSuz20}{}{}, Lem.~3.6]}}\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathsf{ScL}_{\mu,\tau,\mathsf{d}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathsf{SL}_{\mu,\mathsf{d}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathsf{cSL}_{\mu,\tau,\mathsf{d}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces({\mathsf{d}}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SL}_{\mu})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{\cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{Number}{LzDSSuz20}{}{}, Prop.~4.2]}}\,\,\mathrm{.}}

Remark 2.15

(About
({\mathsf{d}}\textrm{-}\mathsf{cSL}_{\mu,\mathsf{d}})
)
.

Let us note that, whereas both
({\mathsf{d}}\textrm{-}\mathsf{cSL}_{\mu,\mathsf{d}})
and
(\mathsf{cSL}_{\tau,\mu,\mathsf{d}})
are implied by
(\mathsf{SL}_{\mu,\mathsf{d}})
and coincide on metric spaces, they do
not
— at least in principle — imply each other on extended metric spaces.
In particular, while the
\mathsf{d}
-Lipschitz representative in
(\mathsf{cSL}_{\tau,\mu,\mathsf{d}})
is taken to coincide with the given
\tau
-continuous one, it is important in the definition of
({\mathsf{d}}\textrm{-}\mathsf{cSL}_{\mu,\mathsf{d}})
to allow for the
\mathsf{d}
-Lipschitz representative to be different from the
\mathsf{d}
-continuous one, and for the former to be only
\Sigma^{\mathsf{m}}
-measurable, rather than
\Sigma
-measurable.

Lemma 2.16

(
[
18
, Prop. 3.7]
)
.

Let
(\mathbb{X},\mathcal{E})
be a quasi-regular strongly local Dirichlet space with
\mathbb{X}
satisfying
2
,
\mathsf{d}\colon X^{\scriptscriptstyle{\times 2}}\to[0,\infty]
be an extended pseudo-distance on
X
, and
\mu\in\mathfrak{M}^{+}_{\sigma}(\mathscr{B}_{\tau},\mathscr{N}_{\mathcal{E}})
be
\mathcal{E}
-moderate.
Further assume that
(\mathbb{X},\mathcal{E})
satisfies
({\mathsf{d}}\textrm{-}\mathsf{Rad}_{\mu})
. If
(X,\tau,\mathsf{d})
is a complete extended metric-topological space (Dfn.
2.3
), then so is
(X,\tau,\mathsf{d}_{\mu})
.

2.5.1.
Metric completions

Let
(\mathbb{X},\mathcal{E})
be a Dirichlet space, and
\mathsf{d}\colon X^{\scriptscriptstyle{\times 2}}\rightarrow[0,\infty]
be an extended distance. Further let
(X^{\iota},\mathsf{d}^{\iota})
be the abstract completion of
(X,\mathsf{d})
and denote by
\iota
the completion embedding
\iota\colon X\rightarrow X^{\iota}
.
If
\iota(X)
is a Borel subset of
X^{\iota}
, then
\iota
is
\mathscr{B}_{\tau_{\mathsf{d}}}/\mathscr{B}_{\tau_{\mathsf{d}^{\iota}}}
-measurable, and the image form
(\mathcal{E}^{\iota},\mathcal{F}^{\iota})
of
(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
via
\iota
is well-defined on the image space
\mathbb{X}^{\iota}
.
When
(\mathbb{X}^{\iota},\mathcal{E}^{\iota})
is a quasi-regular strongly local Dirichlet space, we denote its intrinsic distance by
\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}^{\iota}}
.

Remark 2.17
.

Let
(\mathbb{X},\mathcal{E})
be a quasi-regular strongly local Dirichlet space, and
\mathsf{d}\colon X^{\scriptscriptstyle{\times 2}}\to[0,\infty]
be an extended distance on
X
.
In order to discuss the interplay between
(\mathbb{X},\mathcal{E})
and
\mathsf{d}
— precisely: the relation between
\mathsf{d}
and
\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}}
—, it is
natural
to assume that
(X,\mathsf{d})
be complete.
In particular, it is usually not enough to resort to the completion of
(X,\mathsf{d})
, as we now show.

Indeed, let
A\eqdef X^{\iota}\setminus\iota(X)
be the complement of the
\iota
-image of
X
inside its completion.
The set
A
is in general not
\mathcal{E}^{\iota}
-polar, that is,
(\mathbb{X},\mathcal{E})
and
(\mathbb{X}^{\iota},\mathcal{E}^{\iota})
are in general not quasi-homeomorphic.
Furthermore, even in the case when
(\mathbb{X},\mathcal{E})
and
(\mathbb{X}^{\iota},\mathcal{E}^{\iota})
are quasi-homeomorphic, the corresponding intrinsic distances may be not related in any way.
That is, the metric completion
(X^{\iota},\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}}^{\iota})
of
(X,\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}})
may differ (even as a set) from the (extended) metric space
(X^{\iota},\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}^{\iota}})
, where this time we denoted by
X^{\iota}
the
\mathsf{d}
-completion of
(X,\mathsf{d})
and by
\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}^{\iota}}
the intrinsic distance of the Dirichlet form
(\mathcal{E}^{\iota},\mathcal{F}^{\iota})
.
For examples and counterexamples to the above statements, and for their relationship with the Sobolev-to-Lipschitz property, we refer the reader to
[
18
, Ex. 4.7, §4.1]
.

2.6.
Cheeger energies

The content of this section is mostly taken from the detailed discussion of extended metric measure spaces put forward by L. Ambrosio, N. Gigli, and G. Savaré in
[
6
]
, Ambrosio, M. Erbar, and Savaré in
[
4
]
, and the one — more general still — in Savaré’s monograph
[
37
]
.

Definition 2.18

(Extended metric-topological measure space,
[
4
, Dfn. 4.7]
)
.

By an
extended metric-topological measure space

(X,\tau,\mathsf{d},\mathsf{m})
we mean an extended metric-topological space (Dfn.
2.3
) together with a Radon measure
\mathsf{m}
restricted to the Borel
\sigma
-algebra
\mathscr{B}_{\tau}
.
We use the expression
extended metric-topological probability space
to indicate that
\mathsf{m}
is additionally a probability measure.

Let
(X,\tau,\mathsf{d},\mathsf{m})
be an extended metric-topological measure space.

Minimal relaxed slopes

A
\mathscr{B}_{\tau}
-measurable function
G\colon X\to[0,\infty]
is a (
\mathsf{m}
-)
relaxed slope
of
f\in L^{2}(\mathsf{m})
if there exist
\left(f_{n}\right)_{n}\subset\mathrm{Lip}(\mathsf{d},\mathscr{B}_{\tau})
so that

•

L^{2}(\mathsf{m})
-
\lim_{n}f_{n}=f
and
\left\lvert\mathrm{D}f_{n}\right\rvert

L^{2}(\mathsf{m})
-weakly converges to
\tilde{G}\in L^{2}(\mathsf{m})
;

•

\tilde{G}\leq G

\mathsf{m}
-a.e..

We say that
G
is the (
\mathsf{m}
-)
minimal
(
\mathsf{m}
-)
relaxed slope
of
f\in L^{2}(\mathsf{m})
, denoted by
\left\lvert\mathrm{D}f\right\rvert_{*}
, if its
L^{2}(\mathsf{m})
-norm is minimal among those of all relaxed slopes.
The notion is well-posed, and
\left\lvert\mathrm{D}f\right\rvert_{*}
is in fact
\mathsf{m}
-a.e. minimal as well, see
[
6
, §4.1]
.

Minimal weak upper gradients

A Borel probability measure
{\boldsymbol{\pi}}
on
\mathrm{AC}^{2}(I;X)
is a
test plan of bounded compression
if there exists a constant
C=C_{\boldsymbol{\pi}}>0
such that

\displaystyle(\mathrm{ev}_{t})_{\sharp}{\boldsymbol{\pi}}\leq C\mathsf{m}\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,\qquad\mathrm{ev}_{t}\colon\mathrm{AC}^{2}(I;X)\to X\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,\mathrm{ev}_{t}\colon x_{\,\cdot\,}\mapsto x_{t}\,\,\mathrm{.}

A Borel subset
A\subset\mathrm{AC}^{2}(I;X)
is called
negligible
if
{\boldsymbol{\pi}}(A)=0
for every test plan of bounded compression.
A property of
\mathrm{AC}^{2}(I;X)
-curves is said to hold
for a.e.-curve
if it holds for every curve in a co-negligible set.

A
\mathscr{B}_{\tau}
-measurable function
G\colon X\to[0,\infty]
(
\mathsf{m}
-)
weak upper gradient
of
\hat{f}\in\mathcal{L}^{0}(\mathsf{m})
if

\displaystyle\char 12\relax{\hat{f}(x_{1})-\hat{f}(x_{0})}\leq\int_{0}^{1}G(x_{r})\,\left\lvert\dot{x}\right\rvert_{r}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}r<\infty\quad\text{for a.e.\ curve }\left(x_{t}\right)_{t}\in\mathrm{AC}^{2}(I;X)\,\,\mathrm{.}

We say that
G
is the (
\mathsf{m}
-)
minimal
(
\mathsf{m}
-)
weak upper gradient
of
f\in L^{2}(\mathsf{m})
, denoted by
\left\lvert\mathrm{D}f\right\rvert_{w}
, if it is
\mathsf{m}
-.a.e.-minimal among the weak upper gradients of
\hat{f}
for every representative
\hat{f}
of
f
.
See e.g.
[
6
, Dfn. 2.12]
for the well-posedness of this notion, independently of the representatives of
f
.

Asymptotic Lipschitz constants and asymptotic slopes

For
f\in\mathrm{Lip}_{b}(\mathsf{d},\tau)
set

\displaystyle\mathrm{Lip}^{a}_{\mathsf{d}}[f](x,r)\eqdef\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}y,z\in B^{\mathsf{d}}_{r}(x)\\
\mathsf{d}(y,z)>0\end{subarray}}\frac{\left\lvert f(z)-f(y)\right\rvert}{\mathsf{d}(z,y)}\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,\qquad x\in X\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,r>0\,\,\mathrm{.}

The
asymptotic Lipschitz constant

\mathrm{Lip}^{a}_{\mathsf{d}}[f]\colon X\rightarrow[0,\infty]
is defined as

\displaystyle\mathrm{Lip}^{a}_{\mathsf{d}}[f](x)\eqdef\lim_{r\downarrow 0}\mathrm{Lip}^{a}_{\mathsf{d}}[f](x,r)\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,

with the usual convention that
\mathrm{Lip}^{a}_{\mathsf{d}}[f](x)=0
whenever
x
is a
\mathsf{d}
-isolated point in
X
.
We drop the subscript
\mathsf{d}
whenever apparent from context.
Note that
\mathrm{Lip}^{a}[f]
is
\mathsf{d}
-u.s.c.
by construction, thus if
\mathsf{d}
additionally metrizes
\tau
, then
\mathrm{Lip}^{a}[f]
is
\tau
-u.s.c. as well, and therefore it is
\mathscr{B}_{\tau}
-measurable.

Cheeger energies

Each extended metric-topological measure space
(X,\tau,\mathsf{d},\mathsf{m})
is naturally endowed with a convex local energy functional, the l.s.c.
L^{2}(\mathsf{m})
-relaxation of the natural energy on Lipschitz functions, called the
Cheeger energy
of the space; e.g.
[
6
,
37
]
.
Several —
a priori
inequivalent — definitions of Cheeger energy are possible. We collect here three of them, referring to
[
37
]
for additional ones.

Definition 2.19

(
[
6
, Thm. 4.5]
)
.

The
\mathsf{Ch}_{*}
-
Cheeger energy
of
f\in L^{2}(\mathsf{m})
is defined as

\displaystyle\mathsf{Ch}_{*,\mathsf{d},\mathsf{m}}(f)\eqdef\int\left\lvert\mathrm{D}f\right\rvert_{*}^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mathsf{m}\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,\qquad\mathscr{D}({\mathsf{Ch}_{*,\mathsf{d},\mathsf{m}}})\eqdef\left\{f\in L^{2}(\mathsf{m}):\mathsf{Ch}_{*,\mathsf{d},\mathsf{m}}(f)<\infty\right\}\,\,\mathrm{.}

Definition 2.20

(
[
6
, Rmk. 5.12]
)
.

The
\mathsf{Ch}_{w}
-
Cheeger energy
of
f\in L^{2}(\mathsf{m})
is defined as

\displaystyle\mathsf{Ch}_{w,\mathsf{d},\mathsf{m}}(f)\eqdef\int\left\lvert\mathrm{D}f\right\rvert_{w}^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mathsf{m}\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,\qquad\mathscr{D}({\mathsf{Ch}_{w,\mathsf{d},\mathsf{m}}})\eqdef\left\{f\in L^{2}(\mathsf{m}):\mathsf{Ch}_{w,\mathsf{d},\mathsf{m}}(f)<\infty\right\}\,\,\mathrm{.}

Definition 2.21

(
[
4
, Dfn. 6.1]
)
.

The
\mathsf{Ch}_{a}
-
Cheeger energy
of
f\in L^{2}(\mathsf{m})
is defined as

\displaystyle\mathsf{Ch}_{a,\mathsf{d},\mathsf{m}}(f)\eqdef\inf\liminf_{n}\int g_{n}^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mathsf{m}\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,\quad\mathscr{D}({\mathsf{Ch}_{a,\mathsf{d},\mathsf{m}}})\eqdef\left\{f\in L^{2}(\mathsf{m}):\mathsf{Ch}_{a,\mathsf{d},\mathsf{m}}(f)<\infty\right\}\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,

where the infimum is taken over all sequences
\left(f_{n}\right)_{n}\subset\mathrm{Lip}_{b}(\mathsf{d},\tau)
,
L^{2}(\mathsf{m})
-strongly converging to
f
, and all sequences
\left(g_{n}\right)_{n}
of
\mathscr{B}_{\tau}^{\mathsf{m}}
-measurable functions satisfying
g_{n}\geq\mathrm{Lip}^{a}[f_{n}]

\mathsf{m}
-a.e..

In all cases, we shall omit the specification of either
\mathsf{d}
,
\mathsf{m}
or both, whenever not relevant or apparent from context.
We refer the reader to
[
6
, §4]
for a thorough treatment of
\mathsf{Ch}_{*}
, and to
[
4
, §6]
for a thorough treatment of
\mathsf{Ch}_{a}
in the setting of extended metric-topological probability spaces.

In the following, in order to refer to results in the literature, we shall need to make use of all the above definitions on some extended metric-topological
probability
space.
To this end, we first show that they coincide on every such space.

Proposition 2.22
.

Let
(X,\tau,\mathsf{d},\mathsf{m})
be an extended metric-topological
probability
space. Then,

\mathsf{Ch}_{*,\mathsf{d},\mathsf{m}}=\mathsf{Ch}_{a,\mathsf{d},\mathsf{m}}=\mathsf{Ch}_{w,\mathsf{d},\mathsf{m}}\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,

and each of these functionals is densely defined on
L^{2}(\mathsf{m})
.

Proof.

Since
\mathsf{m}X<\infty
, the domain of
\mathsf{Ch}_{*,\mathsf{d},\mathsf{m}}
contains
\mathrm{Lip}_{b}(\mathsf{d},\mathscr{B}_{\tau})
, and the latter is dense in
L^{2}(\mathsf{m})
by e.g.
[
6
, Prop. 4.1]
.
Let us show the identification.
Firstly, note that our Definition
2.21
(i.e.
[
4
, Dfn. 6.1]
) differs from
[
37
, Dfn. 5.1]
— as do our definition of the asymptotic Lipschitz constant (again after
[
4
]
) and the one in
[
37
]
. Thus, we need to show that our definition of
\mathsf{Ch}_{a}
coincides with the one of
\mathsf{CE}_{2}
in
[
37
, Dfn. 5.1]
.
Once this identity is established, the assertion will be a consequence of the identification of both
\mathsf{Ch}_{*}
and
\mathsf{CE}_{2}
with
\mathsf{Ch}_{w}
.

The identification of
\mathsf{CE}_{2}
with
\mathsf{Ch}_{w}
is shown in
[
37
, Thm. 11.7]
for the choice
\mathscr{A}\eqdef\mathrm{Lip}_{b}(\mathsf{d},\tau)
.
The identification of
\mathsf{Ch}_{*}
with
\mathsf{Ch}_{w}
is shown in
[
6
, Thm. 6.2]
. The assumption in Equation (4.2) there is trivially satisfied since
\mathsf{m}X=1
.
Thus, the proof is concluded if we show that

(2.15)

\mathsf{Ch}_{w}\leq\mathsf{Ch}_{a}\leq\mathsf{CE}_{2}\,\,\mathrm{.}

Since
\tau_{\mathsf{d}}
is finer than
\tau
, for each
x\in X
and for each neighborhood
U
of
x
, there exists
r>0
so that
B^{\mathsf{d}}_{r}(x)\subset U
. Thus, for any
\mathscr{B}_{\tau}
-measurable
f\colon X\rightarrow\overline{{\mathbb{R}}}
,

\displaystyle\sup_{x,y\in B^{\mathsf{d}}_{r}(x)}\frac{f(z)-f(y)}{\mathsf{d}(x,y)}\leq\sup_{x,y\in U}\frac{f(z)-f(y)}{\mathsf{d}(x,y)}\,\,\mathrm{.}

As a consequence,
\mathrm{Lip}^{a}_{\mathsf{d}}[f]
is dominated by the asymptotic Lipschitz constant of
f
as defined in
[
37
, Eqn. (2.48)]
, and the second inequality in (
2.15
) follows.

The first inequality in (
2.15
) is a consequence of
[
4
, Prop. 6.3
(b)
and
(g)
]
. Importantly, we note that
[
4
]
denotes by
\left\lvert\mathrm{D}f\right\rvert_{w}
the minimal relaxed slope
\left\lvert\mathrm{D}f\right\rvert_{*}
.
∎

3.
Localization and globalization

Let
(\mathbb{X},\mathcal{E})
be a quasi-regular Dirichlet space, and
E\subset X
be
\mathcal{E}
-quasi-open, with
\mathsf{m}E>0
.
Further set

(3.1)

\mathcal{F}_{E}\eqdef\left\{u\in\mathcal{F}:\tilde{u}\equiv 0\ \;{\mathcal{E}}\text{-q.e.}\text{ on }X\setminus E\right\}\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,\qquad\mathcal{E}_{E}(u,v)\eqdef\mathcal{E}(u,v)\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,\quad u,v\in\mathcal{F}_{E}\,\,\mathrm{.}

Note that
\mathcal{F}_{E}\subset L^{2}(\mathsf{m}_{E})
.
Let us gather here various results proved by K. Kuwae in
[
31
]
.

Proposition 3.1

(Kuwae)
.

Let
(\mathbb{X},\mathcal{E})
be a quasi-regular Dirichlet space, and
E\subset X
be
\mathcal{E}
-quasi-open, with
\mathsf{m}E>0
.
Then,

(1)

[
31
, Lem. 3.4(i)]

\mathcal{F}_{E}
is dense in
L^{2}(m_{E})
and
(\mathcal{E}_{E},\mathcal{F}_{E})
is a Dirichlet form on
L^{2}(m_{E})
;

(2)

[
31
, Lem. 3.4(ii), Lem. 3.5(ii)&(iv)]

A\subset E
is
\mathcal{E}
-polar, resp.
\mathcal{E}
-quasi-open, if and only if it is
\mathcal{E}_{E}
-polar, resp.
\mathcal{E}_{E}
-quasi-open;

(3)

[
31
, Lem. 3.4(ii)]
the restriction to
E
of any
\mathcal{E}
-quasi-continuous function is
\mathcal{E}_{E}
-quasi-continuous;

(4)

[
31
, Lem. 3.4(ii)]

(\mathcal{E}_{E},\mathcal{F}_{E})
is quasi-regular;

(5)

[
31
, Thm. 4.2]

{{\mathcal{F}}^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{loc}}}(E)={{(\mathcal{F}_{E})}^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{loc}}}
. In particular,
\mathcal{F}\big\lvert_{E}\eqdef\left\{f\big\lvert_{E}:f\in\mathcal{F}\right\}\subset{{(\mathcal{F}_{E})}^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{loc}}}
.

We denote by
\mathbb{X}_{E}\eqdef(E,\tau_{E},\Sigma_{E},\mathsf{m}_{E})
the restricted space of
\mathbb{X}
to
E
, defined in the obvious way, and by
(\mathbb{X}_{E},\mathcal{E}_{E})
the corresponding quasi-regular Dirichlet space constructed in Proposition
3.1
1
,
4
.
When
(\mathbb{X}_{E},\mathcal{E}_{E})
is a quasi-regular strongly local Dirichlet space, we denote by
\mu^{E}_{{\,\cdot\,},{\,\cdot\,}}\colon(\mathcal{F}_{E})_{b}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\times 2}}\to\mathfrak{M}^{+}_{b\text{{r}}}(\mathscr{B}_{\tau},\mathscr{N}_{\mathcal{E}_{E}})
its energy measure, and by
\mu^{E}_{{\,\cdot\,}}
the corresponding extension to
{{(\mathcal{F}_{E})}^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{loc}}}
with values in
\mathfrak{M}^{+}_{\sigma}(\mathscr{B}_{\tau},\mathscr{N}_{\mathcal{E}_{E}})
constructed in Proposition
2.7
.

Corollary 3.2

(Kuwae)
.

Let
(\mathbb{X},\mathcal{E})
be a quasi-regular strongly local Dirichlet space, and
E\subset X
be
\mathcal{E}
-quasi-open, with
\mathsf{m}E>0
.
Then,
(\mathbb{X}_{E},\mathcal{E}_{E})
is a quasi-regular strongly local Dirichlet space. Furthermore,

\mu^{E}_{f,g}=\car_{E}\mu_{f,g}\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,\quad f,g\in\mathcal{F}_{E}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\mu^{E}_{f}=\car_{E}\mu_{f}\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,\quad f\in{{(\mathcal{F}_{E})}^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{loc}}}\,\,\mathrm{.}

Proof.

The quasi-regularity of
(\mathbb{X}_{E},\mathcal{E}_{E})
was noted in Proposition
3.1
4
.
By (the proof
1
1

1

Mistakenly marked as ‘Proof of Corollary 6.1’, see
[
31
, p. 702]
.
of)
[
31
, Cor. 5.1]
, the form
(\mathcal{E}_{E},\mathcal{F}_{E})
is as well strongly local, and
\mu^{E}_{f,g}=\car_{E}\mu_{f,g}
for every
f,g\in\mathcal{F}_{E}
.
The last assertion follows by extension to the broad local domain as in Proposition
2.7
4
.
∎

Remark 3.3

(
Caveat
)
.

Let
(\mathbb{X},\mathcal{E})
be a quasi-regular strongly local Dirichlet space, and
E\subset X
be
\mathcal{E}
-quasi-open with
0<\mathsf{m}E<\infty
.
Then,
(\mathcal{E}_{E},\mathcal{F}_{E})
is conservative (and
\car_{E}\in\mathcal{F}_{E}
) if and only if
E
is additionally
\mathcal{E}
-quasi-closed.
Indeed, if
E
is both
\mathcal{E}
-quasi-open and
\mathcal{E}
-quasi-closed, then it is
\mathcal{E}
-invariant, see e.g.
[
25
, Cor. 4.6.3, p. 194]
.
Thus,
\car_{E}\in\mathcal{F}
by
\mathcal{E}
-invariance of
E
, e.g.
[
25
, p. 53]
, and thus
\car_{E}\in\mathcal{F}_{E}
by definition of
\mathcal{F}_{E}
.
If otherwise,
E
is not
\mathcal{E}
-quasi-closed, then it is not
\mathcal{E}
-invariant, hence
\car_{E}\not\in\mathcal{F}\supset\mathcal{F}_{E}
.

Corollary 3.4
.

Let
(\mathbb{X},\mathcal{E})
be a quasi-regular strongly local Dirichlet space,
E\subset X
be
\mathcal{E}
-quasi-open, with
\mathsf{m}E>0
, and
\mu\in\mathfrak{M}^{+}_{\sigma}(\mathscr{B}_{\tau},\mathscr{N}_{\mathcal{E}})
.
Then, with obvious meaning of the notation,

\displaystyle\mathbb{L}^{\mu}_{\mathrm{loc}}(E)=\mathbb{L}^{\!\!E\ \mu_{E}}_{\mathrm{loc}}\eqdef\left\{f\in{{(\mathcal{F}_{E})}^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{loc}}}:\mu^{E}_{f}\leq\mu_{E}\right\}\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,

\displaystyle\mathbb{L}^{\mu}_{\mathrm{loc},b}(E)=\mathbb{L}^{\!\!E\ \mu_{E}}_{\mathrm{loc},b}\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,\qquad\mathbb{L}^{\mu,\tau}_{\mathrm{loc},b}(E)=\ \mathbb{L}^{\!\!E\ \mu_{E},\tau}_{\mathrm{loc},b}\,\,\mathrm{.}

Further assume that
\mu
is additionally absolutely
\mathcal{E}
-moderate, and that there exists
e_{E}\in\mathcal{F}_{b}
with
0\leq\tilde{e}_{E}\leq 1

\mathcal{E}
-q.e. on
E
.
Then,

(3.2)

\mathbb{L}^{\mu}_{\mathrm{loc},b}\big\lvert_{E}\eqdef\left\{f\big\lvert_{E}:f\in\mathbb{L}^{\mu}_{\mathrm{loc},b}\right\}\subset\mathbb{L}^{\!\!E\ \mu_{E}}_{\mathrm{loc}}\,\,\mathrm{.}

Proof.

We only show the first equality, all others being trivial consequences of the first one.
Firstly, note that
\mu_{E}\in\mathfrak{M}^{+}_{\sigma}(\mathscr{B}_{\tau_{E}},\mathscr{N}_{\mathcal{E}_{E}})
as a consequence of Proposition
3.1
2
.
Thus, the statement is well-posed.
Let
f\in\mathbb{L}^{\mu}_{\mathrm{loc}}(E)
.
Then,
\mu^{E}_{f}=\car_{E}\mu_{f}\leq\car_{E}\mu\eqqcolon\mu_{E}
, where the first equality is shown in Corollary
3.2
and the inequality holds by definition of
\mathbb{L}^{\mu}_{\mathrm{loc}}(E)
.
This concludes the first assertion in light of Proposition
3.1
5
.

We show the second assertion. Let
(G_{\bullet},e_{\bullet})\in\mathscr{G}_{0}
be
\mu
-moderated, and set
G_{k}^{\prime}\eqdef G_{k}\cup E
for every
k\in{\mathbb{N}}
.
Since
e_{E}\equiv 1

\mathsf{m}
-a.e. on
E
, the function
e_{k}\vee e_{E}
satisfies
e_{k}\vee e_{E}\in\mathcal{F}_{b}
and
e_{k}\vee e_{E}\equiv 1

\mathsf{m}
-a.e. on
G_{k}^{\prime}
.
Thus,
G_{\bullet}^{\prime}\in\mathscr{G}_{0}
.
Now, let
(G^{f}_{\bullet},f_{\bullet})
with
G^{f}_{\bullet}\in\mathscr{G}_{0}
be witnessing that
f\in\mathbb{L}^{\mu}_{\mathrm{loc},b}
.
Since
\mu
is absolutely
\mathcal{E}
-moderate, both
G^{f}_{\bullet}
and
G^{\prime}_{\bullet}
are
\mu
-moderated.
Thus,
f\in\mathbb{L}^{\mu}_{\mathrm{loc},b}(G^{f}_{\bullet})=\mathbb{L}^{\mu}_{\mathrm{loc},b}(G^{\prime}_{\bullet})
by Lemma
2.9
.
By definition of the latter space, there exists
f^{E}\in\mathcal{F}
with
f^{E}\equiv f

\mathsf{m}
-a.e. on
E
and
\mu_{f}\leq\mu
.
By Proposition
3.1
5
we conclude that
f^{E}\big\lvert_{E}\in{{(\mathcal{F}_{E})}^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{loc}}}
.
In fact,
f^{E}\big\lvert_{E}\in\mathbb{L}^{\!\!E\ \mu_{E}}_{\mathrm{loc},b}
by locality of
\mu_{f^{E}}
as in the first assertion, which concludes the second assertion and the proof.
∎

Remark 3.5
.

(1)

In general,
\mathbb{L}^{\!\!E\ \mu_{E},\tau}_{\mathrm{loc}}\not\subset\mathbb{L}^{\mu,\tau}_{\mathrm{loc},b}\big\lvert_{E}
.
Indeed, let
\mathcal{E}
be the
0
-form on
L^{2}(\mathsf{m})
. Then,
\mathbb{L}^{\!\!E\ \mu_{E},\tau}_{\mathrm{loc}}=\mathcal{C}_{b}(\tau_{E})\not\subset\mathcal{C}_{b}(\tau)\big\lvert_{E}=\mathbb{L}^{\mu,\tau}_{\mathrm{loc}}\big\lvert_{E}
, unless
E
is additionally
\tau
-closed.

(2)

In general,
\mathbb{L}^{\mu}_{b}\big\lvert_{E}\not\subset\mathbb{L}^{\!\!E\ \mu_{E}}
, since
\car\in\mathbb{L}^{\mu}\big\lvert_{E}
yet
\car_{E}\notin\mathcal{F}\supset\mathcal{F}_{E}
, unless
E
is additionally
\mathcal{E}
-quasi-closed.

Let us state here the following localization property for
(\mathsf{Rad}_{\mathsf{d},\mu})
.

Proposition 3.6

(Localization of the Rademacher property)
.

Let
(\mathbb{X},\mathcal{E})
be a quasi-regular strongly local Dirichlet space with
\mathbb{X}
satisfying
2
,
\mu\in\mathfrak{M}^{+}_{\sigma}(\mathscr{B}_{\tau},\mathscr{N}_{\mathcal{E}})
be absolutely
\mathcal{E}
-moderate, and
\mathsf{d}\colon X^{\scriptscriptstyle{\times 2}}\to[0,\infty]
be an extended pseudo-distance on
X
.
Further assume that

(1)

\tau_{\mathsf{d}}
is separable;

(2)

\mathsf{d}({\,\cdot\,},x_{0})\colon X\to[0,\infty]
is
\Sigma
-measurable for every
x_{0}\in X
.

If
(\mathbb{X},\mathcal{E},\mathsf{d},\mu)
satisfies
(\mathsf{Rad}_{\mathsf{d},\mu})
, then
(\mathbb{X}_{E},\mathcal{E}_{E},\mathsf{d},\mu_{E})
satisfies
(\mathsf{Rad}_{\mathsf{d},\mu_{E}})
for every
\mathcal{E}
-quasi-open
E\subset X
with
\mathsf{m}E>0
.

We need two preparatory results.
The first one is a version of McShane’s Extension Theorem for Lipschitz functions.
We note that our proof of the following statement in
[
18
, Lem. 2.1]
contains a gap, which we fill in the proof below.

Lemma 3.7

(Constrained McShane extensions,
[
18
, Lem. 2.1]
)
.

Let
(X,\mathsf{d})
be an extended metric space. Fix
A\subset X
,
A\neq\emp
, and let
\hat{f}\colon A\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}
be a function in
\mathrm{Lip}_{b}(A,\mathsf{d})
. Further set

(3.3)

\displaystyle\overline{f}\colon x

\displaystyle\longmapsto\sup_{A}\hat{f}\wedge\inf_{a\in A}\big({\hat{f}(a)+\mathrm{L}_{\mathsf{d}}(\hat{f})\,\mathsf{d}(x,a)}\big)\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,

\displaystyle 0\cdot\infty

\displaystyle\eqdef\infty\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,

\displaystyle\underline{f}\colon x

\displaystyle\longmapsto\inf_{A}\hat{f}\vee\sup_{a\in A}\big({\hat{f}(a)-\mathrm{L}_{\mathsf{d}}(\hat{f})\,\mathsf{d}(x,a)}\big)\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,

\displaystyle 0\cdot\infty

\displaystyle\eqdef\infty\,\,\mathrm{.}

Then,

(1)

\underline{f}=\hat{f}=\overline{f}
on
A
and
\inf_{A}\hat{f}\leq\underline{f}\leq\overline{f}\leq\sup_{A}\hat{f}
on
X
;

(2)

\underline{f}
,
\overline{f}\in\mathrm{Lip}_{b}(\mathsf{d})
with
\mathrm{L}_{\mathsf{d}}(\underline{f})=\mathrm{L}_{\mathsf{d}}(\overline{f})=\mathrm{L}_{\mathsf{d}}(\hat{f})
;

(3)

\underline{f}
, resp.
\overline{f}
, is the minimal, resp. maximal, function satisfying
1
-
2
, that is, for every
\hat{g}\in\mathrm{Lip}_{b}(\mathsf{d})
with
\inf_{A}\hat{f}\leq\hat{g}\leq\sup_{A}\hat{f}
,
\hat{g}\big\lvert_{A}=\hat{f}
on
A
and
\mathrm{L}_{\mathsf{d}}(\hat{g})\leq\mathrm{L}_{\mathsf{d}}(\hat{f})
, it holds that
\underline{f}\leq\hat{g}\leq\overline{f}
.

Proof.

It is erroneously claimed in
[
18
]
that, if
\hat{f}
is non-constant, one can assume that
\mathrm{L}_{\mathsf{d}}(\hat{f})>0
with no loss of generality, cf. Remark
2.2
.
Since this does not hold, we treat separately the case when
\mathrm{L}_{\mathsf{d}}(\hat{f})=0
.
In this case, partition
A
into its (pairwise disjoint)
\mathsf{d}
-accessible components
\left(A_{i}\right)_{i\in I}
.
Since
\mathrm{L}_{\mathsf{d}}(\hat{f})=0
, then
\hat{f}\big\lvert_{A_{i}}\equiv a_{i}
is constant for every
i
.
Let
X_{i}
be the unique
\mathsf{d}
-accessible component of
X
containing
A_{i}
.
Set
\underline{f}(x)\eqdef a_{i}
if
x\in X_{i}
and
\underline{f}(x)\eqdef\inf_{A}\hat{f}
otherwise, and similarly
\overline{f}(x)\eqdef a_{i}
if
x\in X_{i}
and
\overline{f}(x)\eqdef\sup_{A}\hat{f}
otherwise.
Then,
\underline{f}
and
\overline{f}
satisfy the required properties.

The rest of the proof is as in
[
18
]
.
∎

Corollary 3.8
.

Let
(X,\mathsf{d})
be a separable extended pseudo-metric space. Further let
\Sigma
be a
\sigma
-algebra on
X
and assume that
\mathsf{d}({\,\cdot\,},x_{0})\colon X\to[0,\infty]
is
\Sigma
-measurable for every
x_{0}\in X
.
Then,
\mathrm{Lip}(\mathsf{d})=\mathrm{Lip}(\mathsf{d},\Sigma)
.

Proof.

By a standard truncation argument, it suffices to show that every
\hat{f}\in\mathrm{Lip}_{b}(\mathsf{d})
is
\Sigma
-measurable.
Choosing
A=X
in Lemma
3.7
we have that
\hat{f}=\overline{f}
, thus it suffices to show that
\overline{f}
is measurable.
Since
(X,\mathsf{d})
is separable, there exists a countable
\mathsf{d}
-dense set
D\subset X
. Since
(x,a)\mapsto\big({\hat{f}(a)+\mathrm{L}_{\mathsf{d}}(\hat{f})\,\mathsf{d}(x,a)}\big)
is jointly
\mathsf{d}
