-continuous,

\inf_{a\in X}\big({\hat{f}(a)+\mathrm{L}_{\mathsf{d}}(\hat{f})\,\mathsf{d}(x,a)}\big)=\inf_{a\in D}\big({\hat{f}(a)+\mathrm{L}_{\mathsf{d}}(\hat{f})\,\mathsf{d}(x,a)}\big)\,\,\mathrm{.}

Since the infimum of a countable family of
\Sigma
-measurable functions is
\Sigma
-measurable, the conclusion follows since
x\mapsto\big({\hat{f}(a)+\mathrm{L}_{\mathsf{d}}(\hat{f})\,\mathsf{d}(x,a)}\big)
is
\Sigma
-measurable for every
a\in X
by assumption.
∎

Proof of Proposition
3.6
.

Let
\hat{f}\in\mathrm{Lip}^{1}_{b}(E,\mathsf{d},\Sigma_{E})
.
By Lemma
3.7
2
and Corollary
3.8
, the upper constrained McShane extension
\overline{f}\colon X\to{\mathbb{R}}
of
\hat{f}\colon E\to{\mathbb{R}}
defined in (
3.3
) satisfies
\overline{f}\in\mathrm{Lip}^{1}_{b}(\mathsf{d},\Sigma)
.
By
(\mathsf{Rad}_{\mathsf{d},\mu})
we conclude that
\big[\overline{f}\big]_{\mathsf{m}}\in\mathbb{L}^{\mu}_{\mathrm{loc},b}
.
Thus,
\big[\overline{f}\big\lvert_{E}\big]_{\mathsf{m}_{E}}\in\mathbb{L}^{\!\!E\ \mu_{E}}_{\mathrm{loc},b}
by (
3.2
) in Corollary
3.4
.
This concludes the assertion since
\overline{f}=\hat{f}
everywhere on
E
by Lemma
3.7
1
.
∎

Remark 3.9
.

(1)

In Proposition
3.6
, assumption
1
is usually stronger than
2
. For instance, if
\mathsf{d}_{\mu}
satisfies the assumption in Proposition
3.6
1
, then it satisfies as well the assumption in Proposition
3.6
2
by
[
18
, Thm. 3.13]
.

(2)

It is essential for the validity of the above proposition that the Rademacher property be phrased with the broad
local
space
\mathbb{L}^{\!\!E\ \mu_{E}}_{\mathrm{loc}}
(as opposed to
\mathbb{L}^{\!\!E\ \mu_{E}}
).
Indeed,
\car_{E}\in\mathrm{Lip}_{b}(E,\mathsf{d},\Sigma_{E})
, yet in general
\car_{E}\notin\mathcal{F}_{E}
as in Remark
3.3
.

The statement analogous to Proposition
3.6
with, e.g.,
(\mathsf{SL}_{\mu,\mathsf{d}})
in place of
(\mathsf{Rad}_{\mathsf{d},\mu})
does
not
hold.
This is a consequence of the failure of the converse inclusion in (
3.2
).
In the next example we discuss a quasi-regular strongly local Dirichlet space
(\mathbb{X},\mathcal{E})
satisfying
(\mathsf{SL}_{\mu,\mathsf{d}})
for which there exists a
\tau
-open set
E
with
\mathsf{m}E=\mathsf{m}X
and such that
(\mathbb{X}_{E},\mathcal{E}_{E},\mathsf{d},\mu_{E})
does not satisfy
(\mathsf{SL}_{\mu_{E},\mathsf{d}})
.

Example 3.10
.

Let
\mathbb{X}
be standard interval
[-1,1]
, and
\mathsf{d}
be the standard Euclidean distance on
X
.
Set
E\eqdef[-1,1]\setminus\left\{0\right\}
, and let
\mu\eqdef\mathsf{m}={\mathrm{Leb}}^{1}
be the standard Lebesgue measure on
X
.
Further let
(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
be the regular Dirichlet form properly associated with the Brownian motion on
X
with reflecting boundary conditions.
Now, let
\hat{f}\colon E\to{\mathbb{R}}
be defined by
\hat{f}\eqdef\car_{(0,1]}-\car_{[-1,0)}
.
Setting
G_{k}^{E}\eqdef[-1,1]\setminus[-\tfrac{1}{k},\tfrac{1}{k}]
and
f^{E}_{k}(x)\eqdef-1\vee kx\wedge 1
, it is straightforwardly verified that
(G^{E}_{\bullet},f^{E}_{\bullet})
witnesses that
f\in\mathbb{L}^{\!\!E\ \mu_{E}}_{\mathrm{loc},b}
.
On the other hand,
f\notin\mathbb{L}^{\mu}_{\mathrm{loc},b}
.
Indeed, argue by contradiction that there exists
(G_{\bullet},f_{\bullet})
, with
G_{\bullet}\in\mathscr{G}_{0}
, witnessing that
f\in\mathbb{L}^{\mu}_{\mathrm{loc},b}
.
Since
\left\{0\right\}
is
not

\mathcal{E}
-polar, there exists
G_{k}\in G_{\bullet}
and
f_{k}\in\mathcal{F}
such that
f\equiv f_{k}

\mathsf{m}
-a.e. on
G_{k}
and
G_{k}\ni\left\{0\right\}
.
This is a contradiction since every
h\in\mathcal{F}
has a
\tau
-continuous
\mathsf{m}
-representative, yet
f
has no
\mathsf{m}
-representative continuous at
0
.

Remark 3.11
.

Again specularly to the case of the Rademacher property discussed in Remark
3.9
, if we had phrased, e.g.,
(\mathsf{SL}_{\mu,\mathsf{d}})
with
\mathbb{L}^{\mu}_{b}
in place of
\mathbb{L}^{\mu}_{\mathrm{loc},b}
, then the localization of
(\mathsf{SL}_{\mu,\mathsf{d}})
to
E
would be immediate, since
\mathbb{L}^{\!\!E\ \mu_{E}}_{b}\subset\mathbb{L}^{\mu}_{b}
because
\mathcal{F}_{E}\subset\mathcal{F}
.

Example
3.10
shows that the lack of a
Zygmund Lemma
— ‘
W^{1,\infty}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Lip}
’ — for
E
is an obstruction to the localization to
E
of the Sobolev-to-Lipschitz property on
X
.
In fact, the situation for
(\mathsf{SL}_{\mu,\mathsf{d}})
is opposite to that for
(\mathsf{Rad}_{\mathsf{d},\mu})
, in the sense that one should rather expect
globalization
(as opposed to:
localization
), which takes the following form.

Proposition 3.12

(Globalization of Sobolev–to–Lipschitz-type properties)
.

Let
(\mathbb{X},\mathcal{E})
be a quasi-regular strongly local Dirichlet space with
\mathbb{X}
satisfying
2
,
\mu\in\mathfrak{M}^{+}_{\sigma}(\mathscr{B}_{\tau},\mathscr{N}_{\mathcal{E}})
be absolutely
\mathcal{E}
-moderate, and
\mathsf{d}\colon X^{\scriptscriptstyle{\times 2}}\to[0,\infty]
be an extended distance on
X
.
Further assume that

(1)

\tau_{\mathsf{d}}=\tau
;

(2)

(X,\mathsf{d})
is a (possibly: extended) length space;

(3)

there exists a
\tau
-open covering
\mathscr{E}
of
X
, with the following properties:

(a)

\mathsf{m}E>0
for each
E\in\mathscr{E}
;

(b)

\mathscr{E}
is
\mathcal{E}
-moderate
, i.e. for each
E\in\mathscr{E}
there exists
e_{E}\in\mathcal{F}
with
0\leq e_{E}\leq 1

\mathsf{m}
-a.e. on
E
, cf.
[
18
, Dfn. 2.19]
;

(c)

(\mathsf{SL}_{\mu_{E},\mathsf{d}})
, resp.
(\mathsf{cSL}_{\tau_{E},\mu_{E},\mathsf{d}})
, holds for
(\mathbb{X}_{E},\mathcal{E}_{E},\mathsf{d},\mu_{E})
for every
E\in\mathscr{E}
.

Then,
(\mathsf{SL}_{\mu,\mathsf{d}})
, resp.
(\mathsf{cSL}_{\tau,\mu,\mathsf{d}})
, holds for
(\mathbb{X},\mathcal{E},\mathsf{d},\mu)
.

Proof.

Arguing as in the proof of
[
18
, Lem. 3.23]
with
\mathsf{d}
in place of
\mathsf{d}_{\mu}
, we may restrict ourselves to the case when
\mathsf{d}
is everywhere finite.

We start with the case of
(\mathsf{SL}_{\mu,\mathsf{d}})
.
Let
f\in\mathbb{L}^{\mu}_{\mathrm{loc},b}
. Since
\mathscr{E}
is
\mathcal{E}
-moderate and
\mu
is absolutely
\mathcal{E}
-moderate, (
3.2
) in Corollary
3.4
holds, and it implies that
f\big\lvert_{E}\in\mathbb{L}^{\!\!E\ \mu_{E}}_{\mathrm{loc},b}
for every
E\in\mathscr{E}
.
We may now apply
(\mathsf{SL}_{\mu_{E},\mathsf{d}})
to conclude that for every
E\in\mathscr{E}
there exists
\hat{f}^{E}\in\mathrm{Lip}^{1}_{b}(E,\mathsf{d},\Sigma_{E})
with
\big[\hat{f}^{E}\big]_{\mathsf{m}_{E}}\equiv f\big\lvert_{E}

\mathsf{m}_{E}
-a.e..
As a consequence of
1
,
\mathrm{Lip}^{1}_{b}(E,\mathsf{d},\Sigma_{E})=\mathrm{Lip}^{1}_{b}(E,\mathsf{d},\tau_{E})
, thus we may omit the notation for representatives, and simply write
f^{E}
in place of both
\hat{f}^{E}
and
\big[\hat{f}^{E}\big]_{\mathsf{m}}
.
Now, since
f^{E_{1}}\equiv f\equiv f^{E_{2}}

\mathsf{m}
-a.e. on
E_{1}\cap E_{2}
for every
E_{1},E_{2}\in\mathscr{E}
, and since
\mathsf{m}
has full
\tau
-support by
2
, it follows from the
\tau_{E_{i}}
-continuity of
f^{E_{i}}
that
f^{E_{1}}\equiv f^{E_{2}}
everywhere on
E_{1}\cap E_{2}
.
Therefore, since
\mathcal{C}(\tau)
is a sheaf on
(X,\tau)
, there exists a unique
\hat{f}\in\mathcal{C}(\tau)
with
\hat{f}\big\lvert_{E}\equiv f^{E}
for every
E\in\mathscr{E}
.
Since
\big[\hat{f}\big]_{\mathsf{m}}\equiv f^{E}\equiv f

\mathsf{m}
-a.e. on
E
for each
E
in the covering
\mathscr{E}
of
X
, we conclude that
\hat{f}
is a (in fact:
the
unique)
\tau
-continuous
\mathsf{m}
-representative of
f
.
As customary, we may therefore drop the distinction between
\hat{f}
and
f
.
It remains to show that
f\in\mathrm{Lip}^{1}_{b}(\mathsf{d})
.
Since
(X,\mathsf{d})
is a length space, it is
1
-quasi-convex (see e.g.
[
14
, Dfn. 2.5.4]
), and the fact that
f\big\lvert_{E}\in\mathrm{Lip}^{1}_{b}(E,\mathsf{d})
for every
E
in the
\tau_{\mathsf{d}}
-open covering
\mathscr{E}
implies that
f\in\mathrm{Lip}^{1}_{b}(\mathsf{d})
, see e.g.
[
14
, Thm. 2.5.6]
, which concludes the proof.

For the case of
(\mathsf{cSL}_{\tau,\mu,\mathsf{d}})
it suffices to note that (
3.2
) still holds when the broad local spaces of functions with bounded energy are replaced by their additionally continuous counterparts, since the restriction to
E
of any
\tau
-continuous function is
\tau_{E}
-continuous.
∎

Remark 3.13
.

One relevant case for the application of Proposition
3.12
is when
\mathsf{d}=\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}}
, in which case the assumption in Proposition
3.12
1
amounts to the
strict locality
of the Dirichlet space
(\mathbb{X},\mathcal{E})
in the sense of
[
18
, Dfn. 3.19]
.
In this case, the assumption in Proposition
3.12
2
holds as soon as
(X,\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}})
is locally complete, see
[
18
, Thm. 3.24]
.

Remark 3.14
.

When
\mathsf{d}=\mathsf{d}_{\mu}
, it would not be difficult to show that Proposition
3.12
(even without the assumption in
2
) holds as well when, e.g.,
(\mathsf{cSL}_{\tau,\mu,\mathsf{d}_{\mu}})
is replaced by
(\mathsf{Rad}_{\mathsf{d}_{\mu},\mu})
and
(\mathsf{cSL}_{\tau_{E},\mu_{E},\mathsf{d}_{\mu}})
is replaced by
(\mathsf{Rad}_{\mathsf{d}_{\mu},\mu_{E}})
.
However, this new statement should not be interpreted as a globalization result for the Rademacher property.
Rather, since
\mathbb{X}
satisfies
2
, the topology
\tau=\tau_{\mathsf{d}_{\mu}}
is separable.
Thus,
(\mathsf{Rad}_{\mathsf{d}_{\mu},\mu})
is simply
verified
for
(\mathbb{X},\mathcal{E},\mathsf{d}_{\mu},\mu)
—that is:
independently
of the Rademacher property for
(\mathbb{X}_{E},\mathcal{E}_{E},\mathsf{d}_{\mu},\mu_{E})
— by virtue of
[
18
, Cor. 3.15]
.

4.
Weighted spaces

In this section, let
\mathbb{X}
and
\mathbb{X}^{\prime}
be structures with same underlying topological measurable space
(X,\tau,\Sigma)=(X^{\prime},\tau^{\prime},\Sigma^{\prime})
and different measures
\mathsf{m}
and
\mathsf{m}^{\prime}
with
\mathsf{m}\sim\mathsf{m}^{\prime}
.
Further let
\mathsf{d}\colon X^{\scriptscriptstyle{\times 2}}\to[0,\infty]
be an extended distance.

Now, let
(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
, resp.
(\mathcal{E}^{\prime},\mathcal{F}^{\prime})
, be a quasi-regular strongly local Dirichlet space with underlying space
\mathbb{X}
, resp.
\mathbb{X}^{\prime}
, defined on
L^{2}(\mathsf{m})
, resp.
L^{2}(\mathsf{m}^{\prime})
, and admitting carré du champ operator
\Gamma
, resp.
\Gamma^{\prime}
.
We write

\big({\Gamma,\mathscr{A}}\big)\leq\big({\Gamma^{\prime},\mathscr{A}^{\prime}}\big)

to indicate that
\mathscr{A}\supset\mathscr{A}^{\prime}
and
\Gamma^{\prime}\geq\Gamma
on
\mathscr{A}^{\prime}
, and analogously for the opposite inequality.

4.1.
Rademacher and Sobolev-to-Lipschitz properties for weighted spaces

Let
(\mathsf{P})
denote either
(\mathsf{Rad}_{\mathsf{d},\mathsf{m}})
,
(\mathsf{ScL}_{\mathsf{m},\tau,\mathsf{d}})
,
(\mathsf{SL}_{\mathsf{m},\mathsf{d}})
, or
(\mathsf{cSL}_{\tau,\mathsf{m},\mathsf{d}})
.
Note that
\mathrm{Lip}^{1}(\mathsf{d},\Sigma)
and
\mathcal{C}_{b}(\tau)
do not depend on
(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
nor on
\mathsf{m}
.
Furthermore, since
\mathsf{m}\sim\mathsf{m}^{\prime}
, we have that
L^{\infty}(\mathsf{m})=L^{\infty}(\mathsf{m}^{\prime})
as Banach spaces.
As a consequence of the facts above,
(\mathsf{P})
is a
local
property in the sense of the following Proposition, a proof of which is straightforward.

Proposition 4.1

(Weighted spaces)
.

Retain the notation above. Then,

(1)

if
\big({\Gamma,\mathbb{L}^{\mathsf{m}}_{\mathrm{loc},b}}\big)\leq\big({\Gamma^{\prime},\mathbb{L}^{\prime\,\mathsf{m}^{\prime}}_{\mathrm{loc},b}}\big)
and
(\mathsf{ScL}_{\mathsf{m},\tau,\mathsf{d}})
, resp.
(\mathsf{SL}_{\mathsf{m},\mathsf{d}})
,
(\mathsf{cSL}_{\tau,\mathsf{m},\mathsf{d}})
holds,
then
(\mathsf{ScL}_{\mathsf{m}^{\prime},\tau,\mathsf{d}})
, resp.
(\mathsf{SL}_{\mathsf{m}^{\prime},\mathsf{d}})
,
(\mathsf{cSL}_{\tau,\mathsf{m}^{\prime},\mathsf{d}})
holds as well;

(2)

if
\big({\Gamma,\mathbb{L}^{\mathsf{m}}_{\mathrm{loc},b}}\big)\geq\big({\Gamma^{\prime},\mathbb{L}^{\prime\,\mathsf{m}^{\prime}}_{\mathrm{loc},b}}\big)
and
(\mathsf{Rad}_{\mathsf{d},\mathsf{m}})
holds, then
(\mathsf{Rad}_{\mathsf{d},\mathsf{m}^{\prime}})
holds as well.

Let us now show that it suffices to verify the assumptions in Proposition
4.1
only on a common pseudo-core (i.e. a linear subspace dense in both domains).
In the statement of the next result, let
\mathscr{G}_{0}\eqdef\mathscr{G}_{0}^{\mathcal{E}}
and
\mathscr{G}_{0}^{\prime}\eqdef\mathscr{G}_{0}^{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}
be defined as in (
2.5
).

Proposition 4.2

(Comparison of square fields)
.

Let
(\mathbb{X},\mathcal{E})
and
(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathcal{E}^{\prime})
be quasi-regular strongly local Dirichlet spaces with same underlying topological measurable space
(X,\tau,\Sigma)=(X^{\prime},\tau^{\prime},\Sigma^{\prime})
and possibly different measures
\mathsf{m}
and
\mathsf{m}^{\prime}
, both satisfying
2
.
Further assume that:

(1)

\mathsf{m}^{\prime}=\theta\mathsf{m}
for some
\theta\in L^{0}(\mathsf{m})
, and there exists
E_{\bullet},G_{\bullet}\in\mathscr{G}_{0}\cap\mathscr{G}_{0}^{\prime}
with the following properties:

(a)

for each
k\in{\mathbb{N}}
there exists a constant
a_{k}
such that
\theta\geq a_{k}>0

\mathsf{m}
-a.e. on
G_{k}
;
(that is,
\theta^{-1}\in{{\big({L^{\infty}(\mathsf{m})}\big)}^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{loc}}}(G_{\bullet})
.)

(b)

for each
k\in{\mathbb{N}}
it holds
E_{k}\subset G_{k}

\mathcal{E}
- and
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
-quasi-everywhere, and there exists
\varrho_{k}\in\mathcal{F}
with
\car_{E_{k}}\leq\varrho_{k}\leq\car_{G_{k}}

\mathsf{m}
-a.e., and
\Gamma(\varrho_{k})\in L^{1}(\mathsf{m}^{\prime}_{G_{k}})
;

(2)

there exists
\mathcal{D}
a pseudo-core for both
(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
on
L^{2}(\mathsf{m})
and
(\mathcal{E}^{\prime},\mathcal{F}^{\prime})
on
L^{2}(\mathsf{m}^{\prime})
, additionally so that
\Gamma\leq\Gamma^{\prime}
on
\mathcal{D}
.

Then,
\big({\Gamma,\mathbb{L}^{\mathsf{m}}_{\mathrm{loc},b}}\big)\leq\big({\Gamma^{\prime},\mathbb{L}^{\prime\,\mathsf{m}^{\prime}}_{\mathrm{loc},b}}\big)
and
\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}}\geq\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}^{\prime}}
.

Remark 4.3
.

(1)

Assumption
1b
in Proposition
4.2
is implied by the following:

(a_{2}^{\prime})

for each
k\in{\mathbb{N}}
it holds
E_{k}\subset G_{k}

\mathcal{E}
- and
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
-quasi-everywhere and there exists
\varrho_{k}\in\mathcal{D}
with
\car_{E_{k}}\leq\varrho_{k}\leq\car_{G_{k}}

\mathsf{m}
-a.e.;

indeed, in this case
\Gamma(\varrho_{k})\leq\Gamma^{\prime}(\varrho_{k})\in L^{1}(\mathsf{m}^{\prime})
by assumption
2
in Proposition
4.2
and since
\mathcal{D}\subset\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
, and we may thus choose
C_{k}\eqdef\Gamma^{\prime}(\varrho_{k})
.
Depending on the choice of
\mathcal{D}
, assumption
(a_{2}^{\prime})
is possibly more easily verified; in particular, it is immediately satisfied if
\mathcal{D}=\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
.

(2)

The existence of the cut-off functions in Proposition
4.2
1b
is a quite mild assumption.
For instance, let
\mathsf{d}\colon X^{\scriptscriptstyle{\times 2}}\to[0,\infty)
be a distance metrizing
\tau
and assume that
(\mathbb{X},\mathcal{E})
satisfies
(\mathsf{Rad}_{\mathsf{d},\mathsf{m}})
.
We say that
A_{1}
,
A_{2}\subset X
are
\tau
-
well-separated
if
\mathrm{cl}_{\tau}A_{1}\cap\mathrm{cl}_{\tau}A_{2}=\emp
.
Then it is readily verified that, for
every
pair of sets
E,G
with

•

E\subset G
, and
G
(hence
E
)
\mathsf{d}
-bounded and of finite
\mathsf{m}^{\prime}
-measure,

•

E
and
G^{\mathrm{c}}

\tau
-well-separated,

the function
\varrho_{E,G}\eqdef 0\vee\big({1-\mathsf{d}(E,G^{\mathrm{c}})^{-1}\mathsf{d}({\,\cdot\,},E)}\big)
satisfies

\car_{\mathrm{cl}_{\tau}E}\leq\varrho_{k}\leq\car_{\mathrm{cl}_{\tau}G}\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,\qquad\Gamma(\varrho_{E,G})\leq\mathsf{d}(E,G^{\mathrm{c}})^{-1}\car_{\mathrm{cl}_{\tau}G}\in L^{1}(\mathsf{m}^{\prime})\,\,\mathrm{.}

Proof of Proposition
4.2
.

Let
\varrho_{k}
be given by
1b
.
Since
G_{\bullet}\in\mathscr{G}_{0}
(resp.
G_{\bullet}\in\mathscr{G}_{0}^{\prime}
), we have in particular that
\mathsf{m}G_{k}<\infty
(resp.
\mathsf{m}^{\prime}G_{k}<\infty
) for every
k\in{\mathbb{N}}
.
Thus,
\varrho_{k}\in L^{p}(\mathsf{m})\cap L^{p}(\mathsf{m}^{\prime})
for every every
p\in[1,\infty]
by interpolation, for every
k\in{\mathbb{N}}
.

Now, let
(G^{\prime}_{\bullet},f_{\bullet})
, with
G^{\prime}_{\bullet}\in\mathscr{G}_{0}^{\prime}
, be witnessing that
f\in\mathbb{L}^{\prime\,\mathsf{m}^{\prime}}_{\mathrm{loc},b}
.
With no loss of generality, by truncation, we may assume that
\sup_{n}\left\lVert f_{n}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathsf{m})}\leq M\eqdef\left\lVert f\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathsf{m})}<\infty
.
In light of Lemma
2.9
and since
G_{\bullet}\in\mathscr{G}_{0}\cap\mathscr{G}_{0}^{\prime}
, we may assume with no loss of generality that
G^{\prime}_{\bullet}=G_{\bullet}
.
For each
k\in{\mathbb{N}}
, since
f_{k}\in\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
, there exists
\left(f^{\scriptscriptstyle{(n)}}_{k}\right)_{n}\subset\mathcal{D}\subset\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
a sequence of functions
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
-converging to
f_{k}
.
With no loss of generality, up to replacing
\left(f^{\scriptscriptstyle{(n)}}_{k}\right)_{n}
with a non-relabeled subsequence, we may further assume that
\lim_{n}f^{\scriptscriptstyle{(n)}}_{k}=f_{k}

\mathsf{m}^{\prime}
- (hence
\mathsf{m}
-)a.e..
By a standard truncation argument, by the Markov property and by the closability of
(\mathcal{E}^{\prime},\mathcal{F}^{\prime})
, we may finally also assume that
\left\lvert f^{\scriptscriptstyle{(n)}}_{k}\right\rvert\leq\left\lvert f_{k}\right\rvert\leq M

\mathsf{m}^{\prime}
-a.e. for every
k,n\in{\mathbb{N}}
.
As a consequence, by Dominated Convergence with dominating function
M\car_{G_{k}}\in L^{2}(\mathsf{m}^{\prime})
we have in particular that

(4.1)

L^{2}(\mathsf{m}^{\prime})\text{-}\lim_{n}f^{\scriptscriptstyle{(n)}}_{k}\varrho_{k}=f\varrho_{k}\,\,\mathrm{.}

Furthermore, by the Leibniz rule for
\Gamma
and by the assumption, we have that
\left(f^{\scriptscriptstyle{(n)}}_{k}\varrho_{k}\right)_{n}\subset\mathcal{F}_{b}
satisfies

\displaystyle\Gamma(f^{\scriptscriptstyle{(n)}}_{k}\varrho_{k}-f^{\scriptscriptstyle{(m)}}_{k}\varrho_{k})=

\displaystyle\ \left\lvert f^{\scriptscriptstyle{(n)}}_{k}-f^{\scriptscriptstyle{(m)}}_{k}\right\rvert^{2}\Gamma(\varrho_{k})+\left\lvert\varrho_{k}\right\rvert^{2}\Gamma(f^{\scriptscriptstyle{(n)}}_{k}-f^{\scriptscriptstyle{(m)}}_{k})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\ \left\lvert f^{\scriptscriptstyle{(n)}}_{k}-f^{\scriptscriptstyle{(m)}}_{k}\right\rvert^{2}\Gamma(\varrho_{k})+\car_{G_{k}}\Gamma^{\prime}(f^{\scriptscriptstyle{(n)}}_{k}-f^{\scriptscriptstyle{(m)}}_{k})\,\,\mathrm{.}

Since
\varrho_{k}\equiv 0

\mathcal{E}
-q.e. on
G_{k}^{\mathrm{c}}
, and since
G_{k}
is
\mathcal{E}
-quasi-open, by (
2.10
) we conclude that

(4.2)

\displaystyle\Gamma(f^{\scriptscriptstyle{(n)}}_{k}\varrho_{k}-f^{\scriptscriptstyle{(m)}}_{k}\varrho_{k})\leq

\displaystyle\ \car_{G_{k}}\big({\left\lvert f^{\scriptscriptstyle{(n)}}_{k}-f^{\scriptscriptstyle{(m)}}_{k}\right\rvert^{2}\Gamma(\varrho_{k})+\Gamma^{\prime}(f^{\scriptscriptstyle{(n)}}_{k}-f^{\scriptscriptstyle{(m)}}_{k})}\big)\,\,\mathrm{.}

Now, since
\theta\geq a_{k}>0
on
G_{k}
, the
L^{2}(\mathsf{m}^{\prime}_{G_{k}})
-convergence implies the
L^{2}(\mathsf{m}_{G_{k}})
-convergence.
Thus, there exists

(4.3)

L^{2}(\mathsf{m})\text{-}\lim_{n}f^{\scriptscriptstyle{(n)}}_{k}\varrho_{k}=L^{2}(\mathsf{m}^{\prime})\text{-}\lim_{n}f^{\scriptscriptstyle{(n)}}_{k}\varrho_{k}=f_{k}\varrho_{k}=f\varrho_{k}\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,

where the last equality holds by definition of
(G_{\bullet},f_{\bullet})
.
Furthermore, integrating (
4.2
) we see that

(4.4)

\displaystyle\lim_{n,m}

\displaystyle\int\Gamma(f^{\scriptscriptstyle{(n)}}_{k}\varrho_{k}-f^{\scriptscriptstyle{(m)}}_{k}\varrho_{k})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mathsf{m}

\displaystyle\leq a_{k}^{-1}\lim_{n,m}\int_{G_{k}}\left\lvert f^{\scriptscriptstyle{(n)}}_{k}-f^{\scriptscriptstyle{(m)}}_{k}\right\rvert^{2}\Gamma(\varrho_{k})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mathsf{m}^{\prime}+\lim_{n,m}\mathcal{E}^{\prime}(f^{\scriptscriptstyle{(n)}}_{k}-f^{\scriptscriptstyle{(m)}}_{k})=0\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,

where the first limit in the right-hand side vanishes by Dominated Convergence with dominating function
4M^{2}\Gamma(\varrho_{k})
, and the second vanishes by definition of
\left(f^{\scriptscriptstyle{(n)}}_{k}\right)_{n}
.

Combining (
4.3
) and (
4.4
) implies the existence of

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}\text{-}\lim_{n}f^{\scriptscriptstyle{(n)}}_{k}\varrho_{k}=f\varrho_{k}\in\mathcal{F}_{b}\,\,\mathrm{.}

Thus, since
G_{\bullet}
is increasing to
X

\mathcal{E}
-quasi-everywhere, and
\left(f\varrho_{k}\right)_{k}\subset\mathcal{F}
is a sequence of functions satisfying
f\varrho_{k}\equiv f
on
G_{k}
, then
f\in{{\mathcal{F}}^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{loc}}}
by definition of broad local space.

Since
\varrho_{k}\equiv 1
on
E_{k}
, we have
\car_{E_{k}}\Gamma(\varrho_{k})\equiv 0
by (
2.10
). Thus, again by the Leibniz rule and by the assumption,

\displaystyle\car_{E_{k}}\Gamma(f^{\scriptscriptstyle{(n)}}_{k}\varrho_{k})=\car_{E_{k}}\left\lvert f^{\scriptscriptstyle{(n)}}_{k}\right\rvert^{2}\Gamma(\varrho_{k})+\car_{E_{k}}\left\lvert\varrho_{k}\right\rvert^{2}\Gamma(f^{\scriptscriptstyle{(n)}}_{k})=\car_{E_{k}}\Gamma(f^{\scriptscriptstyle{(n)}}_{k})\leq\car_{E_{k}}\Gamma^{\prime}(f^{\scriptscriptstyle{(n)}}_{k})\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,

and, taking the limit as
n\to\infty
(possibly, up to choosing a suitable non-relabeled subsequence),

(4.5)

\begin{aligned} \car_{E_{k}}\Gamma(f\varrho_{k})=&\car_{E_{k}}\Gamma(f_{k}\varrho_{k})=\car_{E_{k}}\lim_{n}\Gamma(f^{\scriptscriptstyle{(n)}}_{k}\varrho_{k})\leq\car_{E_{k}}\lim_{n}\Gamma^{\prime}(f^{\scriptscriptstyle{(n)}}_{k})\\
=&\car_{E_{k}}\Gamma^{\prime}(f_{k})=\car_{E_{k}}\Gamma^{\prime}(f)\leq 1\end{aligned}\quad\quad\mathsf{m}\text{-a.e.}\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,

where the last equality holds by locality (
2.10
) of
\Gamma
and definition of
(G_{\bullet},f_{\bullet})
.
Again by (
2.10
), and by (
4.5
),

\displaystyle\car_{E_{k}}\Gamma(f)=\car_{E_{k}}\Gamma(f\varrho_{k})\leq\car_{E_{k}}\Gamma^{\prime}(f)\quad\mathsf{m}\text{-a.e.}\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,

hence, letting
k\to\infty
and since
\mathsf{m}\big({\cap_{k}E_{k}^{\mathrm{c}}}\big)=0
,

\displaystyle\Gamma(f)\leq\Gamma^{\prime}(f)\leq 1\quad\mathsf{m}\text{-a.e.}\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,

which also shows that
f\in\mathbb{L}^{\mathsf{m}}_{\mathrm{loc},b}
, and thus concludes the proof of the first assertion.

The assertion on the intrinsic distances is an immediate consequence of the first assertion together with the definition (
2.13
) of intrinsic distance.
∎

Simmetrizing the assumptions in Proposition
4.2
and combining it with Proposition
4.1
, we obtain the following.

Corollary 4.4

(Mutual implications for weighted spaces)
.

Let
(\mathbb{X},\mathcal{E})
and
(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathcal{E}^{\prime})
be quasi-regular strongly local Dirichlet spaces with same underlying topological measurable space
(X,\tau,\Sigma)=(X^{\prime},\tau^{\prime},\Sigma^{\prime})
satisfying
2
and different measures
\mathsf{m}
and
\mathsf{m}^{\prime}
.
Further let
\mathsf{d}\colon X^{\scriptscriptstyle{\times 2}}\to[0,\infty]
be an extended pseudo-distance.
Assume that:

(1)

there exists
\mathcal{D}
a pseudo-core for both
(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
on
L^{2}(\mathsf{m})
and
(\mathcal{E}^{\prime},\mathcal{F}^{\prime})
on
L^{2}(\mathsf{m}^{\prime})
, additionally so that
\Gamma=\Gamma^{\prime}
on
\mathcal{D}
.

(2)

\mathsf{m}^{\prime}=\theta\mathsf{m}
for some
\theta\in L^{0}(\mathsf{m})
, and there exists
E_{\bullet},G_{\bullet}\in\mathscr{G}_{0}\cap\mathscr{G}_{0}^{\prime}
with the following properties:

(a)

for each
k\in{\mathbb{N}}
there exists a constant
a_{k}>0
such that

0<a_{k}\leq\theta\leq a_{k}^{-1}<\infty\quad\mathsf{m}\text{-a.e.}\quad\text{on }G_{k}\,\,\mathrm{;}\;\,

that is,
\theta,\theta^{-1}\in{{\big({L^{\infty}(\mathsf{m})}\big)}^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{loc}}}(G_{\bullet})
.

(b)

for each
k\in{\mathbb{N}}
it holds
E_{k}\subset G_{k}

\mathcal{E}
- and
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
-quasi-everywhere, and there exists
\varrho_{k}\in\mathcal{D}
with
\car_{E_{k}}\leq\varrho_{k}\leq\car_{G_{k}}

\mathsf{m}
-a.e..

Then,

(1)

\big({\Gamma,\mathbb{L}^{\mathsf{m}}_{\mathrm{loc},b}}\big)=\big({\Gamma^{\prime},\mathbb{L}^{\prime\,\mathsf{m}^{\prime}}_{\mathrm{loc},b}}\big)
and
\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}}=\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}^{\prime}}
;

(2)

(\mathsf{ScL}_{\mathsf{m},\tau,\mathsf{d}})
, resp.
(\mathsf{SL}_{\mathsf{m},\mathsf{d}})
,
(\mathsf{cSL}_{\tau,\mathsf{m},\mathsf{d}})
,
(\mathsf{Rad}_{\mathsf{d},\mathsf{m}})
, holds if and only if
(\mathsf{ScL}_{\mathsf{m}^{\prime},\tau,\mathsf{d}})
, resp.
(\mathsf{SL}_{\mathsf{m}^{\prime},\mathsf{d}})
,
(\mathsf{cSL}_{\tau,\mathsf{m}^{\prime},\mathsf{d}})
,
(\mathsf{Rad}_{\mathsf{d},\mathsf{m}^{\prime}})
holds.

4.2.
Form comparison

In this section we provide a full comparison of a Dirichlet form
(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
with a Cheeger energy
\mathsf{Ch}_{*,\mathsf{d},\mathsf{m}}
defined on the same space.
Roughly speaking, we show that
\mathcal{E}
is dominated by
\mathsf{Ch}_{*,\mathsf{d},\mathsf{m}}
under the Rademacher property for
\mathsf{d}
, and that the reverse domination holds under the continuous-Sobolev–to–Lipschitz property for
\mathsf{d}
and the
\tau
-upper regularity
property for
(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
(Dfn.
4.10
).

4.2.1.
Form comparison under the Rademacher property

The next Proposition
4.8
is an extension of the same result obtained for
energy-measure spaces
by L. Ambrosio, N. Gigli, and G. Savaré in
[
8
]
, and for extended metric-topological
probability
spaces by L. Ambrosio, M. Erbar, and G. Savaré in
[
4
]
.
In the proof we make use of assertions proven in
[
4
]
for extended metric-topological probability spaces, and of assertions proven in
[
6
]
.
The compatibility between different definitions in these references is granted by Proposition
2.22
.

Lemma 4.5
.

Let
(X,\tau,\mathsf{d})
be an extended metric-topological space (Dfn.
2.3
) and
K\subset X
be
\tau
-compact.
Then,
\mathsf{d}({\,\cdot\,},K)
is
\tau
-lower semicontinuous (in particular,
\mathscr{B}_{\tau}
-measurable).

Remark 4.6
.

When
(X,\tau)
is additionally Polish, Lemma
4.5
is claimed without proof in (the proof of)
[
6
, Lem. 4.11]
.
We provide a proof for completeness and for reference.

Proof of Lemma
4.5
.

By
[
4
, Eqn. (4.3)]
the
\tau
-admissible extended distance
\mathsf{d}
is
\tau^{\scriptscriptstyle{\times 2}}
-lower semicontinuous.
Let
\left(x_{\alpha}\right)_{\alpha}\subset X
be any net
\tau
-converging to
x\in X
.
For each
\varepsilon>0
there exists
y_{\alpha(\varepsilon)}\in K
so that
\mathsf{d}(x_{\alpha},K)\geq\mathsf{d}(x_{\alpha},y_{\alpha(\varepsilon)})-\varepsilon
.
By
\tau
-compactness of
K
, for each
\varepsilon>0
there exists
y_{\varepsilon}\in K
a
\tau
-accumulation point of
\left(y_{\alpha(\varepsilon)}\right)_{\alpha(\varepsilon)}
.
Thus, by the above inequality, by
\tau^{\scriptscriptstyle{\times 2}}
-lower-semicontinuity of
\mathsf{d}
, and since
y_{\varepsilon}\in K
,

\displaystyle\liminf_{\alpha}\mathsf{d}(x_{\alpha},K)\geq\liminf_{\alpha(\varepsilon)}\liminf_{\alpha}\mathsf{d}(x_{\alpha},y_{\alpha(\varepsilon)})-\varepsilon\geq\mathsf{d}(x,y_{\varepsilon})-\varepsilon\geq\mathsf{d}(x,K)-\varepsilon\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,

which concludes the assertion by arbitrariness of
\varepsilon>0
.
∎

The following is a rewriting of
[
6
, Lem. 4.11]
. Whereas our assumptions are milder, the proof in
[
6
]
applies with minor modifications.

Lemma 4.7

(
[
6
, Lem. 4.11]
)
.

Let
(X,\tau,\mathsf{d})
be a complete extended metric-topological measure space, and
\mathsf{m}
be a
\sigma
-finite
\tau
-Radon measure on
\mathscr{B}_{\tau}
.
Further let
\mathsf{m}^{\prime}\eqdef\theta\mathsf{m}
be another
\sigma
-finite measure on
\mathscr{B}_{\tau}
with density
\theta
satisfying the following condition: there exists a sequence of
\tau
-compact sets
K_{i}
with
K_{i}\subset K_{i+1}
, and constants
r_{i},c_{i},C_{i}>0
such that

(4.6)

\mathsf{m}\big({\cap_{i}K_{i}^{\mathrm{c}}}\big)=0\qquad\text{and}\qquad 0<c_{i}\leq\theta\leq C_{i}<\infty\quad\quad\mathsf{m}\text{-a.e.}\text{ on }\left\{\mathsf{d}({\,\cdot\,},K_{i})<r_{i}\right\}\,\,\mathrm{.}

Then, the relaxed gradient
\left\lvert\mathrm{D}f\right\rvert_{*,\mathsf{d},\mathsf{m}^{\prime}}
induced by
\mathsf{m}^{\prime}
coincides
\mathsf{m}
-a.e. with
\left\lvert\mathrm{D}f\right\rvert_{*,\mathsf{d},\mathsf{m}}
for every
f\in\mathscr{D}({\mathsf{Ch}_{*,\mathsf{d},\mathsf{m}^{\prime}}})\cap\mathscr{D}({\mathsf{Ch}_{*,\mathsf{d},\mathsf{m}}})
.
If moreover there exists a single
r>0
such that (
4.6
) holds with
r
in place of
r_{i}
, then

f\in\mathscr{D}({\mathsf{Ch}_{*,\mathsf{d},\mathsf{m}}})\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,\quad f\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,\left\lvert\mathrm{D}f\right\rvert_{*,\mathsf{d},\mathsf{m}}\in L^{2}(\mathsf{m}^{\prime})\implies f\in\mathscr{D}({\mathsf{Ch}_{*,\mathsf{d},\mathsf{m}^{\prime}}})\,\,\mathrm{.}

Proof.

Since
\mathsf{m}
is
\sigma
-finite
\tau
-Radon, there exists a sequence of
\tau
-compact sets
K_{i}
satisfying
\mathsf{m}\big({\cap_{i}K_{i}^{\mathrm{c}}}\big)
.
The rest of the proof holds exactly as in
[
6
]
having care to substitute the
\tau
-compact set
K
there with
K_{i}
such that
\mathsf{m}(K\cap K_{i})>0
which exists since
\left(K_{i}\right)_{i}
exhausts
X
up to an
\mathsf{m}
-negligible set.
∎

Proposition 4.8
.

Let
(\mathbb{X},\mathcal{E})
be a quasi-regular strongly local Dirichlet space with
\mathbb{X}
satisfying
2
and admitting carré du champ operator, and
\mathsf{d}\colon X^{\scriptscriptstyle{\times 2}}\to[0,\infty)
be an extended distance.
Further assume that

(1)

(X,\tau,\mathsf{d})
is a complete extended metric-topological space (Dfn.
2.3
);

(2)

\mathsf{m}
is
\mathsf{d}
-moderate
in the following sense: there exists a
\tau
-compact
\mathcal{E}
-nest
\left(K_{i}\right)_{i}
and
\varepsilon>0
such that

(4.7)

\kappa_{i}\eqdef\mathsf{m}\left\{\mathsf{d}({\,\cdot\,},K_{i})<\varepsilon\right\}<\infty\,\,\mathrm{.}

If
(\mathbb{X},\mathcal{E})
satisfies
(\mathsf{Rad}_{\mathsf{d},\mathsf{m}})
for
\mathscr{B}_{\tau}
, then

(4.8)

\Gamma(f)\leq\left\lvert\mathrm{D}f\right\rvert_{w,\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}}}^{2}\leq\left\lvert\mathrm{D}f\right\rvert_{w,\mathsf{d}}^{2}\quad\quad\mathsf{m}\text{-a.e.}\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,\qquad f\in\mathrm{Lip}_{b}(\mathsf{d},\mathscr{B}_{\tau})\,\,\mathrm{.}

In particular,

(4.9)

\mathcal{E}\leq\mathsf{Ch}_{w,\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}},\mathsf{m}}\leq\mathsf{Ch}_{w,\mathsf{d},\mathsf{m}}\,\,\mathrm{.}

Remark 4.9
.

The existence of a
\tau
-compact
\mathcal{E}
-nest in
2
is always satisfied as a consequence of the quasi-regularity of
(\mathbb{X},\mathcal{E})
.
The second condition is a form of
moderance
of
\mathsf{m}
—in the sense of
[
18
, §2.5.2]
— compatible with
\mathsf{d}
.
Together with the assumption of the Rademacher property
(\mathsf{Rad}_{\mathsf{d},\mathsf{m}})
, it is tantamount to the
\mathsf{m}
-uniform algebraic
\tau
-localizability (Dfn.
2.12
) of
(\mathbb{X},\mathcal{E})
.
Consistently with Remark
2.13
, the assumption in (
4.7
) is trivially satisfied whenever
\mathsf{m}X<\infty
, which is why this assumption does not appear in
[
4
]
, addressing only probability spaces.

Proof of Proposition
4.8
.

By (
2.14
) the space
(\mathbb{X},\mathcal{E})
satisfies
({\mathsf{d}}\textrm{-}\mathsf{Rad}_{\mathsf{m}})
, which implies the second inequality by definition of the objects involved.
Thus, it suffices to show the first inequality.
Firstly, let us note that
(X,\tau,\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}})
is a complete extended metric-topological space by Lemma
2.16
.
Suppose for the moment that
\mathsf{m}
be a probability measure.
Since
(X,\tau)
is a topological Luzin space, it is a Radon space.
In particular,
\mathsf{m}
is Radon.
Then, we can apply
[
4
, Thm. 12.5]
to the complete metric-topological Radon probability space
(X,\tau,\mathsf{d},\mathsf{m})
and conclude the assertion.

Heuristics

Now, let us show how to extend the statement to the case of any
\sigma
-finite measure
\mathsf{m}
.
We need to treat simultaneously the square field operator
\Gamma
and the minimal weak upper gradient
\left\lvert\mathrm{D}{\,\cdot\,}\right\rvert_{w,\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}},\mathsf{m}}
.
To this end, we find a probability density
\theta\in L^{1}(\mathsf{m})
, set
\mathsf{m}^{\prime}\eqdef\theta\mathsf{m}
, and show that
\Gamma=\Gamma^{\prime}
and
\left\lvert\mathrm{D}{\,\cdot\,}\right\rvert_{w,\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}},\mathsf{m}}=\left\lvert\mathrm{D}{\,\cdot\,}\right\rvert_{w,\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}},\mathsf{m}^{\prime}}
.
The conclusion follows from these equalities together with the inequality established in the probability case applied to
\mathsf{m}^{\prime}
.

For the square field, we make use of the result on the Girsanov transform of
(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
by a factor
\sqrt{\theta}\in\mathcal{F}
, thoroughly discussed in the generality of quasi-regular Dirichlet spaces by C.-Z. Chen and W. Sun in
[
12
]
.
For the minimal weak upper gradient, we make use of the locality result for
\left\lvert\mathrm{D}{\,\cdot\,}\right\rvert_{w,\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}},\mathsf{m}}
under transformation of the reference measure by a factor
\theta
locally bounded away from
0
and infinity on neighborhoods of compact sets, Lemma
4.7
.

Reduction

Since
\mathsf{m}X=\infty
by assumption, there exists
i_{*}
such that
\mathsf{m}K_{i}\geq 1
for every
i\geq i_{*}
.
Thus, up to discarding the first
i_{*}
elements of
\left(K_{i}\right)_{i}
, we may and will assume with no loss of generality that
\kappa_{i}\geq 1
for every
i
.
As a consequence,
\kappa_{i}^{-1/2}\leq 1
for every
i\in{\mathbb{N}}
.
We shall further assume that
\varepsilon\leq 1
, again with no loss of generality up to replacing
\varepsilon
by
\varepsilon\vee 1
.
Throughout the proof we let
(K_{i})_{\delta}\eqdef\left\{\mathsf{d}({\,\cdot\,},K_{i})<\delta\right\}
for all
\delta>0
.

Construction of a density

We start by showing that there exists a
\mathscr{B}_{\tau}
-measurable
\theta\colon X\to{\mathbb{R}}
satisfying:

(4.10)

\theta\in L^{1}(\mathsf{m})\cap L^{\infty}(\mathsf{m})\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,\qquad\left\lVert\theta\right\rVert_{L^{1}(\mathsf{m})}=1\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,\qquad\theta>0\quad\mathsf{m}\text{-a.e.}\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,\qquad\sqrt{\theta}\in\mathcal{F}\,\,\mathrm{.}

Let
\left(K_{i}\right)_{i}
and
\varepsilon
be as in (
4.7
), and
S\colon[0,\infty]\to[0,1]
be defined by

(4.11)

S(t)\eqdef\begin{cases}-\frac{1}{2}t+1&\text{if }0\leq t\leq 1\\
\frac{1}{2}&\text{if }1\leq t\leq 2\\
-\frac{1}{2}t+\frac{3}{2}&\text{if }2\leq t\leq 3\\
0&\text{if }3\leq t\leq+\infty\end{cases}\,\,\mathrm{.}

Further set (cf. Fig.s
1
and
2
below)

\psi_{i}\eqdef S\big({\tfrac{3}{\varepsilon}\,\mathsf{d}({\,\cdot\,},K_{i})}\big)\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,\qquad f_{i}\eqdef\frac{\varepsilon}{3\sqrt{\kappa_{i}}}\,\psi_{i}\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,\qquad\phi_{n}\eqdef\sum_{i=0}^{n}2^{-i-1}f_{i}\,\,\mathrm{.}

All the above functions are
\mathscr{B}_{\tau}
-measurable (in fact:
\tau
-upper semicontinuous) by Lemma
4.5
.

Figure 1.
The functions
\psi_{i}
.

Figure 2.
The function
\phi_{3}
, taking value
\tfrac{1}{2}+\tfrac{1}{4}+\tfrac{1}{8}
on
K_{1}
.

Since
\kappa_{i}\eqdef\mathsf{m}\left\{\mathsf{d}(x,K_{i})<\varepsilon\right\}<\infty
we have that
f_{i}\in L^{2}(\mathsf{m})
and
\left\lVert f_{i}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathsf{m})}\leq\tfrac{\varepsilon}{3}\leq 1
for every
i
.
Thus, the sequence
\left(\phi_{n}\right)_{n}
satisfies

\displaystyle\left\lVert\phi_{n}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathsf{m})}\leq 1\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,\qquad L^{2}(\mathsf{m})\text{-}\lim_{n}\phi_{n}=\sum_{i}^{\infty}2^{-i-1}f_{i}\eqqcolon\phi\in L^{2}(\mathsf{m})

by Monotone Convergence.
Furthermore,
f_{i}\in\mathrm{Lip}_{b}(\mathsf{d},\mathscr{B}_{\tau})
and
\mathrm{L}_{\mathsf{d}}(f_{i})\leq(\kappa_{i})^{-1/2}\leq 1
for every
i
by definition, hence

(4.12)

\displaystyle\phi_{n}\in\mathrm{Lip}_{b}(\mathsf{d},\mathscr{B}_{\tau})\qquad\text{and}\qquad\mathrm{L}_{\mathsf{d}}(\phi_{n})\leq 1

for every
n
by triangle inequality for the Lipschitz semi-norm
\mathrm{L}_{\mathsf{d}}({\,\cdot\,})
.

As a consequence,
\left(\phi_{n}\right)_{n}
is uniformly bounded in
\mathcal{F}
by
(\mathsf{Rad}_{\mathsf{d},\mathsf{m}})
and thus
\phi\in\mathcal{F}
by e.g.
[
33
, Lem. I.2.12]
.
Since

(4.13)

\phi\big\lvert_{(K_{i})_{\varepsilon/3}}\geq 2^{-i-1}f_{i}\big\lvert_{(K_{i})_{\varepsilon/3}}=\frac{2^{-i-2}\varepsilon}{3\sqrt{\kappa_{i}}}>0

and since
\left(K_{i}\right)_{i}
exhausts
X
up to an
\mathsf{m}
-negligible set, then
\phi>0

\mathsf{m}
-a.e. on
X
.
Letting
\theta\eqdef\phi^{2}/\left\lVert\phi\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathsf{m})}^{2}
shows the assertion in (
4.10
).

Dirichlet forms

Now, let us set
\mathsf{m}^{\prime}\eqdef\theta\mathsf{m}\sim\mathsf{m}
.
Since
\sqrt{\theta}\in\mathcal{F}_{b}
, by
[
12
, Thm. 2.2]
the form

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}^{\prime}(f)\eqdef\int_{X}\Gamma(f)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mathsf{m}^{\prime}\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,\qquad f\in\mathcal{F}\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,

is closable, and its closure
\big({\mathcal{E}^{\prime},\mathcal{F}^{\prime}}\big)
is a Dirichlet form on
L^{2}(\mathsf{m}^{\prime})
with square field operator
\Gamma^{\prime}
satisfying

(4.14)

\displaystyle\Gamma^{\prime}(f)=\Gamma(f)\quad\mathsf{m}^{\prime}\text{-a.e.}\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,\qquad f\in\mathcal{F}\cap L^{\infty}(\mathsf{m}^{\prime})=\mathcal{F}_{b}\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,

and the equality extends to all
f\in\mathcal{F}_{b}^{\prime}
by
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
-density of
\mathcal{F}
in
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
.
In particular, since
\mathsf{m}^{\prime}
is a finite measure,
\mathcal{F}_{b}^{\prime}\supset\mathrm{Lip}_{b}(\mathsf{d},\mathscr{B}_{\tau})
by
(\mathsf{Rad}_{\mathsf{d},\mathsf{m}})
, and (
4.14
) holds for every
f\in\mathrm{Lip}_{b}(\mathsf{d},\mathscr{B}_{\tau})
.

Minimal relaxed slopes

On the complete extended metric-topological measure space
(X,\tau,\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}},\mathsf{m})
we have that

(4.15)

\left\lvert\mathrm{D}f\right\rvert_{*,\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}},\mathsf{m}}=\left\lvert\mathrm{D}f\right\rvert_{w,\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}},\mathsf{m}}\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,\qquad f\in\mathrm{Lip}_{b}(\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}},\mathscr{B}_{\tau})\,\,\mathrm{.}

This readily follows from the locality of both
\left\lvert\mathrm{D}{\,\cdot\,}\right\rvert_{*}
and
\left\lvert\mathrm{D}{\,\cdot\,}\right\rvert_{w}
in the sense of e.g.
[
6
, Prop. 4.8(b)]
and
[
7
, Eqn. (2.18)]
.

Secondly, let us verify that the space
(X,\tau,\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}},\mathsf{m})
satisfies the assumptions of Lemma
4.7
.
The fact that
(X,\tau,\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}})
is an extended metric-topological space was already noted in the beginning of the proof.
Thus, it suffices to show that
\theta
as above satisfies (
4.6
) with
\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}}
in place of
\mathsf{d}
there.
In fact, since
\theta\in L^{\infty}(\mathsf{m})
, it suffices to show the existence of
c_{i}
and
r_{i}
.
We show that there exists
c_{i}
satisfying (
4.6
) with
r_{i}\eqdef\varepsilon/3
.
Since
\mathsf{d}\leq\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}}
by
(\mathsf{Rad}_{\mathsf{d},\mathsf{m}})
and (
2.14
), we have that
\left\{\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}}({\,\cdot\,},K_{i})<\varepsilon/3\right\}\subset(K_{i})_{\varepsilon/3}
for every
i\in{\mathbb{N}}
.
By (
4.13
), it suffices to set
c_{i}\eqdef\left(\frac{2^{-i-2}\varepsilon}{3\sqrt{\kappa_{i}}\left\lVert\phi\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathsf{m})}}\right)^{2}
.

Now, applying Lemma
4.7
to the probability measure
\mathsf{m}^{\prime}
, we have that

(4.16)

\displaystyle\left\lvert\mathrm{D}f\right\rvert_{*,\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}},\mathsf{m}}=\left\lvert\mathrm{D}f\right\rvert_{*,\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}},\mathsf{m}^{\prime}}\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,\qquad f\in\mathrm{Lip}_{b}(\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}},\mathscr{B}_{\tau})\supset\mathrm{Lip}_{b}(\mathsf{d},\mathscr{B}_{\tau})\,\,\mathrm{.}

Intrinsic distances

We claim that
\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}}=\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}^{\prime}}
.
Let us verify the assumptions of Corollary
4.4
with
\mathcal{D}\eqdef\mathcal{F}
.
Indeed,
\mathcal{F}
is a (pseudo-)core for both
(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
and
(\mathcal{E}^{\prime},\mathcal{F}^{\prime})
and
\Gamma=\Gamma^{\prime}
on
\mathcal{F}
by (
4.14
).
This verifies assumption
1
in Corollary
4.4
.
In light of (
4.6
) we see that
\theta
satisfies the assumptions in Corollary
4.4
2a
with
E_{k}\eqdef\interior_{\mathcal{E}}K_{k}
,
G_{k}\eqdef\interior_{\mathcal{E}}(K_{k})_{\varepsilon/3}
, and
a_{k}\eqdef c_{k}
.
Indeed
E_{\bullet},G_{\bullet}\in\mathscr{G}_{0}
since
K_{\bullet}
is an
\mathcal{E}
-nest, and
E_{\bullet},G_{\bullet}\in\mathscr{G}_{0}^{\prime}
since
\mathcal{F}
is
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
-dense in
\mathcal{F}
by construction of
(\mathcal{E}^{\prime},\mathcal{F}^{\prime})
.
Now, let
\varrho_{k}\eqdef\left[1-\tfrac{3}{\varepsilon}\mathsf{d}({\,\cdot\,},K_{k})\right]_{+}
and note that
\varrho_{k}\in\mathrm{Lip}_{b}(\mathsf{d},\mathscr{B}_{\tau})
by Lemma
4.5
.
Thus,
\Gamma(\varrho_{k})\leq\mathrm{L}_{\mathsf{d}}(\varrho_{k})^{2}=9\varepsilon^{-2}
by
(\mathsf{Rad}_{\mathsf{d},\mathsf{m}})
and in fact
\Gamma(\varrho_{k})\leq 9\varepsilon^{-2}\,\car_{G_{k}}
by (
2.10
).
Since
\theta\geq c_{k}>0
on
(K_{k})_{\varepsilon/3}\supset G_{k}
, we conclude that
\Gamma(\varrho_{k})\in L^{1}(\mathsf{m})
too.
Thus, the assumptions in Corollary
4.4
2b
hold as well.
This concludes the verification of the assumptions in Corollary
4.4
, and thus
\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}}=\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}^{\prime}}
.

Conclusion

Respectively by (
4.14
),
[
4
, Thm. 12.5]
, (
4.15
) and
\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}}=\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}^{\prime}}
, (
4.16
), and again (
4.15
),

\displaystyle\Gamma(f)=\Gamma^{\prime}(f)\leq\left\lvert\mathrm{D}f\right\rvert_{w,\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}^{\prime}},\mathsf{m}^{\prime}}^{2}=\left\lvert\mathrm{D}f\right\rvert_{*,\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}},\mathsf{m}^{\prime}}^{2}=\left\lvert\mathrm{D}f\right\rvert_{*,\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}},\mathsf{m}}^{2}=\left\lvert\mathrm{D}f\right\rvert_{w,\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}},\mathsf{m}}^{2}\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,\qquad f\in\mathrm{Lip}_{b}(\mathsf{d},\mathscr{B}_{\tau})\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,

which concludes the proof.
∎

4.2.2.
Form comparison under the Sobolev-to-Lipschitz property

The chain of inequalities (
4.8
) in Proposition
4.8
is completed by including the slope of a Lipschitz function, viz.

\Gamma(f)\leq\left\lvert\mathrm{D}f\right\rvert_{w,\,\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}}}^{2}\leq\left\lvert\mathrm{D}f\right\rvert_{w,\,\mathsf{d}}^{2}\leq\left\lvert\mathrm{D}f\right\rvert_{\mathsf{d}}^{2}\quad\quad\mathsf{m}\text{-a.e.}\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,\qquad f\in\mathrm{Lip}_{b}(\mathsf{d},\mathscr{B}_{\tau})\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,

where the last inequality holds by the very definition of the minimal weak upper gradient.
In the same spirit of duality as in §
3
, this suggests that the opposite chain of inequalities, viz.

(4.17)

\Gamma(f)\geq\left\lvert\mathrm{D}f\right\rvert_{w,\,\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}}}^{2}\geq\left\lvert\mathrm{D}f\right\rvert_{w,\,\mathsf{d}}^{2}\geq\left\lvert\mathrm{D}f\right\rvert_{\mathsf{d}}^{2}\quad\quad\mathsf{m}\text{-a.e.}\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,\qquad f\in\mathbb{L}^{\mathsf{m},\tau}_{\mathrm{loc},b}\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,

may be satisfied if some Sobolev-to-Lipschitz-type property holds.

Indeed, under the assumption of
(\mathsf{cSL}_{\tau,\mathsf{m},\mathsf{d}})
—hence, in light of (
2.14
), under
(\mathsf{SL}_{\mathsf{m},\mathsf{d}})
as well—the second inequality is a consequence of
({\mathsf{d}}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SL}_{\mathsf{m}})
, given by (
2.14
), together with the definition of the objects involved.
The last inequality is in fact an equality, since the opposite inequality always holds, as detailed above.
Thus, (
4.17
) in fact reads

(4.18)

\Gamma(f)\geq\left\lvert\mathrm{D}f\right\rvert_{w,\,\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}}}^{2}\geq\left\lvert\mathrm{D}f\right\rvert_{w,\,\mathsf{d}}^{2}=\left\lvert\mathrm{D}f\right\rvert_{\mathsf{d}}^{2}\quad\quad\mathsf{m}\text{-a.e.}\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,\qquad f\in\mathbb{L}^{\mathsf{m},\tau}_{\mathrm{loc},b}\,\,\mathrm{.}

In order to discuss the validity of (
4.18
) we shall further need the following definition, introduced by L. Ambrosio, N. Gigli, and G. Savaré in
[
8
, Dfn. 3.13]
(also cf.
[
4
, Dfn. 12.4]
for the generality of extended metric-topological measure spaces).

Definition 4.10

(
\tau
-upper regularity)
.

Let
(\mathbb{X},\mathcal{E})
be a (quasi-regular strongly local) Dirichlet space with
\mathbb{X}
satisfying
2
and admitting carré du champ operator
\Gamma
.
We say that
(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
is
\tau
-upper regular
if for every
f
in a pseudo-core of
(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
there exists
\left(f_{n}\right)_{n}\subset\mathcal{F}_{b}\cap\mathcal{C}_{b}(\tau)
and a sequence of bounded
\tau
-upper semi-continuous functions
g_{n}\colon X\to{\mathbb{R}}
such that

(4.19)

L^{2}(\mathsf{m})\text{-}\lim_{n}f_{n}=f\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,\qquad\sqrt{\Gamma(f_{n})}\leq g_{n}\quad\mathsf{m}\text{-a.e.}\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,\qquad\limsup_{n}\,\int_{X}g_{n}^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mathsf{m}\leq\mathcal{E}(f)\,\,\mathrm{.}

Remark 4.11
.

It is noted in the proof of
[
8
, Thm. 3.14]
(after the approximation results for the asymptotic Lipschitz constant in
[
5
, §8.3]
), resp. in
[
4
, Thm. 9.2]
, that the Cheeger energy
\mathsf{Ch}_{w,\mathsf{d},\mathsf{m}}
of a metric measure space, resp. of an extended metric-topological probability space, is always
\tau
-upper regular.
As a consequence, in comparing a general quasi-regular Dirichlet form
(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
with a Cheeger energy on the same (extended metric-topological) space, it is in fact necessary to discuss the
\tau
-upper regularity of
(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
.

Under the assumption of
\tau
-upper regularity, we now show the dual statement to Proposition
4.8
.
Again, we adapt to the
\sigma
-finite case the corresponding result in
[
4
]
for extended metric-topological probability spaces.

Proposition 4.12
.

Let
(\mathbb{X},\mathcal{E})
be a quasi-regular strongly local Dirichlet space with
\mathbb{X}
satisfying
2
and admitting carré du champ operator, and
\mathsf{d}\colon X^{\scriptscriptstyle{\times 2}}\to[0,\infty]
be an extended distance.
Further assume that

(1)

\mathbb{L}^{\mathsf{m},\tau}_{\mathrm{loc},b}
generates
\tau
;

(2)

(\mathbb{X},\mathcal{E},\mathsf{m})
satisfies
(\mathsf{Loc}_{\mathsf{m},\tau})
;

(3)

(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
is
\tau
-upper regular.

If
(\mathbb{X},\mathcal{E})
satisfies
(\mathsf{cSL}_{\tau,\mathsf{d},\mathsf{m}})
, then

(4.20)

\Gamma(f)\geq\left\lvert\mathrm{D}f\right\rvert_{w,\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}}}^{2}\geq\left\lvert\mathrm{D}f\right\rvert_{w,\mathsf{d}}^{2}\quad\quad\mathsf{m}\text{-a.e.}\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,\qquad f\in\mathbb{L}^{\mathsf{m},\tau}_{\mathrm{loc},b}\,\,\mathrm{.}

In particular,
\mathcal{E}\geq\mathsf{Ch}_{w,\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}},\mathsf{m}}\geq\mathsf{Ch}_{w,\mathsf{d},\mathsf{m}}
.

Remark 4.13

(Comparison with Proposition
4.8
)
.

As anticipated above, Proposition
4.12
ought to be understood as ‘dual’ to Proposition
4.8
.
In this respect:

(1)

The assumption in Proposition
4.12
1
is one about the largeness of
\mathbb{L}^{\mathsf{m},\tau}_{\mathrm{loc},b}
.
It is required to guarantee the validity of
[
4
, Eqn. (12.1b)]
for the —in our case:
given
— topology
\tau
.
In Proposition
4.8
such largeness is granted directly by the Rademacher property.
Furthermore, let us note that this assumption is to be compared with the one in Proposition
4.8
1
.
Indeed, recall that
\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}}
is always
\tau^{\scriptscriptstyle{\times 2}}
-lower semicontinuous and
\tau
-admissible, as noted after Definition
2.11
.
Now, since
\mathbb{L}^{\mathsf{m},\tau}_{\mathrm{loc},b}
generates
\tau
, it in particular separates points (since
(X,\tau)
is Hausdorff), hence
\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}}
separates points as well.
Thus,
(X,\tau,\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}})
is an extended metric-topological space (Dfn.
2.3
), which translates into the assumption in Proposition
4.8
1
when
\mathsf{d}
is replace by
\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}}
.

(2)

When
\mathbb{X}
is additionally locally compact, the assumption in Proposition
4.12
1
may be directly replaced with

•

\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}}
separates points, i.e. it is an extended distance (as opposed to: extended
pseudo
-distance).

(3)

As already commented in Remark
4.9
, the assumption in Proposition
4.12
2
is dual to the one in Proposition
4.8
2
.

(4)

As for
\tau
-upper regularity, we refer the reader to
[
8
,
4
]
for a detailed explanation of this condition.
As pointed out in
[
8
,
4
]
, the assumption of
\tau
-upper regularity is
necessary
to the validity of (the first inequality in (
4.20
) in) Proposition
4.12
.

Proof of Remark
4.13
2
.

Since generating
\tau
is a local property, it suffices to show the statement when
(X,\tau)
is compact.
Since
\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}}
separates points, so does
\mathbb{L}^{\mathsf{m},\tau}_{\mathrm{loc},b}
.
Fix a
\tau
-closed set
K\subset X
, and note that it is
\tau
-compact.
For fixed
x\in K^{\mathrm{c}}
and each
y\in K
let
f_{y}\in\mathbb{L}^{\mathsf{m},\tau}_{\mathrm{loc},b}
be separating
x
from
y
.
Without loss of generality —up to possibly changing the sign of
f
and adding to it a constant function in
\mathbb{L}^{\mathsf{m},\tau}_{\mathrm{loc},b}
—, we may assume that
f(x)=0
and
f(y)=a_{y}>0
.
(We may however
not
directly assume that
f(y)=1
, since
\mathbb{L}^{\mathsf{m},\tau}_{\mathrm{loc},b}
is not a linear space.)
Since each
f_{y}
is
\tau
-continuous, the family
\left\{f_{y}>a_{y}/2\right\}_{y\in K}
is a
\tau
-open cover of
K
.
Let
\left(y_{i}\right)_{i\leq n}
be defining a finite subcover and set
f_{x,K}\eqdef\wedge_{i\leq n}f_{y_{i}}
.
Then,
f_{x,K}\in\mathbb{L}^{\mathsf{m},\tau}_{\mathrm{loc},b}
,
f_{x,K}(x)=0
, and
f_{x,K}>0
everywhere on
K
; that is,
f_{x,K}
separates
x
from
K
.
Since
K
was arbitrary,
\mathbb{L}^{\mathsf{m},\tau}_{\mathrm{loc},b}
separates points from
\tau
-closed sets.
By standard arguments, any family of
\tau
-continuous functions separating points from
\tau
-closed sets generates
\tau
.
∎

Proof of Proposition
4.12
.

Since
(\mathsf{cSL}_{\tau,\mathsf{m},\mathsf{d}})
implies
({\mathsf{d}}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SL}_{\mathsf{m}})
by (
2.14
), the second inequality in (
4.20
) holds by definition of the objects involved.
In order to show the first inequality, we argue similarly to the proof of Proposition
4.8
.

Construction of a density

Let
\left(\theta_{i}\right)_{i}
be a latticial approximation of the identity witnessing
(\mathsf{Loc}_{\mathsf{m},\tau})
.
Without loss of generality, we may and will assume that
\left\{\theta_{1}\geq 3\right\}\neq\emp
, hence that
\left\{\theta_{i}\geq 3\right\}\neq\emp
for every
i\in{\mathbb{N}}
.
Further let
S\colon[0,\infty]\to[0,1]
be defined as in (
4.11
), and set

\psi_{i}\eqdef S\big({3-(\theta_{i}({\,\cdot\,})\wedge 3)}\big)\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,\qquad i\in{\mathbb{N}}\,\,\mathrm{.}

Note that
\psi_{i}
has the same shape as in Figure
1
, and, for each
i\in{\mathbb{N}}
,

(1)

since
\car\in\mathbb{L}^{\mathsf{m},\tau}_{\mathrm{loc},b}
and
\Gamma(\car)\equiv 0
by locality, and since
\theta_{i}\in\mathbb{L}^{\mathsf{m},\tau}_{b}
, we have
\psi_{i}\in\mathbb{L}^{\mathsf{m},\tau}_{b}\subset\mathcal{F}
by (
2.9
) and (
2.10
).

(2)

since
\theta_{i}\nearrow_{i}\infty
, the sets
E_{i}\eqdef\inter_{\tau}\left\{\psi_{i}\equiv 1\right\}
form a
\tau
-open exhaustion of
X
;

(3)

since
\theta_{i}\nearrow_{i}\infty
and
\theta_{i}\in\mathcal{C}_{b}(\tau)
(hence
\psi_{i}\in\mathcal{C}_{b}(\tau)
), the sets
G_{i}\eqdef\left\{\psi_{i}>\tfrac{1}{2}\right\}
form a
\tau
-open exhaustion of
X
;

(4)

\mathrm{cl}_{\tau}E_{i}\subset G_{i}
;

(5)

since
\psi_{i}\in L^{2}(\mathsf{m})
, we have
\mathsf{m}G_{i}<\infty
.

Since
\left(G_{i}\right)_{i}
is an exhaustion of
X
, we may and will assume with no loss of generality —up to dropping some of the first elements of all sequences above— that
\mathsf{m}G_{1}\geq 1
, in such a way
(\mathsf{m}G_{i})^{-1/2}\leq 1
for every
i\in{\mathbb{N}}
.
Now, set

f_{i}\eqdef\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mathsf{m}G_{i}}}\psi_{i}\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,\qquad\phi_{n}\eqdef\sum_{i}^{n}2^{-i-1}f_{i}\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,

and note that
f_{i}\in\mathbb{L}^{\mathsf{m},\tau}_{\mathrm{loc},b}
, hence
f_{i}\in\mathrm{Lip}(\mathsf{d},\tau)
and
\mathrm{L}_{\mathsf{d}}(f_{i})\leq\mathrm{L}_{\mathsf{d}}(\psi_{i})\leq 1
by
(\mathsf{cSL}_{\tau,\mathsf{m},\mathsf{d}})
and by
1
above, hence (
4.12
) holds.
Furthermore, similarly to the proof of Proposition
4.8
, we have
\left\lVert\phi_{n}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathsf{m})}\leq 1
for every
n
.
Thus, similarly to the proof of Proposition
4.8
, there exists the monotone limit
\phi\in\mathcal{F}
.
Since
\psi_{i}\nearrow_{i}1
, we additionally have
\phi>0

\mathsf{m}
-a.e..
Letting
\theta\eqdef\phi^{2}/\left\lVert\phi\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathsf{m})}^{2}
shows the assertion in (
4.10
).

Dirichlet forms

Arguing exactly as in the proof of Proposition
4.8
we conclude (
4.14
).
Since
\mathsf{m}^{\prime}
is a finite measure,
\mathcal{F}_{b}^{\prime}\supset\mathbb{L}^{\mathsf{m},\tau}_{\mathrm{loc},b}
, and thus (
4.14
) holds for every
f\in\mathbb{L}^{\mathsf{m},\tau}_{\mathrm{loc},b}
.

Intrinsic distances

We claim that
\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}}=\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}^{\prime}}
.
Let us verify the assumptions in Corollary
4.4
with
\mathcal{D}\eqdef\mathcal{F}
.
Indeed,
\mathcal{F}
is a (pseudo-)core for both
(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
and
(\mathcal{E}^{\prime},\mathcal{F}^{\prime})
and
\Gamma=\Gamma^{\prime}
on
\mathcal{F}
by (
4.14
).
This verifies assumption
1
in Corollary
4.4
.
The sequences
E_{\bullet}
and
G_{\bullet}
are
\tau
-open exhaustions of
X
, and thus satisfy
E_{\bullet},G_{\bullet}\in\mathscr{G}_{0}\cap\mathscr{G}_{0}^{\prime}
(see
2
,
3
above).
Furthermore
a_{i}\eqdef\big({\frac{2^{-i-2}}{\sqrt{\mathsf{m}G_{i}}\left\lVert\phi\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathsf{m})}}}\big)^{2}\leq\theta
everywhere on
E_{i}
.
This verifies assumption
2a
in Corollary
4.4
.

Finally, let
\varrho_{k}\eqdef\big[\theta_{i}\wedge 3-2\big]_{+}\subset\mathcal{F}
and note that
\varrho_{k}\in\mathbb{L}^{\mathsf{m},\tau}_{b}
by (
2.8
) and (
2.10
), and that
\car_{E_{i}}\leq\varrho_{i}\leq\car_{G_{i}}
since
G_{i}\eqdef\left\{\psi_{i}\geq\tfrac{1}{2}\right\}=\left\{\theta_{i}\geq 1\right\}
.
That is, assumption
2b
in Corollary
4.4
is satisfied.
This concludes the verification of the assumptions in Corollary
4.4
, and thus
\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}}=\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}^{\prime}}
.

Minimal relaxed slopes

In light of Remark
4.13
1
, the space
(X,\tau,\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}})
is an extended metric-topological space.
As in the proof of Proposition
4.8
, let us verify that the space
(X,\tau,\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}},\mathsf{m})
satisfies the assumptions of Lemma
4.7
.
By quasi-regularity of
(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
there exists a
\tau
-compact
\mathcal{E}
-nest
\left(K^{\prime}_{i}\right)_{i}
.
For each
i\in{\mathbb{N}}
, the set
K_{i}\eqdef\mathrm{cl}_{\tau}(K_{i}^{\prime}\cap E_{i})
is
\tau
-compact, being the closure of a relatively
\tau
-compact set, satisfies
K_{i}\subset G_{i}
by
4
above, and is thus a
\tau
-compact
\mathcal{E}
-nest since
\left(E_{i}\right)_{i}
and
\left(K_{i}\right)_{i}
are
\mathcal{E}
-nests.
Thus, since
\theta\in L^{\infty}(\mathsf{m})
, it suffices to verify that there exists
\varepsilon>0
and, for every
i\in{\mathbb{N}}
, there exists
c_{i}>0
, so that
\theta>c_{i}
on
(K_{i})_{\varepsilon}\eqdef\left\{\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}}({\,\cdot\,},K_{i})<\varepsilon\right\}
.

Since
\psi_{i}\in\mathbb{L}^{\tau,\mathsf{m}}_{b}
by
1
above,

\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}}({\,\cdot\,},K_{i})\geq\inf_{x\in K_{i}}\big({\psi_{i}(x)-\psi_{i}({\,\cdot\,})}\big)=1-\psi_{i}({\,\cdot\,})\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,

hence

(K_{i})_{\varepsilon}\subset\left\{\psi_{i}\geq 1-\varepsilon\right\}=\left\{f_{i}\geq\frac{1-\varepsilon}{\sqrt{\mathsf{m}G_{i}}}\right\}=\left\{\frac{2^{-i-1}f_{i}}{\left\lVert\phi\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathsf{m})}^{2}}\geq\frac{2^{-i-1}(1-\varepsilon)}{\sqrt{\mathsf{m}G_{i}}\left\lVert\phi\right\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathsf{m})}}\right\}\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,

and choosing
\varepsilon\eqdef 1/2
and
c_{i}\eqdef a_{i}
as above we conclude that
(K_{i})_{1/2}\subset\left\{\theta\geq c_{i}\right\}
.
Thus, (
4.6
) holds and the assumptions of Lemma
4.7
are satisfied.
Now, applying (the second assertion in) Lemma
4.7
to the probability measure
\mathsf{m}^{\prime}
, we have that

(4.21)

\displaystyle\left\lvert\mathrm{D}f\right\rvert_{*,\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}},\mathsf{m}}=\left\lvert\mathrm{D}f\right\rvert_{*,\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}},\mathsf{m}^{\prime}}\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,\qquad f\in\mathscr{D}({\mathsf{Ch}_{*,\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}},\mathsf{m}}})\,\,\mathrm{.}

Conclusion

When
\mathsf{m}
is a probability measure, the sought inequality
\Gamma(f)\geq\left\lvert\mathrm{D}f\right\rvert_{w,\,\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}},\mathsf{m}}^{2}
is shown in
[
4
]
under the assumption of
\tau
-upper regularity.
Thus, it suffices to verify that
\tau
-upper regularity too is transferred from
(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
to
(\mathcal{E}^{\prime},\mathcal{F}^{\prime})
.
Let
\mathcal{D}
be a pseudo-core for
(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
witnessing its
\tau
-upper regularity, and note that
\mathcal{D}
is also a pseudo-core of
(\mathcal{E}^{\prime},\mathcal{F}^{\prime})
by definition of the latter.
Now let
f
,
\left(f_{n}\right)_{n}
, and
\left(g_{n}\right)_{n}
be as in (
4.19
).
Since
\Gamma^{\prime}\equiv\Gamma
by (
4.14
), it is not difficult to show that (
4.19
) holds as well with
\Gamma^{\prime}
in place of
\Gamma
and
\mathsf{m}^{\prime}
in place of
\mathsf{m}
.

Respectively by definition of
\mathbb{L}^{\mathsf{m},\tau}_{b}
, construction of
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
, and by
[
4
, Thm. 12.5]
, we have
\mathbb{L}^{\mathsf{m},\tau}_{b}\subset\mathcal{F}\subset\mathcal{F}^{\prime}\subset\mathscr{D}({\mathsf{Ch}_{*,\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}},\mathsf{m}^{\prime}}})
.
Respectively by (
4.14
),
[
4
, Thm. 12.5]
, (
4.15
) and
\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}}=\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}^{\prime}}
, we see that

\Gamma(f)=\Gamma^{\prime}(f)\geq\left\lvert\mathrm{D}f\right\rvert_{w,\,\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}^{\prime}},\mathsf{m}^{\prime}}^{2}=\left\lvert\mathrm{D}f\right\rvert_{*,\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}},\mathsf{m}^{\prime}}^{2}\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,\qquad f\in\mathbb{L}^{\mathsf{m},\tau}_{b}\,\,\mathrm{.}

Applying Lemma
4.7
while exchanging the roles of
\mathsf{m}
and
\mathsf{m}^{\prime}
with
\theta^{-1}
in place of
\theta
we conclude from the above inequality that, in fact,
\mathbb{L}^{\mathsf{m},\tau}_{b}\subset\mathscr{D}({\mathsf{Ch}_{*,\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}},\mathsf{m}}})
.
Thus, continuing the above chain of inequalities, we conclude by (
4.21
), and again by (
4.15
), that

\Gamma(f)\geq\left\lvert\mathrm{D}f\right\rvert_{*,\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}},\mathsf{m}^{\prime}}^{2}=\left\lvert\mathrm{D}f\right\rvert_{*,\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}},\mathsf{m}}^{2}=\left\lvert\mathrm{D}f\right\rvert_{w,\,\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}},\mathsf{m}}^{2}\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,\qquad f\in\mathbb{L}^{\mathsf{m},\tau}_{b}\,\,\mathrm{.}

This last chain of inequalities extends to
\mathbb{L}^{\mathsf{m},\tau}_{\mathrm{loc},b}
by locality of all the objects involved, and the conclusion follows.
∎

Combining Propositions
4.8
and
4.12
we further obtain the following identification of
\mathcal{E}
with
\mathsf{Ch}_{w,\mathsf{d},\mathsf{m}}
(or, equivalently, with
\mathsf{Ch}_{*,\mathsf{d},\mathsf{m}}
).

Corollary 4.14
.

Let
(\mathbb{X},\mathcal{E})
be a quasi-regular strongly local Dirichlet space with
\mathbb{X}
satisfying
2
and admitting carré du champ operator
\Gamma
, and
\mathsf{d}\colon X^{\scriptscriptstyle{\times 2}}\to[0,\infty]
be an extended distance.
Further assume that
(\mathsf{Rad}_{\mathsf{d},\mathsf{m}})
and
(\mathsf{cSL}_{\tau,\mathsf{m},\mathsf{d}})
hold.
Then,
\mathcal{E}\leq\mathsf{Ch}_{w,\mathsf{d},\mathsf{m}}
, and the equality holds if and only if
(\mathbb{X},\mathcal{E})
is additionally
\tau
-upper regular.

Remark 4.15

(Comparison with
[
8
,
4
]
)
.

Corollary
4.14
ought to be compared with
[
8
, Thm. 3.14]
and
[
4
, Thm. 12.5]
.
Let us first note that the two approaches have different premises: in
[
8
,
4
]
the authors compare the form
(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
with the Cheeger energy
\mathsf{Ch}_{w,\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}},\mathsf{m}}
constructed from (the intrinsic distance
\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}}
of)
(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
.
We rather compare
(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
and
\mathsf{Ch}_{w,\mathsf{d},\mathsf{m}}
for some
assigned

\mathsf{d}
,
a priori
unrelated to
\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}}
.

Apart from the point of view, a main difference among the results in
[
8
]
, those in
[
4
]
, and ours lies in the generality of the respective assumptions, as we now detail.

Comparison with
[
8
]

Under the standing assumption in
[
8
]
:

(1)

\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}}
is a finite distance metrizing
\tau
and
(X,\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}})
is separable and complete;

(2)

(\mathbb{X},\mathcal{E},\mathsf{m})
satisfies (a slightly stronger version of)
(\mathsf{Loc}_{\mathsf{m},\tau})
(see
[
8
, Eqn. (3.28)]
);

it is in fact possible to
prove

(\mathsf{Rad}_{\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}},\mathsf{m}})
—see
[
8
, Thm. 3.9]
or (in a more general setting than in
[
8
]
)
[
18
, Cor. 3.15]
—, while
(\mathsf{cSL}_{\tau,\mathsf{m},\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}}})
holds by definition of
\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}}
(cf. the proof of
[
8
, Thm. 3.9]
).
Furthermore, thanks to the Rademacher property
(\mathsf{Rad}_{\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}},\mathsf{m}})
and to
1
above, the form
(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
is quasi-regular by
[
18
, Prop. 3.21]
.
Thus, our assumptions are weaker (and our result stronger) than those in
[
8
]
.

Comparison with
[
4
]

It is (part of) the assumptions in
[
4
]
that

(1)

(X,\tau,\mathsf{d})
is a completely regular Hausdorff
extended
metric-topological space;

(2)

\mathsf{m}
is a probability measure and —roughly—
\mathbb{L}^{\mathsf{m},\tau}_{\mathrm{loc},b}
generates
\tau
.

Again, these assumptions trivially imply
(\mathsf{cSL}_{\tau,\mathsf{m},\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}}})
.
They are however skew to ours in the level of generality of the spaces involved.
Indeed, whereas we always assume that
(X,\tau)
satisfies
2
(which is less restrictive than
[
8
]
but more restrictive than
[
4
]
), we are able to address the case of
extended
distances (as in
[
4
]
) on
\sigma
-finite spaces (as in
[
8
]
, and as opposed to
[
4
]
, addressing only probability spaces).

Proof of Corollary
4.14
.

It suffices to verify the assumptions in Propositions
4.8
and
4.12
.
∎

Finally, let us now comment on the last equality in (
4.18
), viz.

(4.22)

\left\lvert\mathrm{D}f\right\rvert_{w,\,\mathsf{d}}^{2}=\left\lvert\mathrm{D}f\right\rvert_{\mathsf{d}}^{2}\quad\quad\mathsf{m}\text{-a.e.}\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,\qquad f\in\mathbb{L}^{\mathsf{m},\tau}_{\mathrm{loc},b}\,\,\mathrm{.}

Remark 4.16

(On the validity of (
4.22
))
.

When
\mathsf{d}
is a distance (as opposed to: an extended distance), the equality (
4.22
) is known to hold for all
\mathsf{d}
-Lipschitz functions under some assumptions on the metric measure space
(X,\mathsf{d},\mathsf{m})
.
In particular, it is satisfied whenever
(X,\mathsf{d},\mathsf{m})
is a (measure) doubling metric measure space (see Dfn.
5.1
below) supporting a weak
(1,2)
-Poincaré inequality (see Dfn.
5.17
below).

5.
Tensorization

In this section, we will mostly confine ourselves to
metric measure spaces
in the sense of the following definition.

Definition 5.1

(Metric measure space)
.

A triple
(X,\mathsf{d},\mathsf{m})
is called a
metric measure space
if
(X,\mathsf{d})
is a complete and separable metric space, and
\mathsf{m}
is a Borel measure on
X
finite on
\mathsf{d}
-bounded sets.

For the purpose of comparison with known results in the literature, we recall the definition of infinitesimal Hilbertianity, introduced by N. Gigli in
[
26
]
.

Definition 5.2

(Infinitesimal Hilbertianity)
.

A metric measure space
(X,\mathsf{d},\mathsf{m})
is
infinitesimally Hilbertian
if the Cheeger energy
\mathsf{Ch}_{*,\mathsf{d},\mathsf{m}}
satisfies the parallelogram identity on
L^{2}(\mathsf{m})
, viz.

(
\mathsf{IH}_{\mathsf{d},\mathsf{m}}
)

\displaystyle 2\big({\mathsf{Ch}_{*,\mathsf{d},\mathsf{m}}(f)+\mathsf{Ch}_{*,\mathsf{d},\mathsf{m}}(g)}\big)=\mathsf{Ch}_{*,\mathsf{d},\mathsf{m}}(f+g)+\mathsf{Ch}_{*,\mathsf{d},\mathsf{m}}(f-g)\,\,\mathrm{.}

In the case when
(X,\mathsf{d},\mathsf{m})
is infinitesimal Hilbertian, the Cheeger energy
\mathsf{Ch}_{*,\mathsf{d},\mathsf{m}}
is a quadratic functional.
The non-relabelled bilinear form induced on
L^{2}(\mathsf{m})
by polarization is in fact a local Dirichlet form.

5.1.
Product structures

Let
(\mathbb{X},\mathcal{E})
and
(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathcal{E}^{\prime})
be Dirichlet spaces.
Further let

\mathbb{X}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}=(X^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}},\tau^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}},\Sigma^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}},\mathsf{m}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}})\eqdef(X\times X^{\prime},\tau\times\tau^{\prime},\Sigma\widehat{\otimes}\Sigma^{\prime},\mathsf{m}\widehat{\otimes}\mathsf{m}^{\prime})

be the corresponding product space.
For a function
\hat{f}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}\colon X^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}\to{\mathbb{R}}
and any
\mathbf{x}\eqdef(x,x^{\prime})\in X^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}
, define the sections of
\hat{f}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}
at
\mathbf{x}
by
\hat{f}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}_{\mathbf{x},1}\colon y\mapsto\hat{f}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}(y,x^{\prime})
and
\hat{f}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}_{\mathbf{x},2}\colon y^{\prime}\mapsto\hat{f}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}(x,y^{\prime})
.
Since no confusion may arise, we also write
f^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}\eqdef\big[\hat{f}\big]_{\mathsf{m}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}}
,
f^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}_{\mathbf{x},1}\eqdef\big[\hat{f}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}_{\mathbf{x},1}\big]_{\mathsf{m}}
and
f^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}_{\mathbf{x},2}\eqdef\big[\hat{f}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}_{\mathbf{x},2}\big]_{\mathsf{m}^{\prime}}
.
We stress that the subscript number indicates the
free
coordinate.

5.1.1.
Products of Dirichlet spaces

Set

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}\eqdef\left\{f\in L^{2}(\mathsf{m}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}):\begin{gathered}f^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}_{\mathbf{x},1}\in\mathcal{F}\ {\textrm{\,for ${\mathsf{m}^{\prime}}$-a.e.\,}}x^{\prime}\in X^{\prime}\\
f^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}_{\mathbf{x},2}\in\mathcal{F}^{\prime}\ {\textrm{\,for ${\mathsf{m}}$-a.e.\,}}x\in X\\
\mathcal{E}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}(f^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}})\eqdef\int\mathcal{E}(f^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}_{\mathbf{x},1})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mathsf{m}^{\prime}\ +\int\mathcal{E}^{\prime}(f^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}_{\mathbf{x},2})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mathsf{m}<\infty\end{gathered}\right\}\,\,\mathrm{.}

Proposition 5.3

(Product structures)
.

The following assertions hold:

(1)

If both
\mathbb{X}
and
\mathbb{X}^{\prime}
satisfy either
1
,
2
, or
3
, then so does
\mathbb{X}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}
;

(2)

the quadratic form
(\mathcal{E}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}},\mathcal{D}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}})
is closable, and its closure is a Dirichlet form on
\mathbb{X}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}
with domain

(5.1)

\mathcal{F}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}\eqdef\left\{f\in L^{2}(\mathsf{m}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}):\begin{gathered}x^{\prime}\mapsto f^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}_{\mathbf{x},1}\in L^{2}(\mathsf{m}^{\prime};\mathcal{F})\\
x\mapsto f^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}_{\mathbf{x},2}\in L^{2}(\mathsf{m};\mathcal{F}^{\prime})\end{gathered}\right\}\,\,\mathrm{;}\;\,

(3)

if both
(\mathbb{X},\mathcal{E})
and
(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathcal{E}^{\prime})
are strongly local, then so is
(\mathbb{X}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}},\mathcal{E}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}})
;

(4)

the algebraic tensor product
\mathcal{F}\otimes_{{\mathbb{R}}}\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
is
\mathcal{F}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}
-dense in
\mathcal{F}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}
.

(5)

if both
(\mathbb{X},\mathcal{E})
and
(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathcal{E}^{\prime})
are either quasi-regular or regular, then so is
(\mathbb{X}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}},\mathcal{E}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}})
;

(6)

if
(\mathbb{X},\mathcal{E})
, resp.
(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathcal{E}^{\prime})
, admits carré du champ operator
\Gamma
, resp.
\Gamma^{\prime}
, then
(\mathbb{X}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}},\mathcal{E}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}})
admits carré du champ operator

(5.2)

\Gamma^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}(f^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}})(x,x^{\prime})\eqdef\Gamma(f^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}_{\mathbf{x},1})(x)+\Gamma^{\prime}(f^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}_{\mathbf{x},2})(x^{\prime})\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,\qquad\mathbf{x}\eqdef(x,x^{\prime})\,\,\mathrm{.}

Proof.

A proof of
1
is standard.
Proofs of
2
,
3
, and
6
are found in
[
11
, Prop. V.2.1.2, p. 201]
.
A proof of
4
is found in
[
11
, Prop. V.2.1.3(b), p. 201]
.
A proof of
5
in the regular case follows from
4
.
The quasi-regular case follows from the regular case via the transfer method, cf. e.g.
[
31
,
13
]
.
∎

Combining (
5.2
) with Corollary
2.8
, we see that (
5.2
) extends to
f\in{{\mathcal{F}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}}^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{loc}}}
.

Corollary 5.4
.

Let
(\mathbb{X},\mathcal{E})
and
(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathcal{E}^{\prime})
be quasi-regular strongly local Dirichlet spaces with
\mathbb{X},\mathbb{X}^{\prime}
satisfying
2
.
Then,

(5.3)

\Gamma^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}(f^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}})(x,x^{\prime})\eqdef\Gamma(f^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}_{\mathbf{x},1})(x)+\Gamma^{\prime}(f^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}_{\mathbf{x},2})(x^{\prime})\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,\qquad\mathbf{x}\eqdef(x,x^{\prime})\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,\qquad f\in{{\mathcal{F}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}}^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{loc}}}\,\,\mathrm{.}

As a consequence, we further have

\displaystyle f\otimes\car\in

\displaystyle\ \mathbb{L}^{\mathsf{m}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}}_{\mathrm{loc},b}\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,\car\otimes f^{\prime}\in\mathbb{L}^{\mathsf{m}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}}_{\mathrm{loc},b}\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,

\displaystyle f\in

\displaystyle\ \mathbb{L}^{\mathsf{m}}_{\mathrm{loc},b}\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,f^{\prime}\in\mathbb{L}^{\mathsf{m}^{\prime}}_{\mathrm{loc},b}\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,

(5.4)

\displaystyle f\otimes\car\in

\displaystyle\ \mathbb{L}^{\mathsf{m}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}},\tau^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}}_{\mathrm{loc},b}\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,\car\otimes f^{\prime}\in\mathbb{L}^{\mathsf{m}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}},\tau^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}}_{\mathrm{loc},b}\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,

\displaystyle f\in

\displaystyle\ \mathbb{L}^{\mathsf{m},\tau}_{\mathrm{loc},b}\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,f^{\prime}\in\mathbb{L}^{\mathsf{m}^{\prime},\tau^{\prime}}_{\mathrm{loc},b}\,\,\mathrm{.}

Sectioning

Denote the sections of a set
A^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}\subset X^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}
by

A^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}_{\mathbf{x},1}\eqdef\left\{x\in X:(x,x^{\prime})\in A^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}\right\}\quad\text{and}\quad A^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}_{\mathbf{x},2}\eqdef\left\{x^{\prime}\in X^{\prime}:(x,x^{\prime})\in A^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}\right\}\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,\qquad\mathbf{x}\eqdef(x,x,^{\prime})\in X^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}\,\,\mathrm{.}

Lemma 5.5

(Sectioning of quasi-notions)
.

Let
(\mathbb{X},\mathcal{E})
and
(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathcal{E}^{\prime})
be quasi-regular strongly local Dirichlet spaces with
\mathbb{X},\mathbb{X}^{\prime}
satisfying
2
.
Then, the following assertions hold:

(1)

if
\left(F^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}_{k}\right)_{k}
is an
\mathcal{E}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}
-nest, then
{\big((F^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}_{k})_{\mathbf{x},1}\big)}_{k}
is an
\mathcal{E}
-nest for
\mathsf{m}^{\prime}
-a.e.
x^{\prime}\in X^{\prime}
, resp.
{\big((F^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}_{k})_{\mathbf{x},2}\big)}_{k}
is an
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
-nest for
\mathsf{m}
-a.e.
x\in X
;

(2)

if
P^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}\subset X^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}
is
\mathcal{E}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}
-polar, then
P^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}_{\mathbf{x},1}
is
\mathcal{E}
-polar for
\mathsf{m}^{\prime}
-a.e.
x^{\prime}\in X^{\prime}
, resp.
P^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}_{\mathbf{x},2}
is
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
-polar for
\mathsf{m}
-a.e.
x\in X
;

(3)

if
G^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}\subset X^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}
is
\mathcal{E}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}
-quasi-open, then
G^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}_{\mathbf{x},1}
is
\mathcal{E}
-quasi-open for
\mathsf{m}^{\prime}
-a.e.
x^{\prime}\in X^{\prime}
, resp.
G^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}_{\mathbf{x},2}
is
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
-quasi-open for
\mathsf{m}
-a.e.
x\in X
;

(4)

if
f^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}
is
\mathcal{E}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}
-quasi-continuous, then
f^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}_{\mathbf{x},1}
is
\mathcal{E}
-quasi-open for
\mathsf{m}^{\prime}
-a.e.
x^{\prime}\in X^{\prime}
, resp.
f^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}_{\mathbf{x},2}
is
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
-quasi-open for
\mathsf{m}
-a.e.
x\in X
.

Proof.

We only show
1
, the other assertions being a straightforward consequence.
Let
\left(F^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}_{k}\right)_{k}
be an
\mathcal{E}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}
-nest. For each
x^{\prime}\in X
and for each
k\in{\mathbb{N}}
let
F_{k}\eqdef\left\{x\in X:(x,x^{\prime})\in F^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}_{k}\right\}
be the
x^{\prime}
-section of
F^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}_{k}
.
We show that
\left(F_{k}\right)_{k}
is an
\mathcal{E}
-nest for
\mathsf{m}^{\prime}
-a.e.
x^{\prime}\in X^{\prime}
.
Since
F^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}_{k}
is
\tau^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}
-closed and sectioning preserves closedness,
F_{k}
is
\tau
-closed for every
k
.
It suffices to show that
\cap_{k}F_{k}^{\mathrm{c}}
is
\mathcal{E}
-polar.
When
\mathbb{X}
and
\mathbb{X}^{\prime}
are probability spaces, this is claimed in, e.g.,
[
11
, Exercise V.2.1(2), p. 208]
.

In order to address the case of
\sigma
-finite
\mathsf{m}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}
, let
\varphi\in\mathcal{F}
, resp.
\varphi^{\prime}\in\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
, with
\varphi>0

\mathsf{m}
-a.e., resp.
\varphi^{\prime}>0

\mathsf{m}^{\prime}
-a.e., and set
\varphi^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}\eqdef\varphi\otimes\varphi^{\prime}\in\mathcal{F}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}
.
Consider the Girsanov transforms
(\mathcal{E}^{\varphi},\mathcal{F}^{\varphi})
of
(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
,
(\mathcal{E}^{\varphi^{\prime}},\mathcal{F}^{\varphi^{\prime}})
of
(\mathcal{E}^{\prime},\mathcal{F}^{\prime})
, and
(\mathcal{E}^{\varphi^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}},\mathcal{F}^{\varphi^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}})
of
(\mathcal{E}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}},\mathcal{F}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}})
.
The assertion follows since Girsanov transforms by quasi-everywhere strictly positive functions preserve nests, hence polarity; see e.g.
[
12
, p. 449]
.
∎

Corollary 5.6

(Sectioning of broad local domains)
.

Let
(\mathbb{X},\mathcal{E})
and
(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathcal{E}^{\prime})
be quasi-regular strongly local Dirichlet spaces with
\mathbb{X},\mathbb{X}^{\prime}
satisfying
2
.
If
f^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}\in{{\mathcal{F}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}}^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{loc}}}
, then
f^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}_{\mathbf{x},1}\in{{\mathcal{F}}^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{loc}}}
for
\mathsf{m}^{\prime}
-a.e.
x^{\prime}\in X
and
f^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}_{\mathbf{x},2}\in{{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}}^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{loc}}}
for
\mathsf{m}
-a.e.
x\in X
.

Proof.

Let
(G^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}_{\bullet},f^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}_{\bullet})
be witnessing that
f^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}\in{{\mathcal{F}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}}^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{loc}}}
.
We show the statement for
X
-sections, the one for
X^{\prime}
-sections being analogous.
It suffices to note that, for
\mathsf{m}^{\prime}
-a.e.
x^{\prime}\in X^{\prime}
,

(1)

(G^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}_{n})_{\mathbf{x},1}
is
\mathcal{E}
-quasi-open by Lemma
5.5
3

(2)

(f^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}_{n})_{\mathbf{x},1}\in\mathcal{F}
by definition (
5.1
) of
\mathcal{F}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}
;

(3)

(f^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}_{n})_{\mathbf{x},1}\equiv f^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}_{\mathbf{x},1}
\mathsf{m}
-a.e. on
(G^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}_{n})_{\mathbf{x},1}
by definition
f^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}_{\bullet}
.

∎

Corollary 5.7

(Sectioning of broad local spaces of uniformly bounded energy)
.

Let
(\mathbb{X},\mathcal{E})
, resp.
(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathcal{E}^{\prime})
, be a quasi-regular strongly local Dirichlet spaces with
\mathbb{X}
, resp.
\mathbb{X}^{\prime}
, satisfying
2
.
If
f^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}\in\mathbb{L}^{\mathsf{m}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}}_{\mathrm{loc},b}
, resp.
\mathbb{L}^{\mathsf{m}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}},\tau^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}}_{\mathrm{loc},b}
, then
f^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}_{\mathbf{x},1}\in\mathbb{L}^{\mathsf{m}}_{\mathrm{loc},b}
, resp.
\mathbb{L}^{\mathsf{m},\tau}_{\mathrm{loc},b}
, for
\mathsf{m}^{\prime}
-a.e.
x^{\prime}\in X
and
f^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}_{\mathbf{x},2}\in\mathbb{L}^{\mathsf{m}^{\prime}}_{\mathrm{loc},b}
, resp.
\mathbb{L}^{\mathsf{m}^{\prime},\tau^{\prime}}_{\mathrm{loc},b}
, for
\mathsf{m}
-a.e.
x\in X
.

Proof.

Straightforward consequence of Corollary
5.6
and (
5.2
).
∎

5.1.2.
Products of metric objects

Let
X,X^{\prime}
be any (non-empty) sets.
For extended pseudo-distances
\mathsf{d}\colon X^{\scriptscriptstyle{\times 2}}\to[0,\infty]
and
\mathsf{d}^{\prime}\colon X^{\prime{\scriptscriptstyle{\times 2}}}\to[0,\infty]
, we denote by

\mathsf{d}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}\big({(x,x^{\prime}),(y,y^{\prime})}\big)=(\mathsf{d}\otimes\mathsf{d}^{\prime})\big({(x,x^{\prime}),(y,y^{\prime})}\big)\eqdef\sqrt{\mathsf{d}(x,y)^{2}+\mathsf{d}^{\prime}(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})^{2}}

the (
\ell^{2}
-)product extended pseudo-distance on
X\times X^{\prime}
, and by

(\mathsf{d}\oplus\mathsf{d}^{\prime})\big({(x,x^{\prime}),(y,y^{\prime})}\big)\eqdef\mathsf{d}(x,y)+\mathsf{d}^{\prime}(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})

the (
\ell^{1}
-)product extended pseudo-distance on
X\times X^{\prime}
.
Note that
\mathsf{d}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}
and
\mathsf{d}\oplus\mathsf{d}^{\prime}
induce the same topology on
X\times X^{\prime}
.

Lemma 5.8
.

Let
(\mathbb{X},\mathcal{E})
, resp.
(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathcal{E}^{\prime})
, be a quasi-regular strongly local Dirichlet spaces with
\mathbb{X}
, resp.
\mathbb{X}^{\prime}
, satisfying
2
.
Then,

(5.5)

\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}}\leq\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}}\oplus\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}^{\prime}}\quad\mathsf{m}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}\text{-a.e.}\,\,\mathrm{.}

Proof.

Set
\mathbf{x}\eqdef(x,x^{\prime})
and
\mathbf{y}\eqdef(y,y^{\prime})
. Then,

\displaystyle\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}}\big({(x,x^{\prime}),(y,y^{\prime})}\big)\leq

\displaystyle\ \mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}}\big({(x,x^{\prime}),(y,x^{\prime})}\big)+\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}}\big({(y,x^{\prime}),(y,y^{\prime})}\big)

(5.6)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \inf_{f^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}\in\mathbb{L}^{\mathsf{m}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}},\tau^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}}_{\mathrm{loc},b}}f^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}(x,x^{\prime})-f^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}(y,x^{\prime})+\inf_{g^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}\in\mathbb{L}^{\mathsf{m}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}},\tau^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}}_{\mathrm{loc},b}}g^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}(y,x^{\prime})-g^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}(y,y^{\prime})\,\,\mathrm{.}

Choosing
f^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}
of the form
f\otimes\car
, resp.
\car\otimes f^{\prime}
in the first, resp. second, summand of (
5.6
), we may continue (
5.6
) with

\displaystyle\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}}\big({(x,x^{\prime}),(y,y^{\prime})}\big)\leq

\displaystyle\ \inf_{\begin{subarray}{c}\hat{f}\colon X\to{\mathbb{R}}\\
