f\otimes\car\in\mathbb{L}^{\mathsf{m}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}},\tau^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}}_{\mathrm{loc},b}\end{subarray}}\hat{f}(x)-\hat{f}(y)+\inf_{\begin{subarray}{c}\hat{g}\colon X^{\prime}\to{\mathbb{R}}\\
\car\otimes g\in\mathbb{L}^{\mathsf{m}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}},\tau^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}}_{\mathrm{loc},b}\end{subarray}}\hat{g}(x^{\prime})-\hat{g}(y^{\prime})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\ \inf_{f\in\mathbb{L}^{\mathsf{m},\tau}_{\mathrm{loc},b}}f(x)-f(y)+\inf_{f^{\prime}\in\mathbb{L}^{\mathsf{m}^{\prime},\tau^{\prime}}_{\mathrm{loc},b}}g(x^{\prime})-g(y^{\prime})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}}(x,y)+\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}^{\prime}}(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,

where the second inequality holds in light of (
5.4
).
∎

Now, let
(X,\tau,\mathsf{d},\mathsf{m})
and
(X^{\prime},\tau^{\prime},\mathsf{d}^{\prime},\mathsf{m}^{\prime})
be extended metric-topological measure spaces in the sense of Definition
2.3
.
We denote by

\displaystyle\left\lvert\mathrm{D}f^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}\right\rvert_{w,\,\mathsf{d},\mathsf{d}^{\prime}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}}(x,x^{\prime})\eqdef\sqrt{\left\lvert\mathrm{D}f^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}_{\mathbf{x},1}\right\rvert_{w,\,\mathsf{d}}^{2}(x)+\left\lvert\mathrm{D}f^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}_{\mathbf{x},2}\right\rvert_{w,\,\mathsf{d}^{\prime}}^{2}(x^{\prime})}\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,\qquad f^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}\colon X^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}\to{\mathbb{R}}\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,

the
Cartesian gradient
in
[
7
, p. 1477]
, by

(5.7)

\displaystyle\left\lvert\mathrm{D}f^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}\right\rvert_{c,\mathsf{d},\mathsf{d}^{\prime}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}}(x,x^{\prime})\eqdef\sqrt{\left\lvert\mathrm{D}f^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}_{\mathbf{x},1}\right\rvert_{\mathsf{d}}^{2}(x)+\left\lvert\mathrm{D}f^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}_{\mathbf{x},2}\right\rvert_{\mathsf{d}^{\prime}}^{2}(x^{\prime})}\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,\qquad f^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}\colon X^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}\to{\mathbb{R}}\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,

the
Cartesian slope
in
[
9
, Eqn. (3.3)]
, and by
\left\lvert\mathrm{D}f^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}\right\rvert_{*,c,\mathsf{d},\mathsf{d}^{\prime}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}}
the minimal relaxed gradient associated to the Cheeger energy

\displaystyle\mathsf{Ch}_{*,c,\mathsf{m}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}}(f^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}})\eqdef\inf\left\{\liminf_{n}\int_{X^{\scriptscriptstyle{\times 2}}}\big({\left\lvert\mathrm{D}f^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}_{n}\right\rvert_{c,\mathsf{d},\mathsf{d}^{\prime}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}}}\big)^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mathsf{m}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}\right\}=\int_{X^{\scriptscriptstyle{\times 2}}}\big({\left\lvert\mathrm{D}f^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}\right\rvert_{*,c,\mathsf{d},\mathsf{d}^{\prime}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}}}\big)^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mathsf{m}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,

where the infimum is taken over all sequences
\left(f^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}_{n}\right)_{n}\subset\mathrm{Lip}(\mathsf{d}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}})
with
L^{2}(\mathsf{m}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}})\text{-}\lim_{n}f_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}=f^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}
.

Recall that, by e.g.
[
9
, Thm. 2.2(ii)]
, for every
f^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}\in\mathrm{Lip}_{bs}(\mathsf{d}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}})\subset L^{2}(\mathsf{m}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}})
there exists a sequence of functions
\left(f^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}_{n}\right)_{n}\subset\mathrm{Lip}_{bs}(\mathsf{d}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}})
such that

(5.8)

\displaystyle L^{2}(\mathsf{m}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}})\text{-}\lim_{n}f^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}_{n}=f^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\left\lvert\mathrm{D}f^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}\right\rvert_{*,c,\mathsf{d},\mathsf{d}^{\prime}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}}=L^{2}(\mathsf{m}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}})\text{-}\lim_{n}\left\lvert\mathrm{D}f^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}_{n}\right\rvert_{c,\mathsf{d},\mathsf{d}^{\prime}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}}\,\,\mathrm{.}

5.2.
Tensorization of the Rademacher property

In this section we show the tensorization of the Rademacher property.
In the literature, it has been addressed in the case when
\mathcal{E}=\mathsf{Ch}_{\mathsf{d},\mathsf{m}}
is the Cheeger energy, in connection with the tensorization of the Cheeger energy under the assumptions of

•

infinitesimal Hilbertianity (Dfn.
5.2
), measure doubling and a weak
(1,2)
-Poincaré inequality (see Dfn.
5.17
below) in
[
9
]
;

•

under the Riemannian Curvature-Dimension condition
\mathsf{RCD}(K,\infty)
in
[
7
]
;

•

more recently under the
infinitesimal quasi-Hilbertianity
in
[
22
]
.

Here, we discuss the case of general quasi-regular strongly local Dirichlet spaces
(\mathbb{X},\mathcal{E})
, without any geometric assumption.
In particular, we never require a two-sided comparison of
(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
with
\mathsf{Ch}_{*,\mathsf{d},\mathsf{m}}
implicit in the definition of infinitesimal (quasi-)Hilbertianity in
[
22
]
.

Similarly to the discussion on transfer by weight in §
4
, our assumptions are weaker than those in
[
7
,
9
,
22
]
, since the Cheeger energy
\mathsf{Ch}_{*,\mathsf{d},\mathsf{m}}
of a metric measure space is always quasi-regular by
[
36
, Thm. 4.1]
or
[
18
, Prop. 3.21]
, cf. Remark
4.15
.

Theorem 5.9

(Tensorization of
(\mathsf{Rad})
)
.

Let
\mathbb{X}\eqdef(X,\mathsf{d},\mathsf{m})
be a metric measure space, and
(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
be a quasi-regular strongly local Dirichlet form on
\mathbb{X}
admitting carré du champ operator
\Gamma
and satisfying
(\mathsf{Rad}_{\mathsf{d},\mathsf{m}})
.
Further let
(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathcal{E}^{\prime})
be satisfying the same assumptions as
(\mathbb{X},\mathcal{E})
.

Then, their product space
(\mathbb{X}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}},\mathcal{E}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}})
satisfies
(\mathsf{Rad}_{\mathsf{d}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}},\mathsf{m}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}})
.

Proof.

Since
(X,\mathsf{d},\mathsf{m})
and
(X^{\prime},\mathsf{d}^{\prime},\mathsf{m}^{\prime})
are metric measure spaces in the sense of Definition
5.1
, their product space
(X^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}},\mathsf{d}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}},\mathsf{m}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}})
is so as well.
Since
\tau_{\mathsf{d}}=\tau
, all Lipschitz functions in this proof are continuous (in particular: Borel) for the relative distances/topologies, and we thus omit the specification of measure-representatives of such functions.

Let
\left(G_{k}\right)_{k}
, resp.
\left(G_{k}^{\prime}\right)_{k}
, be a monotone exhaustion of
X
, resp.
X^{\prime}
consisting of well-separated (see Rmk.
4.3
) bounded open sets.
Set
G_{k}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}\eqdef G_{k}\times G_{k}^{\prime}
, and note that
G_{k}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}
is a monotone exhaustion of
X^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}
consisting of well-separated bounded open sets.
Similarly to Remark
4.3
, for each
k
there exists
\varrho_{k}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}\in\mathrm{Lip}_{b}(\mathsf{d}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}})
satisfying condition
1b
in Proposition
4.2
, that is such that
\car_{G_{k}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}}\leq\varrho_{k}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}\leq\car_{{G_{k+1}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}}^{c}}
and

\mathrm{L}_{\mathsf{d}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}}(\varrho_{k}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}})\leq c_{k}\eqdef\mathsf{d}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}(G_{k}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}},{G_{k+1}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}}^{\mathrm{c}})^{-1}<\infty\,\,\mathrm{.}

Throughout the proof, let
\mathbf{x}\eqdef(x,x^{\prime})\in X^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}
.

Step I:
\mathrm{Lip}_{b}(\mathsf{d}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}})\subset\mathcal{F}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}

Without loss of generality, we show that
\mathrm{Lip}^{1}_{b}(\mathsf{d}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}})\subset\mathcal{F}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}
.
Let
f^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}\in\mathrm{Lip}^{1}_{b}(\mathsf{d}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}})
and set
M\eqdef\sup_{X^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}}\left\lvert f^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}\right\rvert
.
Note that, for each fixed
\mathbf{x}\eqdef(x,x^{\prime})\in X^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}
,

(5.9)

\displaystyle f_{k,\mathbf{x},1}\eqdef

\displaystyle(f^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}\varrho_{k}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}})_{\mathbf{x},1}\in\mathrm{Lip}_{b}(\mathsf{d})\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,\quad\mathrm{L}_{\mathsf{d}}(f_{\mathbf{x},k,1})\leq Mc_{k}+1\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,

\displaystyle f_{k,\mathbf{x},2}\eqdef

\displaystyle(f^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}\varrho_{k}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}})_{\mathbf{x},2}\in\mathrm{Lip}_{b}(\mathsf{d}^{\prime})\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,\quad\mathrm{L}_{\mathsf{d}^{\prime}}(f_{\mathbf{x},k,2})\leq Mc_{k}+1\,\,\mathrm{.}

By
(\mathsf{Rad}_{\mathsf{d},\mathsf{m}})
, resp.
(\mathsf{Rad}_{\mathsf{d}^{\prime},\mathsf{m}^{\prime}})
, we have

(5.10)

\displaystyle f_{k,\mathbf{x},1}\in

\displaystyle{{\displaystyle\mathcal{F}}^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{loc}}}\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,

\displaystyle\Gamma(f_{k,\mathbf{x},1})\leq

\displaystyle\mathrm{L}_{\mathsf{d}}(f_{k,\mathbf{x},1})^{2}\quad\mathsf{m}\text{-a.e.}\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,

\displaystyle f_{k,\mathbf{x},2}\in

\displaystyle{{\displaystyle\mathcal{F}^{\prime}}^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{loc}}}\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,

\displaystyle\Gamma^{\prime}(f_{k,\mathbf{x},2})\leq

\displaystyle\mathrm{L}_{\mathsf{d}^{\prime}}(f_{k,\mathbf{x},2})^{2}\quad\mathsf{m}^{\prime}\text{-a.e.}\,\,\mathrm{.}

Furthermore, by definition of
\varrho_{k}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}
we have
f_{k,\mathbf{x},1}\equiv 0
everywhere on
G_{k+1}^{\mathrm{c}}
, resp.
f_{k,\mathbf{x},2}\equiv 0
everywhere on
{G_{k+1}^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{c}}
.
Combing this fact with (
5.9
) and (
5.10
), we conclude from Corollary
2.8
that

(5.11)

\displaystyle\Gamma(f_{k,\mathbf{x},1})\leq

\displaystyle\begin{cases}(Mc_{k}+1)^{2}&\quad\mathsf{m}\text{-a.e.}\ \text{on }G_{k+1}\\
0&\quad\mathsf{m}\text{-a.e.}\ \text{on }{G_{k+1}}^{\mathrm{c}}\end{cases}\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,

\displaystyle\Gamma^{\prime}(f_{k,\mathbf{x},2})\leq

\displaystyle\begin{cases}(Mc_{k}+1)^{2}&\quad\mathsf{m}^{\prime}\text{-a.e.}\ \text{on }G^{\prime}_{k+1}\\
0&\quad\mathsf{m}^{\prime}\text{-a.e.}\ \text{on }{G^{\prime}_{k+1}}^{\mathrm{c}}\end{cases}\,\,\mathrm{.}

Thus, since
G_{k}
, resp.
G_{k}^{\prime}
, has finite
\mathsf{m}
-, resp.
\mathsf{m}^{\prime}
-measure for all
k
, (
5.11
) implies that, for each
k
and every
\mathbf{x}\in X^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}
,

(5.12)

\displaystyle f_{k,\mathbf{x},1}\in

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,

\displaystyle\left\lVert f_{k,\mathbf{x},1}\right\rVert_{\mathcal{F}}^{2}\leq

\displaystyle\mathsf{m}G_{k+1}\big({(Mc_{k}+1)^{2}+M^{2}}\big)\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,

\displaystyle f_{k,\mathbf{x},2}\in

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}^{\prime}\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,

\displaystyle\left\lVert f_{k,\mathbf{x},2}\right\rVert_{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}}^{2}\leq

\displaystyle\mathsf{m}^{\prime}G_{k+1}^{\prime}\big({(Mc_{k}+1)^{2}+M^{2}}\big)\,\,\mathrm{.}

As a consequence,

\displaystyle\left\lVert x^{\prime}\mapsto f_{k,\mathbf{x},1}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathsf{m}^{\prime},\mathcal{F})}^{2}\leq\mathsf{m}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}G_{k+1}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}\big({(Mc_{k}+1)^{2}+M^{2}}\big)\,\,\mathrm{,}

\displaystyle\left\lVert x^{\prime}\mapsto f_{k,\mathbf{x},1}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathsf{m}^{\prime},\mathcal{F})}^{2}\leq\mathsf{m}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}G_{k+1}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}\big({(Mc_{k}+1)^{2}+M^{2}}\big)\,\,\mathrm{,}

which finally shows that
f^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}\varrho_{k}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}\in\mathcal{F}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}
for each
k
by definition (
5.1
) of
\mathcal{F}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}
.
Since
\left(G_{k}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}\right)_{k}
is an open exhaustion of
X^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}
, the sequence
\left(G_{k}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}},f^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}\varrho_{k}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}\right)_{k}
witnesses that
f^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}\in{{\mathcal{F}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}}^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{loc}}}
.

Step II: sharp estimate

We now proceed to estimate of
\Gamma^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}({\,\cdot\,})
by
\mathrm{L}_{\mathsf{d}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}}({\,\cdot\,})
.
Fix
f^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}\in\mathrm{Lip}^{1}_{b}(\mathsf{d}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}})
.
Since
f^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}\in{{\mathcal{F}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}}^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{loc}}}
by Step I, by Corollary
2.8
we have

(5.13)

\Gamma^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}(f^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}})(\mathbf{x})=\Gamma^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}(f^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}\varrho_{k}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}})(\mathbf{x})\quad\text{on }G_{k}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}\,\,\mathrm{.}

Thus, it suffices to show that, for each
k\in{\mathbb{N}}
,

(5.14)

\Gamma^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}(f^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}\varrho_{k}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}})\leq 1\quad\mathsf{m}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}\text{-a.e.}\ \text{on }G_{k}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}\,\,\mathrm{.}

Let
k
be fixed.
Since
f^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}\varrho_{k}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}
is
\mathsf{d}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}
-Lipschitz with
\mathsf{d}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}
-bounded support, we can find a sequence
{\big(f^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}_{k,n}\big)}_{n}
satisfying (
5.8
) with
f^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}\varrho_{k}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}
in place of
f^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}
.
By Step I we further have
f^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}_{k,n}\in{{\mathcal{F}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}}^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{loc}}}
for each
n
, thus we may compute
\Gamma^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}(f^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}_{k,n})
pointwise.

By (
5.3
), and applying Proposition
4.8
on
X
and
X^{\prime}
,

\displaystyle\Gamma^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}(f^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}_{k,n})\leq

\displaystyle\ \Gamma\big({(f^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}_{k,n})_{{\,\cdot\,},1}}\big)+\Gamma^{\prime}\big({(f^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}_{k,n})_{{\,\cdot\,},2}}\big)\leq\char 12\relax{\mathrm{D}(f^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}_{k,n})_{{\,\cdot\,},1}}_{w,\,\mathsf{d}}^{2}+\char 12\relax{\mathrm{D}(f^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}_{k,n})_{{\,\cdot\,},2}}_{w,\,\mathsf{d}^{\prime}}^{2}

(5.15)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\ \char 12\relax{\mathrm{D}(f^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}_{k,n})_{{\,\cdot\,},1}}_{\mathsf{d}}^{2}+\char 12\relax{\mathrm{D}(f^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}_{k,n})_{{\,\cdot\,},2}}_{\mathsf{d}^{\prime}}^{2}\eqqcolon\left(\char 12\relax{\mathrm{D}f^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}_{k,n}}_{c,\mathsf{d},\mathsf{d}^{\prime}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}\right)^{2}\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,

where the inequality (
5.15
) holds since the slope of a Lipschitz function is a weak upper gradient.
Since the right-hand side of (
5.15
) converges in
L^{2}(\mathsf{m}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}})
by (
5.8
), integrating (
5.15
) w.r.t.
\mathsf{m}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}
shows that
{\big(f^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}_{k,n}\big)}_{n}
is uniformly bounded in
\mathcal{F}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}
.
Thus, up to possibly choosing a suitable non-relabeled subsequence, we may and will assume with no loss of generality that
{\big(f^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}_{k,n}\big)}_{n}
is
\mathcal{F}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}
-weakly convergent, in which case its
\mathcal{F}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}
-weak limit is
f^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}\varrho^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}_{k}
in light of its
L^{2}(\mathsf{m}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}})
-convergence to
f^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}\varrho^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}_{k}
and
[
33
, Lem. I.2.12]
.

Fix now
h^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}\in{\mathcal{F}_{b}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}}^{+}
. Then, by e.g.
[
10
, Lem. 3.3(ii)]
,
and by (
5.15
),

(5.16)

\displaystyle\int h^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}\,\Gamma^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}(f^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}\varrho_{k}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mathsf{m}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}\leq

\displaystyle\ \liminf_{n}\int h^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}\,\Gamma^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}(f^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}_{k,n})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mathsf{m}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}\leq\liminf_{n}\int h^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}\left(\char 12\relax{\mathrm{D}f^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}_{k,n}}_{c,\mathsf{d},\mathsf{d}^{\prime}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}\right)^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mathsf{m}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}\,\,\mathrm{.}

Since
h^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}\in L^{\infty}(\mathsf{m}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}})
, by (
5.8
) there exists

(5.17)

\displaystyle\lim_{n}\int h^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}\left(\char 12\relax{\mathrm{D}f^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}_{k,n}}_{c,\mathsf{d},\mathsf{d}^{\prime}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}\right)^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mathsf{m}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}=\int h^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}\left(\char 12\relax{\mathrm{D}(f^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}\varrho_{k}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}})}_{*,c,\mathsf{d},\mathsf{d}^{\prime}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}\right)^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mathsf{m}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}\,\,\mathrm{.}

Combining (
5.16
) with (
5.17
) we thus have

\displaystyle\int h^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}\,\Gamma^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}(f^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}\varrho_{k}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mathsf{m}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}\leq

\displaystyle\int h^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}\left(\char 12\relax{\mathrm{D}(f^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}\varrho_{k}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}})}_{*,c,\mathsf{d},\mathsf{d}^{\prime}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}\right)^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mathsf{m}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,

whence, by arbitrariness of
h^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}
, we conclude that

\displaystyle\Gamma^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}(f^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}\varrho_{k}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}})\leq

\displaystyle\ \left(\char 12\relax{\mathrm{D}(f^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}\varrho_{k}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}})}_{*,c,\mathsf{d},\mathsf{d}^{\prime}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}\right)^{2}=\char 12\relax{\mathrm{D}(f^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}\varrho_{k}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}})}_{*,\mathsf{d}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}}^{2}\leq\char 12\relax{\mathrm{D}(f^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}\varrho_{k}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}})}_{\mathsf{d}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}}^{2}\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,

where the equality is shown in
[
9
, Thm. 3.2]
, and the last inequality holds again by definition of the objects involved.
Since
\varrho^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}_{k}\equiv\car
on
G^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}_{k}
, by locality of
\left\lvert\mathrm{D}{\,\cdot\,}\right\rvert_{\mathsf{d}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}}
and locality of
\Gamma^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}
as in Corollary
2.8
, we finally obtain

\displaystyle\Gamma^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}(f^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}\varrho_{k}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}})\car_{G_{k}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}}\leq\left\lvert\mathrm{D}f^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}\right\rvert_{\mathsf{d}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}}\car_{G_{k}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}}\leq\mathrm{L}_{\mathsf{d}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}}(f^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}})\leq 1

by the assumption that
f^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}\in\mathrm{Lip}^{1}_{b}(\mathsf{d}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}})
, which concludes (
5.14
) and therefore the assertion of the theorem.
∎

5.3.
Tensorization of the Sobolev-to-Lipschitz property

In this section we show a slightly weaker form of tensorization of the Sobolev-to-Lipschitz property.
Precisely, we show that the Sobolev-to-Lipschitz property on the factors implies the
continuous
-Sobolev-to-Lipschitz property on their product space.

Theorem 5.10
.

Let
\mathbb{X}\eqdef(X,\mathsf{d},\mathsf{m})
be a metric measure space (Dfn.
5.1
), and
(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
be a quasi-regular strongly local Dirichlet form on
\mathbb{X}
satisfying
(\mathsf{SL}_{\mathsf{m},\mathsf{d}})
.
Further let
(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathcal{E}^{\prime})
be satisfying the same assumptions as
(\mathbb{X},\mathcal{E})
.
Then, their product space
(\mathbb{X}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}},\mathcal{E}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}})
satisfies
(\mathsf{cSL}_{\tau^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}},\mathsf{m}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}},\mathsf{d}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}})
.

Before discussing a proof of Theorem
5.10
, let us explain why both its statement and the proof presented below are not dual to the tensorization of the Rademacher property in Theorem
5.9
.

Indeed, the proof of Theorem
5.9
crucially relies on Proposition
4.8
.
A dual proof for the tensorization of the Sobolev-to-Lipschitz property would then rely on the chain of inequalities in (
4.18
).
Now, the validity of the first inequality in (
4.18
) is implied by —in essence, equivalent to— the
\tau
-upper regularity of the form
(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
, as discussed in (the proof of) Proposition
4.12
.
The validity of the last equality in (
4.18
), that is, (
4.22
), was already discussed for
Lipschitz

f
in Remark
4.16
under further assumptions on the metric measure structure, such as, e.g., measure doubling and a weak
(1,2)
-Poincaré inequality.
Deducing the same equality for
f\in\mathbb{L}^{\mathsf{m},\tau}_{\mathrm{loc},b}
from the equality for
f\in\mathrm{Lip}_{b}(\mathsf{d})
is equivalent to having already shown the continuous-Sobolev-to-Lipschitz property
(\mathsf{cSL}_{\tau,\mathsf{m},\mathsf{d}})
.
As a consequence, it seems difficult to establish the chain of inequalities in (
4.18
) without assuming further structural properties of the product metric measure structure.

5.3.1.
Semigroups, irreducibility, and maximal functions

Let us collect here some definitions and simple facts about them.
Throughout this section, let
(\mathbb{X},\mathcal{E})
and
(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathcal{E}^{\prime})
be any Dirichlet spaces.

Semigroups

We denote by
\left(T_{t}\right)_{t\geq 0}
,
T_{t}\colon L^{2}(\mathsf{m})\to L^{2}(\mathsf{m})
, the (strongly continuous contraction)
L^{2}(\mathsf{m})
-semigroup associated to
(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
.
We denote again by
T_{t}\colon L^{\infty}(\mathsf{m})\to L^{\infty}(\mathsf{m})
the extension of
T_{t}\colon L^{2}(\mathsf{m})\to L^{2}(\mathsf{m})
to a weakly* continuous semigroup on
L^{\infty}(\mathsf{m})
.

We denote by
T_{t}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}
the semigroup(s) associated to
(\mathbb{X}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}},\mathcal{E}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}})
.
We note that
T_{t}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}=T_{t}\otimes T_{t}^{\prime}
, that is, in particular

(5.18)

T_{t}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}(f\otimes f^{\prime})(x,x^{\prime})=(T_{t}f)(x)(T_{t}^{\prime}f^{\prime})(x^{\prime})\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,\qquad t\geq 0\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,\quad f\in L^{\infty}(\mathsf{m})\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,f^{\prime}\in L^{\infty}(\mathsf{m}^{\prime})\,\,\mathrm{.}

Invariance and irreducibility

A set
A\in\Sigma
is
\mathcal{E}
-invariant
if

T_{t}(\car_{A}f)=\car_{A}T_{t}f\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,\qquad f\in L^{2}(\mathsf{m})\,\,\mathrm{.}

For a characterization of
\mathcal{E}
-invariance in the present setting, see e.g.
[
17
, Prop. 2.38]
.
We say that a Dirichlet space
(\mathbb{X},\mathcal{E})
is
irreducible
if every
\mathcal{E}
-invariant
A\in\Sigma
satisfies either
\mathsf{m}A=0
or
\mathsf{m}A^{\mathrm{c}}=0
.

Lemma 5.11
.

Let
(\mathbb{X},\mathcal{E})
be a quasi-regular strongly local Dirichlet form on
\mathbb{X}
, and
\mathsf{d}\colon X^{\scriptscriptstyle{\times 2}}\to[0,\infty)
be a distance (not: extended) on
X
.
If
(\mathbb{X},\mathcal{E})
satisfies
(\mathsf{SL}_{\mathsf{m},\mathsf{d}})
, then it is irreducible.

Proof.

Argue by contradiction that
(\mathbb{X},\mathcal{E})
is
not
irreducible but
(\mathsf{SL}_{\mathsf{m},\mathsf{d}})
holds.
Since
(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
is not irreducible, there exists an
\mathcal{E}
-invariant
A\in\Sigma
with
\mathsf{m}A>0
and
\mathsf{m}A^{\mathrm{c}}>0
.
By
\mathcal{E}
-invariance of
A
and
[
17
, Lem. 2.39]
we have
\car_{A}\in{{\mathcal{F}}^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{loc}}}
and
\mu_{\car_{A}}\equiv 0
.
Hence, by
(\mathsf{SL}_{\mathsf{m},\mathsf{d}})
, the function
\car_{A}
has a
\mathsf{d}
-Lipschitz representative
\widehat{\car_{A}}
with
\mathrm{L}_{\mathsf{d}}(\widehat{\car_{A}})=0
.
Since
\mathsf{d}
is a distance (not: extended),
\widehat{\car_{A}}
is a constant, which is a contradiction since
\mathsf{m}A,\mathsf{m}A^{\mathrm{c}}>0
.
∎

See
[
17
, §2.6.2]
for relations between
\mathcal{E}
-invariance and metric notions (in particular,
\mathsf{d}
-accessibility) in the non-irreducible case.

Maximal functions

We recall the following adaptation to our setting of results in
[
10
]
. Set

\displaystyle\mathbb{L}^{\mu,A}_{\mathrm{loc},r}\eqdef\left\{f\in\mathbb{L}^{\mu}_{\mathrm{loc}}:f=0\quad\mathsf{m}\text{-a.e.}\text{~on~}A\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,\left\lvert f\right\rvert\leq r\quad\mathsf{m}\text{-a.e.}\right\}\subset\mathbb{L}^{\mu}_{\mathrm{loc},b}\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,\qquad r>0\,\,\mathrm{.}

Proposition 5.12

(
[
17
, Prop. 4.14]
)
.

Let
(\mathbb{X},\mathcal{E})
be a quasi-regular strongly local Dirichlet space.
For each
A\in\Sigma
there exists an
\mathsf{m}
-a.e. unique
\Sigma
-measurable function
\bar{\mathsf{d}}_{\mathsf{m},A}\colon X\rightarrow[0,\infty]
so that
\bar{\mathsf{d}}_{\mathsf{m},A}\wedge r
is the
\mathsf{m}
-a.e. maximal element of
\mathbb{L}^{\mathsf{m},A}_{\mathrm{loc},r}
.

We call the function
\bar{\mathsf{d}}_{\mu,A}
constructed in Proposition
5.12
the
maximal function
of
A\in\Sigma
.
Note that
\bar{\mathsf{d}}_{\mathsf{m},A}
is generally not an element of
{{L^{\infty}(\mathsf{m})}^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{loc}}}
, unless
(\mathbb{X},\mathcal{E})
is irreducible.

In proving the tensorization of the Sobolev-to-Lipschitz property, we shall need the following specialization to our setting of a very general result by T. Ariyoshi and M. Hino.

Theorem 5.13

(
[
10
, Thm. 5.2]
, irreducible case)
.

Let
(\mathbb{X},\mathcal{E})
be an irreducible quasi-regular strongly local Dirichlet space.
Then, for every
A\in\Sigma
with
0<\mathsf{m}A<\infty
,

\displaystyle\nu\text{-}\lim_{t\downarrow 0}\big({-2t\log T_{t}\car_{A}}\big)=\bar{\mathsf{d}}_{\mathsf{m},A}^{2}

for every probability measure
\nu
on
\Sigma
equivalent to
\mathsf{m}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}
.

5.3.2.
Tensorization of the Sobolev-to-Lipschitz property

Let us now show a form of tensorization of the Sobolev-to-Lipschitz property for metric measure spaces.
We recall that, on every such space
(X,\mathsf{d},\mathsf{m})
, the topology
\tau
is always assumed to be the one induced by
\mathsf{d}
, and no other topology is considered.

We start with two preparatory lemmas.

Lemma 5.14
.

Let
(\mathbb{X},\mathcal{E})
, resp.
(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathcal{E}^{\prime})
, be a quasi-regular strongly local Dirichlet spaces with
\mathbb{X}
, resp.
\mathbb{X}^{\prime}
, satisfying
2
.
Further let
\mathsf{d}\colon X^{\scriptscriptstyle{\times 2}}\to[0,\infty]
, resp.
\mathsf{d}^{\prime}\colon X^{\prime{\scriptscriptstyle{\times 2}}}\to[0,\infty]
, be a separable jointly
\tau
-, resp.
\tau^{\prime}
-, continuous extended pseudo-distance on
X
, resp.
X^{\prime}
, and assume
({\mathsf{d}}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SL}_{\mathsf{m}})
, resp.
({\mathsf{d}^{\prime}}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SL}_{\mathsf{m}^{\prime}})
.
Then,

(5.19)

\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}}\leq\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}}\oplus\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}^{\prime}}\quad\text{everywhere on~$X^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}$}\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,

and the topology induced by
\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}}
on
X^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}
is separable.

Proof.

Firstly, note that by
({\mathsf{d}}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SL}_{\mathsf{m}})
,
({\mathsf{d}^{\prime}}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SL}_{\mathsf{m}^{\prime}})
and joint
\tau^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}
-continuity of
\mathsf{d}\oplus\mathsf{d}^{\prime}
, the extended pseudo-distance
\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}}\oplus\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}^{\prime}}
too is jointly
\tau^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}
-continuous.
Now, let
\Omega^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}\in\Sigma^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}
be a set of full
\mathsf{m}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}
-measure on which (
5.5
) holds.
By
\tau^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}
-density of
\Omega^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}
in
X^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}
, for every
\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}\in X^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}
there exists
\left(\mathbf{x}_{n}\right)_{n},\left(\mathbf{y}_{n}\right)_{n}\subset\Omega^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}
with
\tau^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}
-
\lim_{n}\mathbf{x}_{n}=\mathbf{x}
and
\tau^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}
-
\lim_{n}\mathbf{y}_{n}=\mathbf{y}
.
Thus, by joint
\tau^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}
-lower semi-continuity of
\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}}
and joint
\tau^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}
-continuity of
\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}}\oplus\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}^{\prime}}
,

\displaystyle\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})\leq\liminf_{n}\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}}(\mathbf{x}_{n},\mathbf{y}_{n})\leq\liminf_{n}(\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}}\oplus\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}^{\prime}})(\mathbf{x}_{n},\mathbf{y}_{n})=(\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}}\oplus\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}^{\prime}})(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})\,\,\mathrm{.}

Separability of
\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}}

Since
(X,\mathsf{d})
, resp.
(X^{\prime},\mathsf{d}^{\prime})
, is separable, so is
(X,\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}})
, resp.
(X^{\prime},\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}^{\prime}})
, by
({\mathsf{d}}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SL}_{\mathsf{m}})
, resp.
({\mathsf{d}^{\prime}}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SL}_{\mathsf{m}^{\prime}})
.
Thus,
(X^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}},\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}}\oplus\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}^{\prime}})
is separable as well, and
(X,\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}})
is separable as a consequence of (
5.19
).
∎

Let us recall the following results in
[
17
]
.

Lemma 5.15
.

Let
(\mathbb{X},\mathcal{E})
be a quasi-regular strongly local Dirichlet space with
\mathbb{X}
satisfying
2
, and assume that the topology generated on
X
by
\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}}
is separable.
Then,

(1)

(\mathbb{X},\mathcal{E})
satisfies
(\mathsf{Rad}_{\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}},\mathsf{m}})
;

(2)

\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}}({\,\cdot\,},A)
is
\Sigma
-measurable for every
A\in\Sigma
;

(3)

\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}}({\,\cdot\,},A)\leq\bar{\mathsf{d}}_{\mathsf{m},A}

\mathsf{m}
-a.e. for every
A\in\Sigma
.

Proof.

Assertion
1
is
[
17
, Cor. 3.15]
.
In order to show
2
, it suffices to show that
\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}}({\,\cdot\,},x)
is
\Sigma
-measurable for every
x\in X
.
Indeed, this implies the
\Sigma
-measurability of
\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}}({\,\cdot\,},A)
for every
A\in\Sigma
as in the proof of
[
17
, Rmk. 3.12(i)]
.
Since
\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}}({\,\cdot\,},x)
is
\tau
-lower semi-continuous for every
x\in X
, it is also
\Sigma
-measurable, since
\Sigma\supset\mathscr{B}_{\tau}
.
This concludes the proof of
2
.
Assertion
3
follows from
1
and
2
by
[
17
, Lem. 4.16]
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
5.10
.

We prove
({\mathsf{d}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SL}_{\mathsf{m}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}})
and conclude the assertion by (
2.14
).

Step I: comparison with the maximal function

By
(\mathsf{SL}_{\mathsf{m},\mathsf{d}})
, resp.
(\mathsf{SL}_{\mathsf{m}^{\prime},\mathsf{d}^{\prime}})
, the Dirichlet spaces
(\mathbb{X},\mathcal{E})
and
(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathcal{E}^{\prime})
are both irreducible.
Fix
A\in\tau
and
A^{\prime}\in\tau^{\prime}
with
\mathsf{m}A,\mathsf{m}^{\prime}A^{\prime}\in(0,\infty)
to be chosen later, and set
A^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}\eqdef A\times A^{\prime}
.
Let
\nu
, resp.
\nu^{\prime}
, be any probability measure on
(X,\Sigma)
, resp.
(X^{\prime},\Sigma^{\prime})
, equivalent to
\mathsf{m}
, resp.
\mathsf{m}^{\prime}
, and observe that
\nu^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}\eqdef\nu\otimes\nu^{\prime}
is a probability measure on
\Sigma^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}
equivalent to
\mathsf{m}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}
.

On the one hand, by (
5.18
) and by Theorem
5.13
applied to
(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
, resp.
(\mathcal{E}^{\prime},\mathcal{F}^{\prime})
,

(5.20)

\displaystyle\nu^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}\text{-}\lim_{t\to 0}\left(-2t\log T_{t}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}\car_{A^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}}\right)=\nu\text{-}\lim_{t\to 0}\left(-2t\log T_{t}\car_{A}\right)+\nu^{\prime}\text{-}\lim_{t\to 0}\left(-2t\log T_{t}^{\prime}\car_{A^{\prime}}\right)=\bar{\mathsf{d}}_{\mathsf{m},A}^{2}+\bar{\mathsf{d}}_{\mathsf{m}^{\prime},A^{\prime}}^{2}\,\,\mathrm{.}

Since
(X,\mathsf{d},\mathsf{m})
, resp.
(X^{\prime},\mathsf{d}^{\prime},\mathsf{m}^{\prime})
, is a metric measure space,
(\mathsf{ScL}_{\mathsf{m},\tau,\mathsf{d}})
coincides with
(\mathsf{SL}_{\mathsf{m},\mathsf{d}})
, resp.
(\mathsf{ScL}_{\mathsf{m}^{\prime},\tau^{\prime},\mathsf{d}^{\prime}})
with
(\mathsf{SL}_{\mathsf{m}^{\prime},\mathsf{d}^{\prime}})
.
Furthermore, since
A
is
\tau
-open, resp.
A^{\prime}
is
\tau^{\prime}
-open, we have
A\cap\mathrm{cl}_{\tau}\inter_{\tau}A=A
and analogously for
A^{\prime}
in place of
A
.
Thus, combining
(\mathsf{SL}_{\mathsf{m},\mathsf{d}})
, resp.
(\mathsf{SL}_{\mathsf{m}^{\prime},\mathsf{d}^{\prime}})
, with
[
17
, Lem. 4.19, Rem. 4.20(d)]
, it follows from (
5.20
) that

(5.21)

\displaystyle\nu^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}\text{-}\lim_{t\to 0}\left(-2t\log T_{t}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}\car_{A^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}}\right)\leq\mathsf{d}({\,\cdot\,},A)^{2}+\mathsf{d}^{\prime}({\,\cdot\,},A^{\prime})^{2}\quad\mathsf{m}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}\text{-a.e.}\,\,\mathrm{.}

On the other hand, by Theorem
5.13
applied to
(\mathcal{E}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}},\mathcal{F}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}})
,

(5.22)

\displaystyle\nu^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}\text{-}\lim_{t\to 0}\left(-2t\log T_{t}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}\car_{A^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}}\right)=\bar{\mathsf{d}}_{\mathsf{m}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}},A^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}}^{2}\quad\mathsf{m}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}\text{-a.e.}\,\,\mathrm{.}

Combining (
5.21
) and (
5.22
), for
\mathsf{m}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}
-a.e.
\mathbf{x}\eqdef(x,x^{\prime})
,

\displaystyle\bar{\mathsf{d}}_{\mathsf{m}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}},A^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}}(\mathbf{x})^{2}\leq

\displaystyle\ \mathsf{d}(x,A)^{2}+\mathsf{d}^{\prime}(x^{\prime},A^{\prime})^{2}

\displaystyle\eqqcolon

\displaystyle\ \inf_{y\in A}\mathsf{d}(x,y)^{2}+\inf_{y^{\prime}\in A^{\prime}}\mathsf{d}^{\prime}(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})^{2}=\inf_{(y,y^{\prime})\in A^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}}\mathsf{d}(x,y)^{2}+\inf_{(y,y^{\prime})\in A^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}}\mathsf{d}^{\prime}(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\ \inf_{(y,y^{\prime})\in A^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}}\big({\mathsf{d}(x,y)^{2}+\mathsf{d}^{\prime}(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})^{2}}\big)=\inf_{\mathbf{y}\in A^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}}\mathsf{d}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \left(\inf_{\mathbf{y}\in A^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}}\mathsf{d}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})\right)^{2}=\mathsf{d}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}(\mathbf{x},A^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}})^{2}\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,

that is

(5.23)

\displaystyle\bar{\mathsf{d}}_{\mathsf{m}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}},A^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}}\leq\mathsf{d}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}({\,\cdot\,},A)\quad\mathsf{m}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}\text{-a.e.}

Step II: comparison with the intrinsic distance

By Lemma
5.14
the topology induced by
\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}}
is separable.
Thus, by Lemma
5.15
3
applied to
(\mathbb{X}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}},\mathcal{E}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}})
,

(5.24)

\displaystyle\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}}({\,\cdot\,},A^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}})\leq\bar{\mathsf{d}}_{\mathsf{m}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}},A^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}}\quad\mathsf{m}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}\text{-a.e.}\,\,\mathrm{.}

Combining (
5.23
) and (
5.24
) we thus have, for every
A^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}\eqdef A\times A^{\prime}
with
A\in\tau
,
A^{\prime}\in\tau^{\prime}
,

(5.25)

\displaystyle\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}}({\,\cdot\,},A^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}})\leq\mathsf{d}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}({\,\cdot\,},A^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}})\quad\mathsf{m}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}\text{-a.e.}\,\,\mathrm{.}

Now, fix
\mathbf{x}\eqdef(x,x^{\prime})\in X^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}
, choose
A^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}=A^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}_{r}\eqdef B^{\mathsf{d}}_{r}(x)\times B^{\mathsf{d}^{\prime}}_{r}(x^{\prime})
in (
5.25
), and note that

(5.26)

\displaystyle\inf_{r>0}\mathrm{diam}_{\mathsf{d}_{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}}(A^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}})=0\,\,\mathrm{.}

Then, by Lemma
2.1

(5.27)

\displaystyle\sup_{r>0}\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}}({\,\cdot\,},A^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}_{r})\leq\sup_{r>0}\mathsf{d}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}({\,\cdot\,},A^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}_{r})=\mathsf{d}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}({\,\cdot\,},\mathbf{x})\quad\mathsf{m}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}\text{-a.e.}\,\,\mathrm{.}

Let
\Omega^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}\in\Sigma^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}
be a set of full
\mathsf{m}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}
-measure on which (
5.27
) holds, and fix
\mathbf{y}\in\Omega^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}
.
For each
r>0
, further let
\mathbf{x}_{r}\in A^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}_{r}
be satisfying
\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}}(\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x}_{r})\leq\varepsilon+\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}}(\mathbf{y},A^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}_{r})
.
Since
\mathbf{x}_{r}\in A^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}_{r}
, there exists
\tau^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}
-
\lim_{r\downarrow 0}\mathbf{x}_{r}=\mathbf{x}
by (
5.26
).
Thus, for every
\mathbf{y}\in\Omega^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}
,

\displaystyle\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}}(\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})-\varepsilon\leq\liminf_{r\downarrow 0}\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}}(\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x}_{r})-\varepsilon\leq\liminf_{r\downarrow 0}\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}}(\mathbf{y},A^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}_{r})=\sup_{r>0}\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}}(\mathbf{y},A^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}_{r})\leq\mathsf{d}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}(\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,

where the first and last inequality hold by
\tau^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}
-lower semi-continuity of
\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}}
and (
5.27
) respectively.
Since
\varepsilon>0
was arbitrary, we conclude

(5.28)

\displaystyle\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}}(\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})\leq\mathsf{d}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}(\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,\qquad\mathbf{y}\in\Omega^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,\mathbf{x}\in X^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}\,\,\mathrm{.}

Since
(X,\mathsf{d},\mathsf{m})
, resp.
(X^{\prime},\mathsf{d}^{\prime},\mathsf{m}^{\prime})
is a metric measure space in the sense of Definition
5.1
, the set
\Omega^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}
is
\tau^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}
-dense in
X^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}
.
For each fixed
\mathbf{y}_{0}\in X^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}
we may thus find a sequence
\left(\mathbf{y}_{n}\right)_{n}\subset\Omega^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}
with
\tau^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}
-
\lim_{n}\mathbf{y}_{n}=\mathbf{y}_{0}
.
Respectively by
\tau^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}
-lower semi-continuity of
\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}}
, the inequality (
5.28
), and by
\tau^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}
-continuity of
\mathsf{d}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}
,

\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}}(\mathbf{y}_{0},\mathbf{x})\leq\liminf_{n}\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}}(\mathbf{y}_{n},\mathbf{x})\leq\liminf_{n}\mathsf{d}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}(\mathbf{y}_{n},\mathbf{x})=\mathsf{d}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}(\mathbf{y}_{0},\mathbf{x})\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,

which concludes the proof by arbitrariness of
\mathbf{x}
, and
\mathbf{y}_{0}
.
∎

5.3.3.
Tensorization of the intrinsic distance

As a consequence of the tensorization of the Sobolev-to-Lipschitz property we further obtain the following identification of the intrinsic distance of product forms.

Corollary 5.16
.

Let
\mathbb{X}\eqdef(X,\mathsf{d},\mathsf{m})
be a metric measure space (Dfn.
5.1
), and
(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
be a quasi-regular strongly local Dirichlet form on
\mathbb{X}
satisfying
(\mathsf{Rad}_{\mathsf{d},\mathsf{m}})
and
(\mathsf{SL}_{\mathsf{m},\mathsf{d}})
.
Further let
(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathcal{E}^{\prime})
be satisfying the same assumptions as
(\mathbb{X},\mathcal{E})
.
Then,

\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}}=\mathsf{d}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}=\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}}\otimes\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}^{\prime}}\,\,\mathrm{.}

Proof.

By Theorem
5.9
we conclude
(\mathsf{Rad}_{\mathsf{d}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}},\mathsf{m}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}})
, hence
{\mathsf{d}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}}\textrm{-}\mathsf{Rad}_{\mathsf{m}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}}
by (
2.14
).
By Theorem
5.10
we conclude
({\mathsf{d}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SL}_{\mathsf{m}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}})
.
Thus, the first equality holds.
By
(\mathsf{Rad}_{\mathsf{d},\mathsf{m}})
, resp.
(\mathsf{Rad}_{\mathsf{d}^{\prime},\mathsf{m}^{\prime}})
, and (
2.14
) we conclude
({\mathsf{d}}\textrm{-}\mathsf{Rad}_{\mathsf{m}})
, resp.
({\mathsf{d}^{\prime}}\textrm{-}\mathsf{Rad}_{\mathsf{m}^{\prime}})
.
Together with the assumption of
({\mathsf{d}}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SL}_{\mathsf{m}})
and
({\mathsf{d}^{\prime}}\textrm{-}\mathsf{SL}_{\mathsf{m}^{\prime}})
, this implies
\mathsf{d}=\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}}
and
\mathsf{d}^{\prime}=\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}^{\prime}}
, which shows the second equality.
∎

5.3.4.
Doubling-and-Poincaré spaces

Let
(X,\mathsf{d},\mathsf{m})
be a metric measure space in the sense of Definition
5.1
.
In this short section we show that, under measure doubling and a weak Poincaré inequality for the factors, the Sobolev-to-Lipschitz property of the Cheeger energy tensorizes.
We start by recalling the necessary definitions.

Definition 5.17

(Measure doubling and Poincaré inequality)
.

We say that
(X,\mathsf{d},\mathsf{m})
is (
measure
)
doubling
if there exists a constant
C>0
such that

(
\mathsf{D}_{\mathsf{d},\mathsf{m}}
)

\mathsf{m}B_{2r}(x)\leq C\,\mathsf{m}B_{r}(x)\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,\qquad x\in X\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,r>0\,\,\mathrm{.}

We say that
(X,\mathsf{d},\mathsf{m})
satisfies a
weak
(1,2)
-Poincaré inequality
if, for some constants
c,\lambda>0
,

(
\mathsf{P}_{\mathsf{d},\mathsf{m}}
)

-\!\!\!\!\!\!\int_{B_{r}(x)}\left\lvert f-f_{x,r}\right\rvert\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mathsf{m}\leq cr\left(-\!\!\!\!\!\!\int_{B_{\lambda r}(x)}\left\lvert\mathrm{D}f\right\rvert_{\mathsf{d}}^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mathsf{m}\right)^{1/2}\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,\qquad f\in\mathrm{Lip}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathsf{d})\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,\quad x\in X\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,r>0\,\,\mathrm{.}

(Here,
-\!\!\!\!\!\!\int_{A}f\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mathsf{m}
denotes the averaged integral of
f
over a Borel set
A
, and
f_{x,r}\eqdef-\!\!\!\!\!\!\int_{B_{r}(x)}f\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mathsf{m}
.)

We write
(\mathsf{DP}_{\mathsf{d},\mathsf{m}})
to indicate that
(X,\mathsf{d},\mathsf{m})
satisfies both
(\mathsf{D}_{\mathsf{d},\mathsf{m}})
and
(\mathsf{P}_{\mathsf{d},\mathsf{m}})
.

On a metric measure space satisfying
(\mathsf{DP}_{\mathsf{d},\mathsf{m}})
we have the following self-improvement of the continuous-Sobolev-to-Lipschitz property to the Sobolev-to-Lipschitz property.

Lemma 5.18

(Self-improvement of
(\mathsf{cSL}_{\tau,\mathsf{m},\mathsf{d}})
to
(\mathsf{SL}_{\mathsf{m},\mathsf{d}})
)
.

Let
\mathbb{X}\eqdef(X,\mathsf{d},\mathsf{m})
be an infinitesimally Hilbertian metric measure space satisfying
(\mathsf{DP}_{\mathsf{d},\mathsf{m}})
.
Then,
(\mathbb{X},\mathsf{Ch}_{w,\mathsf{d},\mathsf{m}})
satisfies
(\mathsf{SL}_{\mathsf{m},\mathsf{d}})
if and only if it satisfies
(\mathsf{cSL}_{\tau,\mathsf{d},\mathsf{m}})
.

Proof.

One implication holds by definition, cf. (
2.14
).
For the reverse implication it suffices to note that every
f\in\mathbb{L}^{\mathsf{m}}_{\mathrm{loc},b}
admits a
\tau
-continuous (in fact
\mathsf{d}
-Lipschitz) representative, by e.g.
[
28
]
, also cf.
[
9
, Prop. 2.11]
.
∎

In light of this self-improvement, for metric measure spaces satisfying
(\mathsf{DP}_{\mathsf{d},\mathsf{m}})
the tensorization of the Sobolev-to-Lipschitz property takes the following strong form.

Corollary 5.19
.

Let
\mathbb{X}\eqdef(X,\mathsf{d},\mathsf{m})
, resp.
\mathbb{X}^{\prime}\eqdef(X^{\prime},\mathsf{d}^{\prime},\mathsf{m}^{\prime})
, be an infinitesimally Hilbertian metric measure space satisfying
(\mathsf{DP}_{\mathsf{d},\mathsf{m}})
, resp.
(\mathsf{DP}_{\mathsf{d}^{\prime},\mathsf{m}^{\prime}})
.
If
(\mathbb{X},\mathsf{Ch}_{w,\mathsf{d},\mathsf{m}})
, resp.
(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathsf{Ch}_{w,\mathsf{d}^{\prime},\mathsf{m}^{\prime}})
, satisfies
(\mathsf{cSL}_{\tau,\mathsf{m},\mathsf{d}})
, resp.
(\mathsf{cSL}_{\tau^{\prime},\mathsf{m}^{\prime},\mathsf{d}^{\prime}})
, then
\mathsf{Ch}_{w,\mathsf{d}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}},\mathsf{m}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}}
satisfies
(\mathsf{SL}_{\mathsf{m}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}},\mathsf{d}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}})
.

Proof.

Note that
(\mathsf{DP}_{\mathsf{d},\mathsf{m}})
and
(\mathsf{DP}_{\mathsf{d}^{\prime},\mathsf{m}^{\prime}})
together imply
(\mathsf{DP}_{\mathsf{d}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}},\mathsf{m}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}})
.
The assertion holds combining Theorem
5.10
with Lemma
5.18
.
∎

5.3.5.
Tensorization of the Varadhan short-time asymptotics

As a further consequence of the tensorization of both the Rademacher and the Sobolev-to-Lipschitz property, we further obtain the tensorization of Varadhan’s short-time asymptotics for the heat flow.

Recall the notation for semigroups in §
5.3.1
.
Whenever it exists, we denote by
p_{t}(x,y)
the
heat kernel
on
X
, i.e. the integral kernel of
T_{t}
.
The kernel
p_{t}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})
on
X^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}
is defined analogously.

Corollary 5.20
.

Let
\mathbb{X}\eqdef(X,\mathsf{d},\mathsf{m})
be an infinitesimally Hilbertian metric measure space satisfying
(\mathsf{DP}_{\mathsf{d},\mathsf{m}})
, and
3
, with
\mathsf{Ch}_{\mathsf{d},\mathsf{m}}
satisfying
(\mathsf{SL}_{\mathsf{m},\mathsf{d}})
.
Further let
\mathbb{X}^{\prime}
be satisfying the same assumptions as
\mathbb{X}
.
Then, for every
\mathbf{x}\eqdef(x,x^{\prime}),\mathbf{y}\eqdef(y,y^{\prime})\in X^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}
,

\lim_{t\downarrow 0}\big({-2t\log p_{t}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})}\big)=\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})^{2}=\mathsf{d}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})^{2}=\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}}(x,y)^{2}+\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{m}^{\prime}}(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})^{2}\,\,\mathrm{.}

Proof.

It suffices to verify the assumptions in
[
34
, Thm. 4.1]
and
[
38
, Thm. 0.1]
.
Firstly,
\mathbb{X}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}
satisfies
3
since so do its factors.
On every space satisfying
3
, our definition (
2.13
) of intrinsic distance coincides with the one in
[
34
, p. 282]
by
[
18
, Prop. 2.31]
.
Secondly, the Cheeger energy
\mathsf{Ch}_{\mathsf{d}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}},\mathsf{m}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}}
is strongly regular on
\mathbb{X}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}
in light of Corollary
5.16
.
Finally, the validity of
(\mathsf{DP}_{\mathsf{d}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}},\mathsf{m}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\otimes}}})
follows from that of
(\mathsf{DP}_{\mathsf{d},\mathsf{m}})
and
(\mathsf{DP}_{\mathsf{d}^{\prime},\mathsf{m}^{\prime}})
.
The inequality
\geq
for the first equality then follows from
[
34
, Thm. 4.1]
.
The inequality
\leq
is a consequence of the upper heat-kernel estimate
[
38
, Thm. 0.1]
.
The second and third equalities follow from Corollary
5.16
.
∎

6.
Direct integrals

In this section we study the validity of the Rademacher and Sobolev-to-Lipschitz properties for direct integrals of Dirichlet forms as introduced in
[
15
]
, to which we refer the reader for a complete discussion; see also
[
20
]
for additional results, and
[
32
]
for a different but related decomposition.

We briefly recall the main definitions. For the sake of simplicity, we confine ourselves to the case of probability spaces. We expect that the result in this section can be adapted to the case of
\sigma
-finite measures by means of Propositions
4.1
and
4.2
.

Throughout this section, let
\mathbb{X}
be satisfying
2
with
\mathsf{m}
a
probability
measure, and
(Z,\mathcal{Z},\nu)
be a countably generated probability space.
A
disintegration
of
\mathsf{m}
over
(Z,\mathcal{Z},\nu)
is a family
\left(\mathsf{m}_{\zeta}\right)_{\zeta\in Z}
of non-zero measures on
(X,\Sigma)
so that
\zeta\mapsto\mathsf{m}_{\zeta}A
is
\nu
-measurable for every
A\in\Sigma
and

(6.1)

\mathsf{m}A=\int_{Z}\mathsf{m}_{\zeta}A\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\nu(\zeta)\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,\qquad A\in\Sigma\,\,\mathrm{.}

A disintegration is
separated
if there exists a family of pairwise disjoint sets
\left(A_{\zeta}\right)_{\zeta\in Z}\subset\Sigma^{\mathsf{m}}
so that
A_{\zeta}
is
\mathsf{m}_{\zeta}
-conegligible for
\nu
-a.e.
\zeta\in Z
.

Henceforth, let
\left(\mathsf{m}_{\zeta}\right)_{\zeta\in Z}
be a separated disintegration of
\mathsf{m}
over
(Z,\mathcal{Z},\nu)
as above.
For a fixed Borel
\mathsf{m}
-representative
\hat{f}
of
f\in L^{2}(\mathsf{m})
, write
f_{\zeta}\eqdef\big[\hat{f}\big]_{\mathsf{m}_{\zeta}}
.
As it turns out, the choice of the
\mathsf{m}
-representative
\hat{f}
of
f
is immaterial, and the assignment
\iota\colon f\mapsto(\zeta\mapsto f_{\zeta})
defines a unitary isomorphism providing a representation of
L^{2}(\mathsf{m})
as the direct integral of the Hilbert spaces
L^{2}(\mathsf{m}_{\zeta})
, see
[
15
, Prop. 2.25(i)]
, viz.

L^{2}(\mathsf{m})\cong\sideset{\!\!\!}{{}_{Z}^{\scriptstyle\oplus}}{\int}L^{2}(\mathsf{m}_{\zeta})\,\,\mathrm{.}

Again since
\left(\mathsf{m}_{\zeta}\right)_{\zeta\in Z}
is separated, such isomorphism is additionally a Riesz homomorphism, i.e. satisfying

(6.2)

f\geq 0\quad\mathsf{m}\text{-a.e.}\quad\iff\quad f_{\zeta}\geq 0\quad\mathsf{m}_{\zeta}\text{-a.e.}\quad{\textrm{\,for ${\nu}$-a.e.\,}}\zeta\in Z\,\,\mathrm{.}

Lemma 6.1
.

Let
f\in L^{\infty}(\mathsf{m})\subset L^{2}(\mathsf{m})
. Then,

\left\lVert f\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathsf{m})}=\nu\text{-}\esssup\left\lVert f_{\zeta}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathsf{m}_{\zeta})}\,\,\mathrm{.}

Proof.

It follows from (
6.2
) that if
\left\lvert f\right\rvert\leq a

\mathsf{m}
-a.e. for some constant
a>0
, then
\left\lvert f_{\zeta}\right\rvert\leq a

\mathsf{m}_{\zeta}
-a.e. for
\nu
-a.e.
\zeta\in Z
.
On the one hand, as a consequence,
\left\lVert f_{\zeta}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathsf{m}_{\zeta})}\leq\left\lVert f\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathsf{m})}
for
\nu
-a.e.
\zeta\in Z
, which shows the inequality ‘
\geq
’ in the assertion.
On the other hand, for every
a<\left\lVert f\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathsf{m})}
there exists a set
A_{a}\in\Sigma
with
\mathsf{m}A>0
and
\left\lvert f\right\rvert\geq a

\mathsf{m}
-a.e. on
A_{a}
.
Thus, by (
6.1
) and since the disintegration is separated, there exists a set
B\in\mathcal{Z}
of positive
\nu
-measure so that
\mathsf{m}_{\zeta}(A_{a}\cap A_{\zeta})>0
for every
\zeta\in B
, and
\left\lvert f_{\zeta}\right\rvert\geq a

\mathsf{m}_{\zeta}
-a.e. on
A_{a}\cap A_{\zeta}
again by (
6.2
).
Since
\mathsf{m}_{\zeta}(A_{a}\cap A_{\zeta})>0
we have
\left\lVert f_{\zeta}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathsf{m}_{\zeta})}>a
, and since
\nu B>0
we conclude that
\nu
-
\esssup\left\lVert f_{\zeta}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathsf{m}_{\zeta})}>a
.
The inequality ‘
\leq
’ in the assertion follows by arbitrariness of
a<\left\lVert f\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathsf{m})}
.
∎

The next definition of direct integrals of Dirichlet forms is taken from
[
15
]
.
We present here a simplified definition for probability spaces, and refer the reader to
[
15
, §2]
for a detailed discussion.

Definition 6.2

(Direct integrals of Dirichlet forms,
[
15
, Dfn.s 2.11, 2.26, and 2.31]
)
.

For each
\zeta\in Z
let
(\mathcal{E}_{\zeta},\mathcal{F}_{\zeta})
be a quasi-regular strongly local Dirichlet form on
L^{2}(\mathsf{m}_{\zeta})
, and assume that
\zeta\mapsto\mathcal{F}_{\zeta}
is a
\nu
-measurable field of Hilbert subspaces of
\zeta\mapsto L^{2}(\mathsf{m}_{\zeta})
.
The
direct integral of Dirichlet forms
of the field
\zeta\mapsto(\mathcal{E}_{\zeta},\mathcal{F}_{\zeta})
is the Dirichlet form

(6.3)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \mathcal{F}\eqdef&\ \left\{f\in L^{2}(\mathsf{m})\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,\int_{Z}\left(\mathcal{E}_{\zeta}(f_{\zeta})+\left\lVert f_{\zeta}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathsf{m}_{\zeta})}^{2}\right)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\nu(\zeta)<\infty\right\}\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,\\
\mathcal{E}(f,g)\eqdef&\ \int_{Z}\mathcal{E}_{\zeta}(f_{\zeta},g_{\zeta})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\nu(\zeta)\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,\qquad f,g\in\mathcal{F}\,\,\mathrm{.}\end{aligned}

In the rest of this section, we will work under the following standing assumption.

Assumption 6.3
.

Let
(\mathbb{X},\mathcal{E})
be a
conservative
quasi-regular strongly local Dirichlet space, with
\mathbb{X}
satisfying
2
and
\mathsf{m}
a
probability
measure.
We assume that there exists a countably generated probability space
(Z,\mathcal{Z},\nu)
with the following property:
for every
\zeta\in Z
there exists a quasi-regular strongly local Dirichlet space
(\mathbb{X}_{\zeta},\mathcal{E}_{\zeta})
with
\mathbb{X}_{\zeta}=\mathbb{X}
for
\nu
-a.e.
\zeta\in Z
, and
(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
is the direct-integral Dirichlet form of
\zeta\mapsto(\mathcal{E}_{\zeta},\mathcal{F}_{\zeta})
.

Everywhere in the following, without loss of generality up to removing a
\nu
-negligible set from
Z
, we may and will assume that
\mathbb{X}_{\zeta}=\mathbb{X}
for
every

\zeta\in Z
.
Whereas we require
\mathbb{X}_{\zeta}=\mathbb{X}
, we stress that we do not require
(\mathbb{X},\mathcal{E}_{\zeta})
to satisfy
2
, that is, we do
not
require that
\mathrm{supp}[\mathsf{m}_{\zeta}]=X
.

Lemma 6.4
.

Let
(\mathbb{X},\mathcal{E})
be satisfying Assumption
6.3
. Then,

(6.4)

\displaystyle f\in\mathbb{L}^{\mathsf{m}}_{\mathrm{loc},b}\iff f_{\zeta}\in\mathbb{L}^{\mathsf{m}_{\zeta}}_{\mathrm{loc},b}\quad{\textrm{\,for ${\nu}$-a.e.\,}}\zeta\in Z\,\,\mathrm{.}

Proof.

Since
(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
is conservative by assumption, and since
\mathsf{m}
is a finite measure, we have
\car\in\mathcal{F}
, hence
\car\in\mathcal{F}_{\zeta}
for every (without loss of generality, as opposed to:
\nu
-a.e.)
\zeta\in Z
.
By Lemma
2.10
we have that

(6.5)

\displaystyle\mathbb{L}^{\mathsf{m}}_{\mathrm{loc},b}=\mathbb{L}^{\mathsf{m}}_{b}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\mathbb{L}^{\mathcal{E}_{\zeta}\ \mathsf{m}_{\zeta}}_{\mathrm{loc},b}=\mathbb{L}^{\mathcal{E}_{\zeta}\ \mathsf{m}_{\zeta}}_{b}\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,\quad\zeta\in Z\,\,\mathrm{.}

By
[
11
, §V.3, Exercise 3.2(2), p. 216]
the form
(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
admits carré du champ operator

\Gamma(f)(x)=\int\Gamma_{\zeta}(f_{\zeta})(x)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\nu(\zeta)\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,\qquad f\in\mathcal{F}_{b}\,\,\mathrm{.}

By Lemma
6.1
we conclude

(6.6)

\left\lVert\Gamma(f)\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathsf{m})}=\nu\text{-}\esssup\left\lVert\Gamma_{\zeta}(f_{\zeta})\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathsf{m}_{\zeta})}\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,\qquad f\in\mathcal{F}_{b}\,\,\mathrm{.}\,\,\mathrm{.}

Combining (
6.3
) and (
6.6
) we have therefore that

\displaystyle\mathbb{L}^{\mathsf{m}}_{b}=\left\{f\in L^{2}(\mathsf{m})\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,f_{\zeta}\in\mathbb{L}^{\mathsf{m}_{\zeta}}_{b}{\textrm{\,for ${\nu}$-a.e.\,}}\zeta\in Z\right\}\,\,\mathrm{.}

By (
6.5
) we finally conclude that

(6.7)

\displaystyle f\in\mathbb{L}^{\mathsf{m}}_{\mathrm{loc},b}\iff f_{\zeta}\in\mathbb{L}^{\mathsf{m}_{\zeta}}_{\mathrm{loc},b}{\textrm{\,for ${\nu}$-a.e.\,}}\zeta\in Z\,\,\mathrm{.}

Theorem 6.5
.

Let
(\mathbb{X},\mathcal{E})
be satisfying Assumption
6.3
, and
\mathsf{d}\colon X^{\scriptscriptstyle{\times 2}}\to[0,\infty]
be an extended pseudo-distance.
Then, the following are equivalent:

(1)

(\mathbb{X},\mathcal{E})
satisfies
(\mathsf{Rad}_{\mathsf{d},\mathsf{m}})
;

(2)

(\mathbb{X},\mathcal{E}_{\zeta})
satisfies
(\mathsf{Rad}_{\mathsf{d},\mathsf{m}_{\zeta}})
for
\nu
-a.e.
\zeta\in Z
.

Proof.

Fix
\hat{f}\in\mathrm{Lip}^{1}_{b}(\mathsf{d},\Sigma)
.
Assume first
(\mathsf{Rad}_{\mathsf{d},\mathsf{m}_{\zeta}})
for
\nu
-a.e.
\zeta\in Z
.
Then,
\Gamma_{\zeta}(f_{\zeta})\leq 1

\mathsf{m}_{\zeta}
-a.e. for
\nu
-a.e.
\zeta\in Z
by the assumption, and combining this fact with (
6.4
) proves
(\mathsf{Rad}_{\mathsf{d},\mathsf{m}})
.

Vice versa, assume
(\mathsf{Rad}_{\mathsf{d},\mathsf{m}})
. Then
\Gamma(f)\leq 1

\mathsf{m}
-a.e. by assumption, and combining this fact with (
6.4
) proves
(\mathsf{Rad}_{\mathsf{d},\mathsf{m}_{\zeta}})
for
\nu
-a.e.
\zeta\in Z
.
∎

No statement analogous to Theorem
6.5
holds for the Sobolev-to-Lipschitz property.
This is discussed in some detail
[
18
, Ex. 4.7]
for the simplest possible direct-integral form, namely the direct sum of two forms each defined on a quasi-connected component of the resulting direct-sum space.
In
[
18
, Ex. 4.7]
however, the measures
\mathsf{m}_{\zeta}
,
\zeta\in\left\{\pm 1\right\}
are absolutely continuous w.r.t.
\mathsf{m}
, and one might expect the situation to improve in the case when
\mathsf{m}
is properly ‘disintegrated’ into
\mathsf{m}
-negligible fibers (as opposed to: split into
\mathsf{m}
-non-negligible components).
The next example shows that this is not the case, and again the Sobolev-to-Lipschitz property is not preserved by direct integration.

Example 6.6

(cf., e.g.,
[
15
, Ex. 2.32]
)
.

Let
I\eqdef[0,1]
, and
X\eqdef I^{\scriptscriptstyle{\times 2}}
with standard topology,
2
-dimensional Euclidean distance
\mathsf{d}_{\scriptscriptstyle{\times 2}}
, Borel
\sigma
-algebra, and endowed with the
2
-dimensional Lebesgue measure
\mathsf{m}\eqdef{\mathrm{Leb}}^{2}
.
Consider the form

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}\eqdef

\displaystyle\left\{f\in L^{2}(X):f({\,\cdot\,},x_{2})\in W^{1,2}(I)\quad{\textrm{\,for ${{\mathrm{Leb}}^{1}}$-a.e.\,}}x_{2}\in I\right\}

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}(f)\eqdef

\displaystyle\int_{I}\left\lvert\partial_{1}f(x_{1},x_{2})\right\rvert^{2}{\mathrm{dLeb}}^{2}(x_{1},x_{2})\,\,\mathrm{.}

The form
(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
is the direct integral over
(Z,\nu)\eqdef(I,{\mathrm{Leb}}^{1})
of the forms

\mathcal{E}_{\zeta}(f)\eqdef\int_{I}\left\lvert\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}f({\,\cdot\,},\zeta)\right\rvert^{2}{\mathrm{dLeb}}^{1}\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,\qquad f\in\mathcal{F}_{\zeta}\eqdef W^{1,2}(I)\,\,\mathrm{,}\;\,

with
\mathsf{m}_{\zeta}\eqdef{\mathrm{Leb}}^{1}\otimes\delta_{\zeta}
.
In particular,
\mathcal{F}=L^{2}(Z;W^{1,2}(I))
.

Now, since
\car\in\mathcal{F}
and
\car_{\zeta}\in W^{1,2}(I)\eqqcolon\mathcal{F}_{\zeta}
for every
\zeta\in Z
, by (
6.4
) we have that
\mathbb{L}^{\mathsf{m},\tau}_{\mathrm{loc},b}=\mathbb{L}^{\mathsf{m},\tau}_{b}
, and analogously for
\mathsf{m}_{\zeta}
in place of
\mathsf{m}
for every
\zeta\in Z
.
Thus, it suffices to show that there exists
f\in\mathbb{L}^{\mathsf{m},\tau}_{b}
not admitting any
\mathsf{d}_{{\scriptscriptstyle{\times 2}}}
-Lipschitz
\mathsf{m}
-representative.
In fact, it is readily seen that
\zeta\mapsto\big({\mathcal{E}_{\zeta},\mathcal{F}_{\zeta}}\big)
is the ergodic decomposition of
(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})
in the sense of
[
15
]
, hence

\mathbb{L}^{\mathsf{m},\tau}_{b}=\left\{f\in\mathcal{C}_{b}(X):f_{\zeta}\in\mathbb{L}^{\mathsf{m}_{\zeta},\tau}_{b}{\textrm{\,for ${\nu}$-a.e.\,}}\zeta\in Z\right\}\,\,\mathrm{.}

In particular, every function of the form
f=\car\otimes g
with
g\in\mathcal{C}_{b}(I)
satisfies
f\in\mathbb{L}^{\mathsf{m},\tau}_{b}
and
\mathcal{E}(f)=0
.
Taking
g\notin\mathrm{Lip}^{1}_{b}(I,\mathsf{d})
shows that
f
is in general not
\mathsf{d}_{{\scriptscriptstyle{\times 2}}}
-Lipschitz.

A positive result in the spirit of Theorem
6.5
holds only for
(\mathsf{cSL}_{\tau,\mathsf{m},\mathsf{d}})
under further assumptions, granting a consistent choice of representatives.

Theorem 6.7
.

Let
(\mathbb{X},\mathcal{E})
be satisfying Assumption
6.3
, and
\mathsf{d}\colon X^{\scriptscriptstyle{\times 2}}\to[0,\infty]
be an extended pseudo-distance.
Further assume that
(\mathbb{X},\mathcal{E}_{\zeta})
satisfies
(\mathsf{cSL}_{\tau,\mathsf{m}_{\zeta},\mathsf{d}})
and
\mathrm{supp}[\mathsf{m}_{\zeta}]=X
for every
\zeta\in B
for some
B\in\mathcal{Z}
with
\nu B>0
.
Then,
(\mathbb{X},\mathcal{E})
satisfies
(\mathsf{cSL}_{\tau,\mathsf{m},\mathsf{d}})
.

Proof.

Fix
f\in\mathbb{L}^{\mathsf{m},\tau}_{b}
.
Then
f_{\zeta}\in\mathbb{L}^{\mathsf{m}_{\zeta},\tau}_{b}
for
\nu
-a.e.
\zeta\in Z
by (
6.4
).
In particular, there exists
\zeta\in B
with
f_{\zeta}\in\mathbb{L}^{\mathsf{m}_{\zeta},\tau}_{b}
.
For such fixed
\zeta
, by
(\mathsf{cSL}_{\tau,\mathsf{m}_{\zeta},\mathsf{d}})
, there exists a
\tau
-continuous
\mathsf{m}_{\zeta}
-representative of
f_{\zeta}
, additionally an element of
\mathrm{Lip}^{1}(\mathsf{d},\tau)
.
Since
\mathsf{m}_{\zeta}
has full
\tau
-support, such representative is unique, again denoted by
f_{\zeta}
, and we conclude that
f_{\zeta}\equiv f
everywhere on
X
.
Thus,
f\in\mathrm{Lip}^{1}(\mathsf{d},\tau)
and the proof is concluded.
∎

With a proof similar to the one of Theorem
6.7
, one can show the following.

Proposition 6.8
.

Let
(\mathbb{X},\mathcal{E})
be satisfying Assumption
6.3
, and
\mathsf{d}\colon X^{\scriptscriptstyle{\times 2}}\to[0,\infty]
be an extended pseudo-distance.
Further assume that there exists
\mathsf{d}\text{-}\mathrm{supp}[\mathsf{m}]=X
, and that
(\mathbb{X},\mathcal{E}_{\zeta})
satisfies
({\mathsf{d}}\textrm{-}\mathsf{cSL}_{\mathsf{m}_{\zeta},\mathsf{d}})
and there exists
\mathsf{d}\text{-}\mathrm{supp}[\mathsf{m}_{\zeta}]=X
for every
\zeta\in B
for some
B\in\mathcal{Z}
with
\nu B>0
.
Then,
(\mathbb{X},\mathcal{E})
satisfies
({\mathsf{d}}\textrm{-}\mathsf{cSL}_{\mathsf{m},\mathsf{d}})
.

Remark 6.9
.

The assumption in Theorem
6.7
that
\mathsf{m}_{\zeta}
have full
\tau
-support for all
\zeta
in a set of positive
\nu
-measure may at first sight seem to contrast our standing assumption that
\left(\mathsf{m}_{\zeta}\right)_{\zeta\in Z}
be separated.
This is however not the case, since
\mathsf{m}_{\zeta}
may be in general concentrated on sets much smaller than its support.
For instance, let
Y
be a locally compact Polish space, and denote by
\Upsilon
the configuration space over
Y
, i.e. the space of all locally finite point measures on
Y
, endowed with the vague topology and the corresponding Borel
\sigma
-algebra.
Further let
\sigma
be a Radon measure on
Y
and, for
s\in{\mathbb{R}}_{+}
, let
\pi_{s\sigma}
be the Poisson measure on
\Upsilon
with intensity measure
s\sigma
.
Finally, let
\lambda
be a diffuse Borel probability measure on
{\mathbb{R}}_{+}
.
The mixed Poisson measure on
\Upsilon
with Lévy measure
\lambda
and intensity measure
\sigma
is the probability measure
\mu_{\lambda,\sigma}\eqdef\int_{{\mathbb{R}}_{+}}\pi_{s\sigma}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\lambda(s)
.
Then,
\left(\pi_{s\sigma}\right)_{s\in{\mathbb{R}}_{+}}
is a
separated
disintegration of
\mu_{\lambda,\sigma}
over
({\mathbb{R}}_{+},\lambda)
, yet
\mu_{\lambda,\sigma}
and each
\pi_{s\sigma}
has
full
topological support on
\Upsilon
; for details on all these constructions see e.g.
[
17
]
.
We refer the reader to
[
15
, Ex. 3.13]
for further details and for the construction of a relevant direct integral of Dirichlet forms in this case.
